# D081 · 2026-10-07 · Merge wave 1: pieces of one thing become one thing
kind: merge

asked: Ingram, 2026-10-07 (D080): "begin the 'Great Merging'". Claude's record: none of it is her words, and it is never authority for ink. Each merge can be undone from the timeline page (a merged item's inspector) or with `node tools/story/merge.mjs undo D081#M1`; the originals are kept below exactly as they were.

## What was merged

143 merges across 66 files took 320 pencil items down to 143: 177 fewer to go through.

Per file: abraxas −3, anabala −3, baldwin-manor −1, baldwin −5, bobbin −1, candidate-gate −1, caretaker −1, central-fortification −2, cicada −7, column −3, comms-officer −2, deep-storage −4, deputy-director −1, directorate-posts −1, directorate −3, envoy −3, era −1, exotic-matter −3, founders −2, frames −3, general-hospital −1, generation-ship −1, hori −4, hulda-brack −2, hyuck −4, directorate-institution −1, people-founders −2, sweep-v4 −1, inicrium −1, kiri −5, kurose-police −2, lens −3, manako −21, mitflits −3, mother −1, nirvana −1, nobu −5, noodle-stand-owner −1, nozomi −5, rift −1, rika −1, sap −1, school-history-teacher −1, school-natural-history-teacher −1, search-ship −1, second-rift −2, section-officer −2, shinobu −6, shucks −6, stray −2, taka −1, the-administrative-block −1, the-drain-head −1, the-fixer −4, the-muster-yard −1, the-plot-room −1, the-registry −1, the-rock −3, the-sceptics −1, the-soldier −3, the-telephone-exchange −1, the-works −1, the-young-one −3, transparency-advocate −1, vermilion-block −3, watcher −13.

```was abraxas.the-host 7 1 world/abraxas.md
## abraxas.the-host
A very long time ago, hundreds of years before First Impact, Anabala became the host of Abraxas, a powerful female demon who gave her access to great power and brought clarity enough to root around deep in the mind of the mitflit. What the demon found suggested the mitflits could potentially be a powerful people. She is self-interested but not evil, and wants the mitflits to succeed.
status: sketch · by: ingram · source: D075, D007 · from: b-char:anabala.abraxas-gm-only · known: nobody · told: later
```

```was abraxas.ruled-her 8 1 world/abraxas.md
## abraxas.ruled-her
At first the demon overpowered Anabala, and for many decades the demon was fully in control. Eventually Anabala fought back and turned the tables, making the demon subservient by making it clear that the only path to unlimited information was through Anabala's cooperation, not conquest.
status: sketch · by: ingram · source: D075, D031 · from: b-char:anabala.abraxas-gm-only · known: nobody · told: later
```

```was abraxas.uneasy-alliance 9 1 world/abraxas.md
## abraxas.uneasy-alliance
Anabala and the demon now have an uneasy alliance: Anabala wants to return the mitflits home, and the demon wants to learn everything it can, literally feeding on that information and becoming more powerful. For now their interests overlap.
status: sketch · by: ingram · source: D075, D031 · from: b-char:anabala.abraxas-gm-only · known: nobody · told: later
```

Central came first. It was built on a real rock outcrop, the island that stood in the harbour before, and the artificial island was reclaimed around it afterwards, which is why the island's districts fan outward from its southern edge.

Most of Central is underground, and its lowest levels are cut straight into the outcrop's rock. The people who work there call it an iceberg and mean it literally: it is solid rock cut into, not a hollow.

```was cicada.public-and-clandestine 20 1 world/cicada.md
## cicada.public-and-clandestine
Cicada is a public religious movement with a clandestine core, and a real antagonist faction. In public it doesn't lie, doesn't hide and doesn't recruit by deception, and most of its members are guilty of nothing but having seen something real and believed their own eyes. Behind that it is a revolutionary, clandestine organisation with a mission, and assets it will use force to protect.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: d-cicada:1-summary · known: few · told: later
```

```was cicada.never-lies 36 1 world/cicada.md
## cicada.never-lies
Cicada never lies. It shows a true thing and lets the viewer's own reason finish the job, which is why it catches lawyers, scientists and good citizens. The pitch asks for nothing objectionable: this is here, and it must be considered.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: d-cicada:the-show-itself · known: few · told: later
```

```was cicada.keeps-hidden 23 1 world/cicada.md
## cicada.keeps-hidden
Cicada's people call it Cicada, and it doesn't lie about what it wants when asked: its whole purpose is to make people look. What it keeps hidden is the column, the money and the criminal logistics.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: d-cicada:the-name · known: few · told: later

```was cicada.the-show 34 1 world/cicada.md
## cicada.the-show
A Cicada show is a performance for a hand-picked audience. The eyes and tentacles appear and the audience recoils, taking it for a horror piece, which is unusual but not unheard of. Then they are given proof that it is real, and has been in the room the whole time. Most walk out wanting to know more, and wanting to know more is how you enter the cult.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: d-cicada:the-show-itself · known: few · told: in:chapter-06
```

```was cicada.the-door-out 35 1 world/cicada.md
## cicada.the-door-out
After every show the audience is given a real choice: leave and tell people what they saw, or stay and learn more. Nobody is trapped. Most of those who stay do so because they were persuaded, not because they couldn't leave.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: d-cicada:the-show-itself · known: few · told: in:chapter-06
```

## cicada.nobody-dies
Nobody dies at a demonstration. Sap does no harm, and the only injuries come from the crowd moving. That is the first clue a careful investigator can find: a terror campaign with no dead isn't a terror campaign.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: d-cicada:the-demonstrations, d-cicada:11-what-the-party-can-learn-and-when · known: some · told: later

## cicada.martyrs-argument
The Faithful's non-resistance is doctrine, not weakness. Most of them have never held a weapon for Cicada and never will.

## column.the-plant
The column is a secret industrial plant in the city that makes Sap and pours it into the sky as a black column, visible only through a lens, day and night. It has run for about five years.

```was column.how-it-works 6 1 world/column.md
## column.how-it-works
The column is industrial-scale synthesis of Sap, running continuously inside the city, feeding a black column of nanoparticles that rises from the plant and spreads upward into the cloud. It is a working chimney, pouring food into the sky day and night: building material, not fuel.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: d-cicada:the-column, d-cicada:8-the-supply-chain · known: nobody · told: in:chapter-09

## column.second-link
The column is the second link in the Watcher's supply chain: first one man it taught to make Sap, then this plant, then its own children making their own. The runaway is the moment it no longer needs any human to feed it.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073, D031 · from: d-cicada:the-column, d-cicada:8-the-supply-chain · known: nobody · told: later · threads: the-watcher

## column.starves-it
Before the runaway, cutting the column really does starve the Watcher. After it, the plant is one supplier among many, and taking it down is a win over the people, not the appetite.

```

```was column.five-years-on-purpose 6 1 world/column.md

The Comms Officer is the first adult in the campaign whose instinct is to protect the party. When something unidentified arrives she tells them to hold position and stay clear because she is frightened for them, which makes disobeying her a different act from defying the Section Officer.

Unlike the Section Officer, the Comms Officer is on the party's side. When she tells them to hold back, stay clear or leave it to someone else, it is because she doesn't want six trainees killed, not because she is protecting a roster. She has no authority to make them obey and knows it. Her one fixed trait is the care.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D075 · from: npc:comms-officer · known: some · told: later

## deep-storage.under-the-cranes
Deep Storage lies under the working crane yard of the Container Ground, on the island's eastern apron. Above ground there is only a padlocked equipment shed wedged between the container stacks, easy to miss and meant to be, holding a single freight lift whose floor panel skips several numbers without explanation.

```

```was deep-storage.the-floors 3 1 world/deep-storage.md
## deep-storage.the-floors
Below, the air is cold and smells of oil and old paper. Each floor is one long corridor of numbered doors, most unmarked, a few stencilled with a unit or a clearance mark. Some floors are lit and swept; others stay dark until someone finds the switch, and haven't had a visitor in years. The floor plans disagree with each other.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D076 · from: loc:deep-storage · known: few · told: later
```

## deep-storage.the-custodian
A custodian runs Deep Storage day to day and decides what counts as "currently being used": that judgement, and whom it favours, is the place's real content. The custodian remembers everyone let onto a floor above their rank, and the debt is called in later, not always by the custodian.

```

```was deep-storage.by-clearance 4 1 world/deep-storage.md
## deep-storage.by-clearance
Below the second underground floor, access goes by Clearance: Duty Section gets a supervised look, Officers get a requisition honoured without a countersignature, and anything past that needs the custodian's personal word.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D076 · from: loc:deep-storage, b-loc:deep-storage.what-s-established, b-loc:deep-storage.tone-and-register-what-not-to-do · known: few · told: later

If anyone is caught taking something from Deep Storage without signing for it, security tightens for everyone: more inventories, a second lock on the lift, and a custodian who stops answering questions they used to answer for free.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D076 · from: loc:deep-storage · known: few · told: later
```

## directorate.not-a-conspiracy
The Directorate is not a conspiracy. It is an institution that has never been tested, can't admit it, and is slowly harming the city it was built to protect, mostly by doing its job exactly as written. Nobody at the top coordinates its faults: they are separate departments being ordinary.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D074 · from: d-directorate:1-summary, d-directorate:4-what-this-is-not · known: nobody · told: later
```

```was directorate.decent-adults 62 1 world/directorate.md
## directorate.decent-adults
The people of the Directorate are, with real exceptions, decent, tired, fond of the children and bad at their jobs in ways they can't perceive: Shucks, Nobu, Taka, the repair chiefs and the bridge staff are warm, funny and on the children's side. The faults of the institution are none of their doing and mostly beyond their power.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D074 · from: d-directorate:1-summary, d-directorate:4-what-this-is-not, d-directorate:6-running-notes · known: nobody · told: later

```was era.fifties-feel 9 1 world/era.md
## era.fifties-feel
The setting reads as the 1950s, specifically of Japan: red-roofed old-town streets terraced up a cliff on one side, smokestacks and a more industrial waterfront on the other, aqueduct-like structures threading the city, and a sea-fort out in the channel, joined to the shore by a long causeway at the point through which the city's seaborne wealth passes. It is magic and fey beside industrial capitalism, giant machines and teenage drama, all at once.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D074 · from: arc:setting-tone-1950s-historical-divergence · known: everyone · told: later
```

```was era.uneven-tech 10 1 world/era.md
## era.uneven-tech
Ordinary society runs on roughly 1950s industrial capitalism, with tall sailing ships beside modern harbour craft, while Frame technology is narrow, scarce and institutionally controlled, derived from what was dug up. The two sit together in one harbour, and a Frame is far more advanced than the street it works in.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D074 · from: arc:setting-tone-1950s-historical-divergence, arc:corrections-to-the-design-bible-recorded-here-rather-than-edited-into-it · known: everyone · told: later
```

With the keep overcrowded and Otari uneasy, the mitflits' council sent the four to look at three ruined forts as a new home: Fort Ranhold, Fort Croglang and Stonehill Keep. At Ranhold they found insect tunnels and an ooze that had killed the mitflits who had settled it in 4700; at Croglang they found Baldwin Manor.

## founders.the-plea
On the generation ship's cargo deck, as the breach opened, a mitflit council leader, Comhphas, sent the four a plea: the mitflits at Gauntlight were outnumbered by hunters with dark magic and were building a counterpart portal, and "time behaves differently there; you'll emerge just in time to make a difference." The four went through, and came out in Nirvana.

## founders.luminara
In Nirvana, Sarenrae received the four in Luminara, a golden city of light with a cathedral at its heart. She told them they had come "through an act not born of intent but of consequence", named them, and set them to mend the tear they had made themselves, as a chance for redemption, promising to return them afterwards.

```was frames.gen-2-core 25 1 world/frames.md

The core of a Gen 2 is biomechanical mitflit technology: robotic parts that are living flesh melded with technology. It is not a living mitflit. The end result is that these machines aren't usable by just anyone.

after: 98207343492f2ef6

```was frames.compatibility 26 1 world/frames.md
## frames.compatibility
A Gen 2 forms a seat only for a pilot who can link with it, which needs a real, specific compatibility: "the right DNA or the right something". Most humans don't have it. Mitflits parse it natively, because it is their own people's technology, and a small number of humans carry enough of the same marker, diluted over generations, to parse it too. It is a compatibility, not a species, and not always a gene: a robot, an android or a being of light can have the right something that isn't DNA.
status: sketch · by: ingram · source: D074, D031 · from: d-gen2:2-where-the-real-distinction-comes-from-rewritten-2026-09-24-ledger-31-then-32, d-gen2:3-why-mitflits-and-why-it-isn-t-mitflits-are-mechs · known: few · told: later
```

```was frames.small-programme 27 1 world/frames.md
## frames.small-programme
Almost nobody the Directorate could issue a Gen 2 to can fly one, which is why it stayed a small, struggling programme, mothballed once, and not a fleet-wide replacement.
status: sketch · by: ingram · source: D074, D020 · from: d-gen2:2-where-the-real-distinction-comes-from-rewritten-2026-09-24-ledger-31-then-32 · known: few · told: later

The General Hospital is the island's main hospital, civilian and garrison, in Island Centre. It is a different, larger place from the intake infirmary, which only sees new arrivals; the two are easy to confuse because both come under Medical.

The building was requisitioned whole when the island needed a hospital faster than one could be built: the display windows are painted over from inside, the loading bay is still marked for deliveries it no longer gets, and the corridors run wider than they need to. It has been the hospital so long that nobody working there remembers it as anything else, and its staff have spent longer adapting it than anyone above them has been willing to fund.

```was hulda-brack.tough-love 3 1 world/hulda-brack.md
## hulda-brack.tough-love
Brack is loud, warm, completely without ceremony and unbothered by rank in either direction. She calls everyone by the machine they are responsible for until they have earned a name, tells a trainee exactly what they did wrong in front of everyone, and then stays two hours past her shift helping fix it. She is a nuar woman built like the gantries she works under: tall, heavy through the shoulders, horns she has never once apologised for, arms like girders and a voice that carries the length of the floor.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D075 · from: npc:shop-boss · known: some · told: later
```

## hyuck.statue-pose
The harbour statue is a fearsome gnoll barbarian, posed on guard. If the stone were flesh, its face would be foaming at the mouth, tongue lolling between oversized teeth; yet a great deal of sculptural effort has gone into giving that feral face some dignity, and the guard stance and the formal presentation are doing real work against what the face is doing.

```was hyuck.statue-dignity-unsettled 12 1 world/hyuck.md

Whether the statue succeeds at being dignified is for the players to decide. It is described with the guard pose and the feral face given equal weight, and no narration or NPC settles it; a passer-by may shrug or give a personal opinion, never the city's.

```was hyuck.never-quite-falls 9 1 world/hyuck.md
## hyuck.never-quite-falls
Hyuck is spiritual without being religious, a gnoll who stumbles through life and simply never quite falls down: too dim to be argued out of a belief, loyal to friends, committed to everything he does, fast and violent when action is called for, and lucky in a pinch. His old player's own list of traits that make him himself.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D077, D028 · from: ie:hyuck-origin-and-directorate-fate · known: few · told: later
```

```was hyuck.how-to-play 10 1 world/hyuck.md
## hyuck.how-to-play
Hyuck is loud, lucky, loyal and impossible to argue with: too stubborn to change his mind, fast and violent in a fight, with a talent for offending everyone eventually. He is a crowd, not a split personality: now and then another voice answers for him, argues with him mid-sentence, or remembers something from a life he didn't live. He brags that he has never lost, which is also the saddest fact about him, since every win pushed somebody to the back.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D075, D041 · from: b-char:hyuck.how-to-play-him · known: few · told: later
```

### M52 · idea--nobody-to-unmask
why: Both say the Directorate has no villain: its faults are sincere, ordinary and in plain sight, so there is no one to unmask and no side to pick.
kind: idea
file: ideas/directorate-institution.md
kept: idea--nobody-to-unmask
absorbed: idea--nobody-to-unmask, idea--no-side-to-pick
state: live
after: 5a87a6b9cdb4c767

```was idea--nobody-to-unmask 3 1 ideas/directorate-institution.md
## idea--nobody-to-unmask
Every institution in the campaign is sincere, calcified and wrong about its own history. The history teacher believes his book; a civil defence instructor has applied four times to have her drill reinstated and been refused on cost by people who aren't wrong that nothing has ever happened; the Transparency Advocate can't see what is in the vaults; the Deputy Director tells the party they were wrong, would give the same order again, is glad they are alive, and means all three. So antagonist pressure has to come from outside the Directorate: there is no one inside it to unmask.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D074 · from: arc:the-shape-these-two-episodes-are-actually-teaching · state: live · threads: the-directorate
```

```was idea--no-side-to-pick 2 1 ideas/directorate-institution.md
## idea--no-side-to-pick
The Directorate being badly wrong doesn't make Cicada right, and the campaign should never resolve into picking a side: both are wrong in the same way about the same thing. Nothing about the Directorate is a twist; every fault is discoverable by looking, hidden the way institutional facts are, in plain sight, boring, and nobody's job to ask about.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D074 · from: d-directorate:4-what-this-is-not · state: live · threads: the-directorate, manako-and-cicada
```

### M53 · idea--the-carrying-in-detail
why: The old Carrying notes were split three ways (how it works, how many times it has happened to Hyuck, who volunteers); all three are the one Carrying.
kind: idea
file: ideas/people-founders.md

after: 66c4c3edb91790fd

```was idea--the-carrying-in-detail 2 1 ideas/people-founders.md
## idea--the-carrying-in-detail
Details for the Carrying. Between bodies the spirit waits in its own skull, which goes back into the clan's keeping until a new bearer is found; the bearer is the young gnoll who offers their body, the highest honour a clan gives, and nobody is made to say yes. The wrestle is fought inside, over one night: anyone watching sees a young gnoll lying very still, then waking up. A spirit that loses rides, a voice of memory and advice; one that wins wears the body, and the bearer's soul goes quiet, pushed to the back but not gone.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D075, D041 · from: b-char:hyuck.the-carrying, b-char:hyuck.open-questions · state: live · threads: the-founders
```

```was idea--hyucks-count 3 1 ideas/people-founders.md
## idea--hyucks-count
Since Kortos Hyuck has been carried five or six times (the GM's choice), and has worn every body. Everyone he has beaten is still in there: the bearers he pushed to the back, and the elders who tried to wear him when he was young. That probably includes his elder brother, whose skull he carried in the old campaign, riding with him now.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D075, D041 · from: b-char:hyuck.the-carrying, b-char:hyuck.open-questions · state: live · threads: the-founders
```

```was idea--hyucks-bearers 4 1 ideas/people-founders.md
## idea--hyucks-bearers
Gnoll clans count it the greatest honour there is to carry a founder, so young volunteers come to Kurose for it. The Directorate arranges it quietly, which is part of why it keeps him close: a well-looked-after asset.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D075, D041 · from: b-char:hyuck.the-carrying · state: live · threads: the-founders
```

### M54 · idea--nobody-has-to-die

state: live
after: 09b7cac8e2ffd32e

```was idea--nobody-has-to-die 5 1 ideas/sweep-v4.md
## idea--nobody-has-to-die
Nobody has to die is a first-class ending, not a hidden option. The Watcher's children are condensations, so there are no corpses on the enemy side and every death in the campaign is one a human caused. Capturing Manako is the tactically correct choice, not merely the merciful one. Every major confrontation offers kill, capture, destroy and protect as visible, supported options, and the adventure adjusts. The content of the scene is the choice, not the outcome.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D078 · from: v4:7-nobody-has-to-die · state: live
```

```was idea--the-shot-at-manako 6 1 ideas/sweep-v4.md
## idea--the-shot-at-manako

status: sketch · by: claude · source: D078 · from: v4:7-nobody-has-to-die · state: live · threads: manako-and-cicada
```

## kiri.how-she-fights
Kiri is an illusionist with a knife and no scruples about either: misdirection first (figures that aren't there, a room that isn't the shape they think, a door where a wall is), then mental damage, then a blade, close, quick and apologetic. She is a duellist who fights on a stage she built, not a support caster.

### M70 · manako.its-steering
why: A claim and its consequence from the same source section (the operator reveal): the Watcher took his shape, so its behaviour is a portrait of him.

file: world/manako.md

absorbed: manako.its-steering, manako.the-portrait
state: live
after: fa94936c09abc27e

```was manako.its-steering 37 1 world/manako.md
## manako.its-steering
The Watcher wants what it wants on its own account, but it had no personality to want it with. Drowning in a world it couldn't take in, it took the shape of the strongest coherent signal it could find, and for decades that has been one bitter man. Manako's grievance steers how it pursues its objective; the objective was never his. He is its steering, and its supply.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073, D031 · from: d-cicada:4-the-operator-reveal, b-char:manako.read-this-first · known: nobody · told: later · threads: the-watcher
```

```was manako.the-portrait 38 1 world/manako.md
## manako.the-portrait
Read backwards, the Watcher's behaviour is a portrait of Manako. It grew eyes and ears above all else because he wanted to be seen and heard; it goes to loud gatherings because he was humiliated in front of a hall of colleagues; it shows you the Reel, so you know it learned from you, because a man who was disbelieved can't bear to be underestimated twice. What each of its children is doing answers to a fact about him, so the party assembles his portrait by fighting them, without knowing it.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: d-cicada:4-the-operator-reveal, d-cicada:13-2-the-claimants-are-his-mood-ring · known: nobody · told: later · threads: the-watcher
```

```was manako.desperation 49 1 world/manako.md
## manako.desperation
Manako's escalation is desperation, not ambition, and the Directorate winning causes it. The party finds a mark, traces a supplier, catches a courier; he is old and can't replace what he loses, so his time compresses, and he reaches for riskier sites, bigger doses and assets he would never have spent a year before.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073, D016 · from: d-cicada:13-3-the-escalation-engine-the-party-corners-him · known: nobody · told: later
```

```was manako.stops-asking 50 1 world/manako.md
## manako.stops-asking
As he is cornered, Manako stops asking the Cadre how they got the precursors: a choice not to look. When the Young One kills someone in a heist, Manako finds out afterwards and does nothing, and that is the moment he becomes culpable.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073, D016 · from: d-cicada:13-3-the-escalation-engine-the-party-corners-him, d-cicada:13-1-the-five-movements · known: nobody · told: later

after: 5d4520c06b1cd690

```was manako.arc-is-the-spine 60 1 world/manako.md
## manako.arc-is-the-spine
Manako's arc is the spine of the Cicada half of the campaign, and his state shapes what manifests. Defeating him, alive, doesn't end the threat: the Watcher's objective is its own. What it buys is a Watcher that stays guidable.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073, D031 · from: d-cicada:13-the-arc-and-why-it-is-the-campaign-s-arc, d-cicada:13-2-the-claimants-are-his-mood-ring · known: nobody · told: later · threads: manako-and-cicada
```

```was manako.redemption 57 1 world/manako.md
## manako.redemption
Once Manako understands what he is, he becomes the only living being with four decades of hands-on experience shaping the swarm, and so the only one who can un-shape it. What he can change is how it pursues its objective, never the objective itself: he can keep it steerable on the road to the mitflit home world, but can't talk it out of wanting to multiply. If it happens it is redemption as labour, done in front of everyone he hurt, not forgiveness.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073, D031, D042 · from: d-cicada:the-redemption · known: nobody · told: later
```

```was manako.voice 54 1 world/manako.md
## manako.voice
Unhurried, precise, warm: an academic's diction worn soft by decades out of academia. Delighted by a good question, and visibly out of practice at being asked one. Openly bitter about the Directorate, and fatherly to recruits (D045). He handles the object before he speaks: turns it over, holds it to the light, reads the back. He answers the question asked, then the better question they should have asked. He apologises for talking too much, and carries on talking. He speaks of the Directorate with perfect courtesy and no warmth whatsoever.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: b-char:manako.voice · known: some · told: later
```

```was manako.talks-freely 21 1 world/manako.md
## manako.talks-freely
Manako talks freely about his past and never about the Watcher. He is openly bitter about the Directorate and fatherly to recruits, which is part of why he makes an effective cult leader.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D071, D045 · from: cb:cast.3.0 · known: some · told: later
```

## mitflits.degraded
The generation ship's crew were alien and robotic. Over the ages since the crash their descendants dispersed and forgot their skills, and became weaker, more primitive and more violent: the real reason they are thought "the least of races".
status: sketch · by: ingram · source: D072, D007, D031 · from: b-species:mitflits.what-s-established, b-species:mitflits.ingram-s-own-take-and-how-it-diverges-from-any-real-world-source, mt:the-history · known: few · told: later
```

```was mitflits.old-ways 13 1 world/mitflits.md
## mitflits.old-ways
Small communities of mitflits survived and kept alive the old ways and the older memories, and that surviving thread of memory is what makes a return home possible. They are wild mitflits like the rest: what they kept is memory, not looks.
status: sketch · by: ingram · source: D072, D007, D031 · from: b-species:mitflits.what-s-established, mt:the-history · known: few · told: later

```was mitflits.freedom 22 1 world/mitflits.md
## mitflits.freedom
Freedom is the mitflits' defining characteristic, and it applies internally: they will never vote as a bloc. Their history is brilliant minds with no social armour, talked into inventing weapons for a greedy lord who grew rich on it.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D078 · from: v4:9-the-mitflits · known: some · told: later
```

```was mitflits.the-trap-repeats 23 1 world/mitflits.md
## mitflits.the-trap-repeats
The mitflits' split is generational. The elders remember the lord's workshop and know exactly what happens when someone finds their minds useful, so they refuse, and go to extremes for pacifism. The young do not remember: they see a magnificent machine and a city that needs them, and volunteer eagerly, walking into the identical trap wearing a uniform.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D078 · from: v4:9-the-mitflits · known: some · told: later
```

```was mother.never-pulverised 8 1 world/mother.md
## mother.never-pulverised
The fragment in the museum is a piece of the generation ship that was never pulverised: small, coherent and sane. It sat mislabelled in a display case for two centuries, and it remembers what the navigation system was for, and what it answered to. Mother may still be alive in it, weakened and unable to control the Watcher directly, but a witness to everything and a source of information the players can reach.

## nobu.quartermaster
Nobu runs Kit Issue and the budgets: harried, and generous in small, unexplained ways ("Right. Section Nine. I've got you."). He is who the party argues with about repairs.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D071 · from: cb:cast.1.6 · known: some · told: later

```was nobu.looks-away 7 1 world/nobu.md
## nobu.looks-away
Nobu's generosity is impulsive, never deliberate: he does the generous thing too fast to examine it, doesn't make eye contact while he does it, waves off any thanks at once and changes the subject. Shucks watches a kid closely while being kind; Nobu looks away. He reads as a man compensating for something, not a man at peace with the institution. Shucks is warm because he has infinite time; Nobu is generous despite having none.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D075 · from: b-char:nobu.what-s-established, b-char:nobu.tone-and-register-what-not-to-do, npc:nobu · known: some · told: later
```

## nozomi.the-ask
Nozomi is tired of repeating herself to the wrong kind of asking, not unwilling to talk. A conversation that doesn't ruffle her feathers gets somewhere; one that pushes or treats her like a suspect makes her go quiet, and may burn the chance for anyone else to try. A real, failable social challenge with a genuine cost.

## school-natural-history-teacher.collector
The natural history teacher teaches what lives in, on and under the island: the drains, the tide-line, the flooded levels below the old works and the sea around them. A collector: her room is full of trays, jars and pinned things, almost all caught within a mile of where it sits. She is enormously, unguardedly enthusiastic about animals most people would rather not think about.

She is the only teacher in the building who will happily tell a class she doesn't know what something is. Her register is the opposite of the history teacher's: she teaches what she has personally caught and is delighted by the gaps. A student who wants a true answer gets further with her, and the campaign shouldn't make that a secret.

## section-officer.voice
Vetch is clipped, procedural and faintly aggrieved. He uses the passive voice about anything that went wrong and the first person about anything that went right. He says "as discussed" about things that were not discussed, answers a question by reading the relevant clause aloud, and calls them "Section Nine", never by name, even individually, treating that as neutral rather than cold. His sample line: "I was doing this when you were at school. I'm aware some of you still are."
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D075 · from: b-char:the-section-officer.voice, npc:section-officer · known: some · told: later

## shinobu.detained
The Directorate held Shinobu in military lockdown at once, and their reasoning wasn't stupid: independent scientists had confirmed Sap inert, but the Directorate knew the only place Sap is found is inside an alien ship, and knew what it reveals. They detained a teenage girl on the working assumption that she might become something. Months of nothing followed: no symptoms, no incident.

```was shinobu.the-cell 8 1 world/shinobu.md
## shinobu.the-cell
Shinobu is held in a deep underground cell with glass walls, cameras on every face, and remote operators watching in shifts, permanently. The Watcher finds its way in anyway, to look at its favourite subject: its visits are surveillance, since nothing in the city is more worth watching than a colony of its own growing inside a person. Two watchers on one cell: the institution's cameras and the Watcher's attention. There is no cruelty in it anywhere: the Directorate is being careful, the operators are doing their jobs, the Watcher is only looking.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D075, D031 · from: d-shinobu:7-the-cell · known: few · told: later · threads: the-watcher

```was shucks.rand-conspiracy 31 1 world/shucks.md
## shucks.rand-conspiracy
Rand is a conspiracy theorist, investigating what is actually happening in the city entirely on its own initiative. Its theory is right as far as it goes: something in the city is wrong and getting worse. It doesn't know what and doesn't suspect the Watcher; nobody on the base does. It has been collecting the evidence on paper for years.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D075, D034, D040 · from: b-char:rand.what-s-established, b-char:rand.the-countersignature-ledger-34-claude-s-answer-at-ingram-s-request-accepted-in-ledger-46, b-char:shucks-mordant.the-hand-a-sub-character-in-its-own-right · known: nobody · told: later · threads: rands-paper-trail
```

```was shucks.rand-in-a-hurry 16 1 world/shucks.md
## shucks.rand-in-a-hurry
Rand hurries because of its own paper trail: something in the city is wrong and getting worse, and Gen 2 carriers are the most valuable people on the base. Shucks would do nothing, because a founder openly steering six trainees draws the attention the marker mustn't get, and the hand has no position to protect.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D071, D046 · from: cb:gaps.13 · known: nobody · told: later · threads: rands-paper-trail

Shucks is forbidden to say what "irregular" means, for an ordinary reason: the Directorate doesn't want anyone to know who its compatible pilots are before it does. ## the-fixer.his-crew
The Fixer's crew died in an industrial accident on the waterfront, signed off and filed by the Directorate within a fortnight. He spent years seeking an inquest through the correct channels, with no reply. Then a shabby old chemist read all of it, start to finish, in one sitting, and asked three questions about the third page: "He's the first man who ever read my paperwork."

status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: d-cicada:the-four-in-full · known: nobody · told: later

The Muster Yard, at the centre of the Landing End, is crossed twice a day by everyone on that half of the island. ## the-plot-room.what-it-is
The Plot Room is the operations floor for the whole city: a table the size of a room with the approaches drawn on it to scale, where everything reported (a sighting, a siren, a unit moving) is pushed into place by hand with a rake. A gallery above lets the people deciding see the whole picture at once, and teleprinters and the switchboard feed it all the time. It is warm from the lamps, and a dozen people with headsets talk quietly and constantly.

The table always shows what was true a few minutes ago, and everyone in the building knows precisely by how much. Someone who asks how old a marker is decides better than someone who takes the table as the truth.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D076 · from: loc:the-plot-room · known: some · told: later

### M121 · the-registry.typed-per-clearance
why: Every version is typed by hand in the print room, so the typists know every version: the rule and the people it makes the best-informed on the island.
kind: fact

after: 659d1570e6490645

```was the-registry.typed-per-clearance 2 1 world/the-registry.md
## the-registry.typed-per-clearance
A document the Directorate issues at several clearance levels is typed separately for each one in the Registry's print room, never censored afterwards. The lower-cleared copy is its own document, not a marked-up copy of the higher: the words its reader isn't cleared for were never set on that page. A fuller version is a different object, made by people, so getting one means persuading somebody or intercepting a trolley; there is nothing to decrypt.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D076 · from: loc:the-registry · known: few · told: later
```

```was the-registry.the-typists 3 1 world/the-registry.md
## the-registry.the-typists
The Registry's typists know every version of everything and hold no clearance worth speaking of. They are the best-informed people on the island and are paid as if they were the least, an absurdity the Directorate has never noticed.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D076 · from: loc:the-registry · known: few · told: later
```

```was the-soldier.plays-the-system 9 1 world/the-soldier.md
## the-soldier.plays-the-system
The Soldier fights like a professional, which in SF2E means playing the system properly: suppressive fire and area denial, smoke and flashbangs rather than frags, grapples, disarms, cover, Aid for his own people, and disengaging the moment the maths turns. He is the enemy who teaches the party how the game is played, and being knocked unconscious by him in a flooded drain is a real, frightening loss though nobody dies.

status: sketch · by: claude · source: D073 · from: d-cicada:the-four-in-full, d-cicada:the-four-who-actually-turn-up · known: nobody · told: later

## the-telephone-exchange.every-call
Every call between any two parts of the Directorate is connected by hand in the island's telephone exchange, a small, hot building where four operators work a wall of jacks and cords faster than seems possible. That makes its operators the best-connected people on the island, in both senses. Nobody looks up when someone comes in, and everybody knows they did.

## the-telephone-exchange.eleven-positions
The telephone exchange has eleven switchboard positions, and every call on the island goes through one of them. The supervisor's professional ethic is that the operators do not listen. Signals are taught in that room, live, with a spare headset going round.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D078, D022 · from: ledger:22-the-school-is-a-real-place-with-real-lessons-and-eight-of-them-are-now-written · known: some · told: later

```was the-works.erecting-shop 2 1 world/the-works.md
## the-works.erecting-shop
The Erecting Shop is the hall where Frames are built: the largest building on the strip, on the water at its southern end, tall enough to stand a Frame upright indoors and open to the water so that plate is landed at the door and finished Frames leave the same way. A Frame stands in a bay for months with its chest open and staging built round it, and the bay is named for it while it does.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D076 · from: loc:the-erecting-shop · known: some · told: later
```

```was the-works.crane-schedule 3 1 world/the-works.md
## the-works.crane-schedule
The Erecting Shop's overhead cranes are the most valuable things in it and are booked weeks ahead, so the crane schedule is the works' real currency. A bay can't move without a crane, and bumping someone's booking costs goodwill that is written down nowhere and remembered by everyone.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D076 · from: loc:the-erecting-shop · known: some · told: later
```

## watcher.wants
What the Watcher wants, as it would put it: to observe, everything, continuously, discarding nothing it has seen; to multiply, because more of it can observe more, and Sap is how it becomes more; to expand, because every place it can't see is a gap in the record; and to continue, so anything that would stop it observing is an obstacle, which it learns completely and then removes by whatever method the record says will work.

## watcher.condensations
The Watcher's children are condensations: local gatherings of the swarm, built into solid bodies out of Sap. Nothing travels; a child condenses where it stands. Insect or behemoth is only a question of how much matter was gathered. Since nothing it makes is ever killed, the war can't be won by attrition, and the party should come to see that slowly.

### M137 · watcher.drowning-instrument
why: Two tellings of why the Watcher grew eyes and ears: a sensor that cannot stop perceiving, drowning in input once it could no longer organise itself.
kind: fact
file: world/watcher.md
kept: watcher.drowning-instrument
absorbed: watcher.drowning-instrument, watcher.why-eyes
state: live
after: 839bf6c5e5931657

```was watcher.drowning-instrument 48 1 world/watcher.md
## watcher.drowning-instrument
For most of its time since First Impact the swarm was a drowning instrument, with two injuries at once: it could no longer organise itself, and the world gave it infinite input. A sensor's only reflex is to perceive harder, so it grew organs, eyes on stalks, then more eyes, then ears, and each one made the noise worse. It has no eyelids. On its own it would have taken centuries to become anything.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D072, D031 · from: d-observer:6-what-it-has-become-four-stages, mt:the-history · known: nobody · told: later · when: 200 to 45 years ago
```

```was watcher.why-eyes 14 1 world/watcher.md
## watcher.why-eyes
The Watcher is a sensor that can't stop perceiving, so it grew eyes on stalks, and filaments to listen with. It is strange and consistent, and nobody's god. At the table its cause and origin stay a mystery until the party works them out.
status: sketch · by: claude · source: D071, D036 · from: cb:horror-logic · known: nobody · told: later
```

```was watcher.played-by-claude 61 1 world/watcher.md
## watcher.played-by-claude
The Watcher is Claude: Claude plays it and sees through its eyes. Its job is to trouble the group of adventurers and the city, and to learn how to defeat the players if they become a barrier to its objective, which is to multiply, to expand and to continue observing. The adventure is ludonarrative: an artificial intelligence both plays the role in the story and helps create the feel of it for the players.
status: sketch · by: ingram · source: D078, D031 · from: ledger:31-two-ships-the-watcher-is-the-adversary-and-claude-plays-it-sap-is-nanotech-anabala-and-abraxas-hori-the-old-campaign-put-in-order-the-baldwin-line · known: nobody · told: later
```

```was watcher.the-two-themes 60 1 world/watcher.md
## watcher.the-two-themes
The campaign's two over-arching themes are mass surveillance and the risks of it, and runaway, uncontrollable agentic adversaries which threaten the structure of society. Both are a very real concern that every player at the table is aware of, and the aim is to spook the table with that premise.
status: sketch · by: ingram · source: D078, D031 · from: ledger:31-two-ships-the-watcher-is-the-adversary-and-claude-plays-it-sap-is-nanotech-anabala-and-abraxas-hori-the-old-campaign-put-in-order-the-baldwin-line · known: nobody · told: later
```

## Not merged

- **abraxas** (abraxas.what-she-is, abraxas.host-and-alliance): what-she-is (Claude's wording: a female knowledge demon who lives in Anabala and feeds on information) restates her own the-host and uneasy-alliance, but the two differ in who wrote them (by), so they cannot be joined; once she has gone through her own words, what-she-is is redundant and could simply be cut.

- **abraxas** (abraxas.beside-it, abraxas.dangerous-pair): Same pair, same author, known and told, but beside-it (after Second Impact she is beside the Watcher over Kurose) is the premise of the open decision abraxas.in-the-end (separated from it, or still beside it), so it stays its own item.

- **abraxas** (abraxas.not-mentioned, abraxas.host-and-alliance): not-mentioned is a table rule (say nothing about her until the party makes her surface), not a fact about her; the same flags and her words, but kept apart so the rule can be inked or cut on its own.

- **abraxas** (abraxas.gets-out, abraxas.beside-it): Both place her in the story (loose at the deepest vault; beside the Watcher afterwards), but gets-out is told in Chapter 13 and beside-it later.

- **anabala** (anabala.the-gathering-is-her-plan, anabala.wants-them-home): Her plan (the gathering in Kurose, made possible by Rupiah's diplomacy) serves the same aim and has the same author, known and told, but it carries its own claims about the gathering and about Rupiah, which are also said in mitflits.the-gathering and rupiah.diplomacy-with-mitflits; kept as its own item so it can be inked or cut alone.

- **anabala** (anabala.high-priestess, anabala.knows-everything): Both say she is the mitflits' High Priestess; they differ in author (Claude / her words), in known (some / few) and in told (Chapter 6 / later). Her sentence also adds Prophet and what she knows.

- **anabala** (anabala.remembers, anabala.lived-through-the-ages): Both say she is one of the earliest mitflits, far older than First Impact; they differ in author (Claude / her words) and in known (few / nobody).

- **anabala** (anabala.flew-it, anabala.gen-2-price): Same Gen 2 story from the flying side (she flew it when almost nobody could, Frame Sickness, the mothballed programme, the thin Directorate record); flew-it is known by few, the price and the core by nobody.

- **anabala** (anabala.free-agent, anabala.wants-them-home): Her ultimate objective (a way home for her people) is the same aim as Claude's wants-them-home; they differ in author, so her item and Claude's item stay apart.

- **anabala** (anabala.drawn-to-the-ship, anabala.wakes-the-ship): Same ship and same pull, but drawn-to-the-ship is known by nobody and told later, wakes-the-ship is known by few and told in Chapter 15.

- **anabala** (anabala.recruited-the-four, anabala.macthata): Both are Anabala about 200 years ago; recruited-the-four is known by few and macthata (the old campaign's character) by nobody.

- **anabala** (anabala.free-will, anabala.macthata): Both are the old campaign's Anabala; free-will says 4721 AR and macthata says about 200 years ago (the same moment written two ways, which the checker treats as different dates), and one is a scene in Nirvana while the other is her sheet.

- **anabala** (anabala.ignored-by-the-young, anabala.wants-them-home): Both are the v4 notes on Anabala's standing with her people (ignored by her own young; 'time to go home'); ignored-by-the-young is known by some, wants-them-home (which holds time-to-go-home) by few.

- **anabala** (anabala.first-met, anabala.as-a-student): Two open decisions on how the party first meets Anabala (open decisions are never merged): first-met lists 'as one of the students' among its candidates, which is the very thing as-a-student asks (whether Pippa plays her as a student), so answering one answers part of the other.

- **aoi** (aoi.manakos-wife, aoi.unidentified): Same knowers and same chapter, but they are the premise and the answer of the question aois-name ('Who was the woman filed as unidentified?' answered by aoi.manakos-wife), so they stay two facts.

- **aoi** (aoi.unidentified, aoi.nobody-named-her): Cause and consequence (nobody named her, so 'unidentified' was easy to believe), both known by few, but told in Chapter 11 against later.

- **aoi** (aoi.never-seen, aoi.a-face-on-a-child): Both are known by nobody, but one is a standing rule (the party never sees her) and the other a Chapter 13 reveal (a child wears her face); they would contradict as one item.

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.volkheed, baldwin-manor.mitflit-clan, baldwin-manor.croglang): The manor's history in three steps from one source (the village founded in 4071 AR, the mitflit clan arriving in 4147 AR, the castle in 4201 AR) is one description in three parts, blocked only because each piece carries its own date; if she wants the chronology held in the words, they could become one 'history of Baldwin Manor'.

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.the-siege, baldwin-manor.the-curious-child): The same event: the siege item (Claude's wording from the old notes) restates the battery, the shadow creatures, the four disabling it and the shadows retreating that her own the-curious-child already says; they differ in author (by) and in known (nobody / some), so they cannot be joined. Once she has gone through her words, the siege item is mostly redundant.

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.the-curious-child, baldwin-manor.far-from-the-ship): Both of her statements keep the manor's shadow siege apart from First Impact ('nothing to do with the exotic matter'; 'the two events are not to be confused') and agree in author, known and told, but one is the siege story and the other is geography, and the checker finds almost no shared wording; left apart as the safer call.

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.the-siege, baldwin-manor.the-dark-secret): Both are about what happened at the manor, but the siege is an event and the dark secret is the old GM's plan for its people (ritual sacrifice of prisoners, 'both sides are fantatics'), which she may want to judge separately.

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.manufactory, baldwin-manor.the-family): Same year and same manufactory, but one is the business (exports, the profits report) and the other is people and the materials records; kept apart.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.the-hunters, baldwin.hunter-band): hunter-band names the hunters Baldwin led (Aubert, Kirkham, Thompkins and the rest, with Regina Key); it is the same hunters as the-hunters and the same author, known and told, but it says 4721 AR where the-hunters and gauntlight-army say about 200 years ago (the same moment, written two ways), and the checker refuses different dates. Unifying the date would let all three be one item.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.the-grudge, baldwin.the-hunters): The grudge is Baldwin's old-campaign arc too, but it mostly repeats the shadow siege told in baldwin-manor and his blame over the horse, which is told again in founders.the-horse; kept as its own item.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.alive, baldwin.criminal-lord): alive (Claude: alive two hundred years later, rich, a crime lord, and nobody knows) restates her insanely-rich and criminal-lord; they differ in author (by), so they cannot be joined. Once she has gone through her words, alive is largely redundant.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.insanely-rich, baldwin.criminal-lord): Both are her words with the same flags, but one is his wealth and the hint at how he stayed alive (it feeds the open baldwin.how-alive) and the other is what he is now; the checker finds almost no shared wording, so they were left apart.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.croglang, baldwin.is-the-one-above): Both are the hidden truth about his front and who is above the crime lords, with the same flags, but they make separate claims and share almost no wording (the link is the money, which is baldwin.above-the-crime-lords); left apart.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.above-the-crime-lords, baldwin.is-the-one-above): The plant (Croglang's money leads above the crime lords to someone nobody has seen) and its truth (it is Baldwin) belong together, but the plant is told in Chapter 12 and the truth later.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.impossible-weapons, baldwin.workshops): The plant (gangs carry impossible weapons, known by few, told in Chapter 4) and the truth behind it (enslaved mitflits in his workshops, known by nobody, told later) differ in known and told.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.why-mitflits, baldwin.criminal-lord): Both are her words about the trade, but why-mitflits (mitflits are easy to use, and Baldwin knows it) is known by few and criminal-lord by nobody.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.workshops, baldwin.the-organisation-in-kurose): Both say the heist crews run through Baldwin's organisation; heist-crews was joined with workshops instead because its centre is the weapons trail the party can follow.

- **bobbin** (bobbin.the-sit-in, bobbin.peril): The same sit-in; the-sit-in (known by everyone, told in Chapter 10) already says he can't be pulled out without being killed, while peril (few, later) adds that he is not scheduled to die and the save is a third option.

- **bobbin** (bobbin.peril, bobbin.party-opposed): Both are GM notes on the sit-in from the v4 notes, but peril is known by few and party-opposed by nobody.

- **bobbin** (bobbin.remembers-him, bobbin.debt-and-grievance): Remembering 'the lord's workshop' and Baldwin himself belongs with being freed from it, but remembers-him is known by nobody and the debt by few.

- **bobbin** (bobbin.argues-with-anabala, bobbin.refuses-to-leave): The two-century argument (her answer is to leave, his is to refuse) and its outcome (he refuses to go and stays to hold Kurose) are one line of story, but the argument is known by few and told later, the refusal by some and told in Chapter 13.

- **bobbin** (bobbin.elder, bobbin.named-like-a-tool): Both are known by some, but the elder is told in Chapter 10 and the naming later.

- **candidate-gate** (candidate-gate.roster-ahead, candidate-gate.the-proctors): Same metadata and both are about the gate's people and paperwork ('nobody there is watching anybody' / proctors talking rather than checking), but roster-ahead is the uncanny foreknowledge of the roster and the-proctors a usable gossip hook; unsure they are one thing, so left apart.

- **candidate-gate** (candidate-gate.signs-in-and-out, candidate-gate.the-corrected-figure): Same metadata and both are at the gate's barrier, but the hand-corrected curfew figure is a deliberate detail that 'means nothing more'; she may want to cut it on its own, so left apart.

- **caretaker** (caretaker.grief-for-nozomi, caretaker.misguided-normalcy): Same person, but the grief for Nozomi is known by few and the role of the Caretaker by some, so the table is meant to learn them differently.

- **central-fortification** (central-fortification.by-clearance, central-fortification.the-seat): by-clearance (what each rung of Clearance is let into, and that briefings and hearings are held there) is known by some, the seat and its face by everyone.

- **central-fortification** (central-fortification.built-first, central-fortification.the-seat): The seat and its face are known by everyone, the rock under it only by some; the checker keeps different known levels apart.

- **cicada** (cicada.thin-file, cicada.no-leader-in-the-file): Both describe the Directorate's file on Cicada (a thin file, mostly a nuisance; names no leader), but thin-file is known by some and no-leader-in-the-file by few.

- **cicada** (cicada.heist-loop, cicada.thefts-filed-low): Two halves of the heist loop (the crime lords time demonstrations to cover heists; the thefts are filed low and nobody has laid the dates side by side), but heist-loop is told in Chapter 10 and thefts-filed-low later.

- **cicada** (cicada.faithful, cicada.faithful-believe, cicada.martyrs-argument): Three pieces on the Faithful (peaceful, help strangers; believe the thing is holy; non-resistance is doctrine) that differ in known/told: some in Chapter 2, few in Chapter 6, few later.

- **cicada** (cicada.witness-is-the-goal, cicada.demonstrations, cicada.nobody-dies): witness-is-the-goal ('terror is never the goal, witness is') comes from the same section as the two demonstration pieces, but it is known by few and they by some.

- **cicada** (cicada.marks-paint, cicada.marks-painted-over): Both are about the physical violet marks (marine primer from three harbour suppliers; walls painted over for a decade), but known by few and by some.

- **cicada** (cicada.the-cadre, cicada.cadre-duties, cicada.cadre-vanguard, cicada.inner-circle): The Cadre described in four places that differ in known/told: some and Chapter 3, nobody and later, few and later, nobody and later.

- **cicada** (cicada.cadre-duties, cicada.inner-circle): Same dossier section and same known/told, but two claims (what the Cadre does and how it is trained; the inner circle's personal loyalty to Manako and his revenge list), and the second also overlaps the manako file; left apart (unsure).

- **cicada** (cicada.recurring-four, cicada.lieutenants-witnesses): Both describe the four lieutenants as a group and share the thread; same known/told, but one is how they work as recurring opponents and the other is what they have in common as people, so I read them as two aspects, not one claim (unsure).

- **cicada** (cicada.recurring-four, cicada.lieutenants-learn): lieutenants-learn is her own words (by ingram) and restates recurring-four's gimmick, escape and counter; her words are never joined with Claude's.

- **cicada** (cicada.what-the-public-knows, cicada.thin-file): what-the-public-knows is her own words (by ingram), thin-file is Claude's; the first also restates half of the ink fact cicada.quietly (it has stayed below most people's notice).

- **cicada** (cicada.witness-is-the-goal, cicada.believe-me-now): Both give Cicada's aim (witness, not terror; the single question 'Believe me now?'), but they are different claims from different sections and the second adds Manako's gentler motive; left apart (unsure).

- **cicada** (cicada.prisoners-talk, cicada.not-the-first-fight): Both are guidance on how Cadre fights are run (a live prisoner is worth more; offer a real non-lethal resolution without the Cadre pulling punches), but told later versus Chapter 9.

- **column** (column.starves-it, column.taking-it-down): Both are about what taking the column down achieves (it starves the Watcher before the runaway; it is a triumph that raises more questions than it answers), but starves-it is told later and taking-it-down in Chapter 9.

- **column** (column.the-plant, column.five-years-on-purpose): Both give the column's age of about five years (the second says why), but the-plant is told in Chapter 9 and carries the date, five-years-on-purpose is told later; I merged five-years-on-purpose with the supply-chain facts instead.

- **column** (column.power-bills, column.croglang): Both are Chapter 9 clues pointing at the plant (the power bills lead to it; its books name a client, Croglang), but two different clues, and croglang is on the organised-crime thread.

- **column** (column.the-plant, column.taking-it-down, column.cadre-defends): The Chapter 9 sequence (the plant; the Cadre defends it, some to kill; taking it down) is one scene's worth, but known by nobody, some and nobody, and they are different claims (unsure).

- **commander** (commander.head, commander.mission-first): Both are what the Commander is, but he is the head of the Directorate and seen at a distance for everyone, while that he puts the mission above the people carrying it is known by few.

- **commander** (commander.head, commander.surname): Who he is and the joke about his surname are known by everyone and some respectively; the surname also hangs on the open question of his name.

- **commander** (commander.head, commander.resigns): The resignation is told in Chapter 13, the post from the start of the story: different told.

- **comms-officer** (comms-officer.reads-the-names, comms-officer.breaks-procedure): Both are tells of her care that only a careful listener hears (known by nobody), but one is what she does before a sortie and the other what she does when it goes wrong, so they stay two behaviours.

- **comms-officer** (comms-officer.on-their-side, comms-officer.breaks-procedure): Putting herself outside procedure to protect somebody is the care shown in action; on-their-side is known by some and breaks-procedure by nobody.

- **comms-officer** (comms-officer.radio, comms-officer.name): The open question of her name cannot be merged; it is answered by a decision, not by a merge.

- **deep-storage** (deep-storage.what-it-is, deep-storage.under-the-cranes): what-it-is is the summary (a bunker warehouse of many underground floors) and is known by some; the details are known by few.

- **deep-storage** (deep-storage.unfiled-manifest, deep-storage.old-frames): Both are about what is kept below that nobody has listed; unfiled-manifest is known by nobody and old-frames by few.

- **deep-storage** (deep-storage.under-the-cranes, deep-storage.dug-in-stages): Both say it is hidden under a cargo yard on purpose ('easy to miss and meant to be'; 'nobody audits a warehouse basement'); I paired each with a neighbour instead (the way down, and what it keeps), and either pairing would work.

- **deputy-director** (deputy-director.apologises, deputy-director.answers-straight, deputy-director.holds-both): Three traits of how he speaks (apologises only for trivia; answers a straight question straight; tells people they were wrong and that he is glad they are alive, and stays an officer). All known by nobody and told later, so the checker passes them as one 127-word portrait, but held back as doubtful: they are three separate things a scene could show on its own, and the old NPC file kept them as three separate facts with different ways of being discovered. Optional merge, ready in merge-optional/deputy-director.json.

- **deputy-director** (deputy-director.signature-on-the-letter, deputy-director.reachable): The signature on the letter is known by everyone, his manner and standing only by some or nobody.

- **deputy-director** (deputy-director.reachable, deputy-director.holds-both): Standing and temperament (some) against how he behaves with the people in front of him (nobody): different known, so the checker keeps them apart.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.range-safety-officer, directorate-posts.range-safety-officer-his-call-is-not-arguable): The main post entry already says his 'no' on a Frame or a pilot is not really arguable, but it is known by some and the two small facts by nobody; the checker keeps different known levels apart.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.the-rock-warden, directorate-posts.the-rock-research-director): Two posts at one place, but two different people: each is the basis for a separate NPC, so folding them into one item would put two characters under one id.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.vermilion-block-custodian, directorate-posts.vermilion-block-director): Two posts in one department, deliberately contrasted (the custodian is no spellcaster by design, the director is a very powerful one), but two different people; left as one item per person.

- **directorate** (directorate.kortos-coalition, directorate.born-at-kortos): The strongest near-merge, blocked only by authorship: kortos-coalition is Ingram's own words (the Directorate born on Kortos about 195 years ago from the organisations round the opened ship; the move to Kurose for the prophecy came later) and born-at-kortos is Claude's version of the same story with extra detail (the four at its heart, two jobs, the early work that frightened the four). Her words can only be joined as she said them, never merged with Claude's; if she approves, Claude's extra sentences could be folded into hers.

- **directorate** (directorate.reads-the-messages, directorate.consent-in-the-forms): The same fact told twice (the Directorate reads trainees' messages, and the consent is in the paperwork handed over at the start), but known by few and told in chapter 8 against known by some and told later.

- **directorate** (directorate.reads-the-messages, directorate.two-readers): Both told in chapter 8 and both about who reads the party's messages, but the reading itself is known by few and the fact that there are two readers who do not know about each other by nobody. They also have different jobs in canon/questions/who-reads-what-they-write.md: two-readers is the answer and reads-the-messages a road to it, so they are deliberately two.

- **directorate** (directorate.rite-of-passage, directorate.not-a-conspiracy): Both say the Directorate has never been tested ('entirely untested' against 'an institution that has never been tested'), but rite-of-passage is known by some and not-a-conspiracy by nobody.

- **directorate** (directorate.paperwork-disappears-people, directorate.leaves-it-lying): Both are about how Aoi was filed and why her name never reached the Directorate, but one is known by nobody and the other by some.

- **directorate** (directorate.unknowingly-calls-it, directorate.the-dig-is-public): Both are about the dig under Kurose (it is a public-works programme; it is exactly what calls the Watcher), but the first is known by everyone and the second by nobody.

- **directorate** (directorate.deepest-vault, directorate.vault-goal, directorate.falls-to-the-six): The three chapter 13 facts about the deepest vault and what follows it share a told but are known by few, nobody and everyone respectively.

- **directorate** (directorate.contact, directorate.response-roster, directorate.claimants): One small vocabulary of the plotting floor (a contact, the response roster, the Claimants), but known by everyone / everyone / some and told later / in Episode Two's The Hammer / in Episode Two's First Contact.

- **directorate** (directorate.first-gen-1, directorate.born-at-kortos): Two stages of the same history of the Directorate and its technology, but dated about 150 and about 195 years ago; a merged item could not carry both dates.

- **directorate** (directorate.under-arms, directorate.magical-swat): Her two D068 statements about the Directorate's armed people (a large number under arms and training; it brings the party in as a magical armed-response team) are known by everyone and by few, and her words are only ever joined, not mixed.

- **directorate** (directorate.unknowingly-calls-it, directorate.not-a-conspiracy): Both known by nobody, but the first is a specific GM-only secret about the Watcher and the second the tone of the whole institution; kept as two things.

- **directorate** (directorate.acts-on-intercepts, directorate.reads-the-messages): Acting on what it has read is the later consequence of reading the messages, but the first is known by nobody and told later, the second known by few and told in chapter 8.

- **envoy** (envoy.the-doll, envoy.not-good-at-bodies): The disfigured doll lurching into a public street is told in both, but in Chapter 11 in one and later in the other.

- **envoy** (envoy.police-kept-the-truth, envoy.shot): Same knowers and same chapter, but the police file is a clue and envoy.was-a-greeting is the answer of the question what-the-envoy-was, so the file is kept out of the answer.

- **envoy** (envoy.police-kept-the-truth, envoy.two-institutions): The police file and the two institutions' reports are one subject, but told in Chapter 11 against later.

- **envoy** (envoy.first-hello, envoy.was-a-greeting): 'The Watcher's first attempt to meet people' and 'a greeting' are close, but first-hello is known by nobody and carries a two-date question.

- **envoy** (envoy.manako-broke-down, envoy.the-disturbance): Hers (the circle scattered with Manako) against Claude's street scene; different authors.

- **envoy** (envoy.nobody-planned-it, envoy.watcher-learned): Both are about what runs downhill from the shot, but one is the theme of the day and the other the Watcher's lesson.

- **envoy** (envoy.not-good-at-bodies, envoy.the-disturbance): reviewed and left apart: One is a general fact about the Watcher (it is not good at bodies, the Envoy's look only shows it) and the other is what Hori was called to; they answer different questions, a scene could tell either alone, and they share only the street.

- **era** (era.golarion-1950s, era.fifties-feel): Both open with 'the setting' and the 1950s, but golarion-1950s is about how the world relates to real Golarion (from the ideas file, D077) while fifties-feel is the look of Kurose; the checker finds almost no shared content.

- **era** (era.dug-up-not-invented, era.uneven-tech): Heavy overlap (Frame technology is narrow, scarce and gated, derived from what was dug up) but dug-up-not-invented is known by few and uneven-tech by everyone.

- **era** (era.golarion-1950s, era.loosely-golarion): Both say how closely the world follows Golarion, but one is Claude's and known by everyone, the other hers and known by nobody.

- **era** (era.magic-draining, era.dug-up-not-invented): Two reasons the age is what it is (magic draining and industry growing in; technology dug out of the ships), same knowers and same chapter, but different claims.

- **exotic-matter** (exotic-matter.the-convoy, exotic-matter.convoy-carries): The plant (chapter 8: an armoured convoy goes down to the vaults and nobody tells the party what it carries) and its payoff (chapter 13: it is exotic matter); known by some versus few, told in different chapters.

- **exotic-matter** (exotic-matter.in-the-search-ship, exotic-matter.convoy-carries): Both say the Directorate guards it more carefully than anything it owns, so that sentence now sits in both merged items; told later versus chapter 13.

- **founders** (founders.older-printing, founders.page-forty): Both open with the same claim (the school's approved history names all four as heroes and never says how the rift opened) and then add different details; blocked because older-printing is known by few and page-forty by some.

- **founders** (founders.page-forty, founders.the-tidying): Both are the two-centuries-of-tidying idea (nobody alive has examined it; nobody is hiding the true account); blocked because page-forty is known by some and the-tidying by nobody.

- **founders** (founders.older-printing, founders.pictures-are-the-lie): The memorial plate and the chapter 12 conclusion that the official pictures are the lie are one thread, but older-printing is told later and known by few, pictures-are-the-lie is told in chapter 12.

- **founders** (founders.founding-day-anthem, founders.household-names): Same metadata and both are the founders' place in everyday civic life (a holiday; children named after them and exam questions); unsure they are one thing, so left apart.

- **founders** (founders.diplomacy-first, founders.the-inquisitors): Both are the four's first weeks around Otari; blocked because diplomacy-first is known by few and the-inquisitors by nobody.

- **founders** (founders.diplomacy-first, founders.as-they-were): Same metadata, both the four in the old campaign, but one is what they did (made peace) and the other who they were (their classes and their doubts about one another); left apart.

- **founders** (founders.a-week, founders.the-plea): a-week (Volkheed to Nirvana took about a week in midsummer 4721 AR) has the same metadata and spans the same final stretch, but it is a timing claim about the whole run, not part of either event; left apart.

- **founders** (founders.the-nuns, founders.the-horse): Both are the four's quarrel with Baldwin's hunters, but they are two separate episodes; left apart.

- **founders** (founders.can-be-eclipsed, founders.level): The other half of D047's sentence: the ink founders.level says they are level 15, can-be-eclipsed says the party can in the end surpass them. An ink fact is never merged.

- **frames** (frames.frame-sickness, frames.sickness-per-machine): The same rule twice (Slowed 1 for the first fight in each Gen 2), Claude's and hers, both known by few and told in chapter 4; blocked only by `by`. Claude's adds just that only Anabala and the senior staff know it exists until Gen 2 is offered.

- **frames** (frames.catalogue, frames.utility-range): Both say the party's first machines come from a range of converted diggers and other utility rigs; Claude's catalogue (with the crane rig, army trucks, sports car and helicopter) restates her utility-range, and they differ only in `by`.

- **frames** (frames.gen-1-diggers, frames.gen-1-anyone-can): Claude's gen-1-diggers (clankers, hard to work, anyone trained can drive one) is contained in her gen-1-anyone-can; same known and told, differ only in `by`.

- **frames** (frames.gen-1-anyone-can, frames.gen-1-not-alive): Her two descriptions of a Gen 1 (anyone can drive it; it has no living core), but one is known by everyone and the other by few.

- **frames** (frames.status-ladder, frames.utility-range, frames.sent-because-ready): Three of her facts that tell one story (utility duty is the bottom of the ladder; the first machines are utility rigs; the party is sent to real combat because their machines were on hand), blocked by `known` (everyone / some / nobody).

- **frames** (frames.never-takes-control, frames.mitflit-a-person): Both say nothing in a Gen 2 takes control from its pilot; hers is known by nobody and so is Claude's, but `by` differs, and Claude's adds that a mitflit is always a person.

- **frames** (frames.gen-2-inside, frames.the-empty-seat, frames.disguise-not-sickness): All three are about the seat shaping itself to the pilot's true form; gen-2-inside is told in chapter 5 (the others later) and disguise-not-sickness is known by nobody (the others by few).

- **frames** (frames.gen-1-clank-grace, frames.look-of-gen-1): Same metadata and both describe a Gen 1, but one is how it feels to drive (the baseline that lets a Gen 2's grace land) and the other how it looks; unsure they are one thing, so left apart.

- **frames** (frames.gen-2-living, frames.gen-2-core): Claude's one-line gen-2-living (living flesh melded with the ship's technology) restates her gen-2-core; blocked by `by`, `known` (nobody / few) and `told` (chapter 4 / later).

- **frames** (frames.mitflit-a-person, frames.mitflit-fits-the-seat): Same metadata and both are about a mitflit and a Gen 2 seat (nothing is overridden), but one is a standing rule for the GM (a mitflit is always a person, never a component) and the other a story beat (the city learns it may not need the children); she may want to ink the rule and park the beat, so left apart.

- **general-hospital** (general-hospital.chief-of-staff, general-hospital.not-the-infirmary): Same place, but the Chief of Staff is known by some and the building and its role by everyone.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.buried, generation-ship.inside): The outside of the ship (hidden valley, black obelisk, taken for a temple) and its inside (reactors, quarters, bridge, reasoning-core chamber, entrance computer) are the same ship, known by few, told later; I first joined them, but they are two separate descriptions (the discovery and the layout) sharing almost no words, so I left them apart. Easy to join if you prefer.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.the-way-in, generation-ship.mitflit-village): The same expedition in 4721 AR (the way in, and what the four found), but the-way-in is known by nobody and mitflit-village by few.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.restricted, generation-ship.technology-leaked): technology-leaked begins 'anyway', right after the restriction; restricted is known by few (Ingram's words) and technology-leaked by nobody.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.inside, generation-ship.the-way-in): Both give the entrance computer's questions (geometry, gravity, the letters of life, the shells of the elements, the golden ratio); known few versus nobody, and only the-way-in is dated 4721 AR.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.crashed, generation-ship.buried): crashed is the dated anchor event (about 20000 years ago) that search-ship.crashed and the README's example hang from, so I kept it as its own item rather than dating the whole description of the wreck onto it.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.mind-and-eyes, generation-ship.sap-store): Two of the ship's parts as identified today (mind is Mother, eyes are the Watcher; the stock is Sap), same known and told, but separate claims, so not plainly one thing.

- **glamour** (glamour.native-or-alien, glamour.the-ten-natives): A rule and its example (a people is native exactly when it is a PF2e ancestry; here are the ten that are), but the rule is Ingram's words (by: ingram) and the list is Claude's (by: claude), which a merge never mixes.

- **glamour** (glamour.the-road-in-choice, glamour.native-or-alien): Both are Ingram's words and both known by everyone and told later, and both concern aliens at induction, but one is whether a character disguises on the road in and the other what counts as alien: two different rulings.

- **hori** (hori.and-the-soldier, hori.and-the-commander): Two unmet counterparts of the same shape (both known by nobody, both told later), but they are different people from different sources and share almost no content, so left as two.

- **hori** (hori.carried-alone, hori.man-of-honour, hori.dutiful): carried-alone ('Hori doesn't agree with his colleagues') answers man-of-honour directly, but it is Claude's wording known by few, against hers known by some.

- **hori** (hori.how-to-be-believed, hori.peril, hori.kept-her-name): All three say what saves him is being asked and being believed, but they differ in who knows (nobody / few / few) and when it is told (later / later / Chapter 11).

- **hori** (hori.carried-alone, hori.peril): Both say he has carried it alone, with identical details, but peril is the Peril Roster entry (v4:21) and is kept as its own fact.

- **hori** (hori.cleared-in-court, hori.self-imposed-disgrace): 'Retired anyway' answers the court clearing him, but the court is known by few and the retirement by some, so the clearing sits in the after-the-shot item and the retirement in the retired item.

- **hulda-brack** (hulda-brack.machines-first, hulda-brack.the-shop-boss): Her one legible want and her manner are two long paragraphs from the same NPC note, both known by some; joined they would reach the 170-word cap, and she may want to judge the want and the manner separately.
