- **hulda-brack** (hulda-brack.refuses-officers, hulda-brack.machines-first): refuses-officers (she has refused officers to their faces; rank does not move her) restates parts of machines-first (nothing leaves her floor she hasn't signed off) and of her manner (unbothered by rank); known by few versus some.

- **hulda-brack** (hulda-brack.useless-is-right, hulda-brack.no-secrets): Both known by nobody and from the same note, but one is a tip about winning her attention and the other the NPC's design role (no hidden agenda, the Section Officer's inverse).

- **hulda-brack** (hulda-brack.nuar, hulda-brack.the-shop-boss): The shop-boss description (tough-love) already gives her build and horns, and nuar says she is an alien who wears no glamour; they differ in known (everyone / some).

- **hyuck** (hyuck.the-carrying, hyuck.never-lost): The Carrying (the gnoll rite) and Hyuck's own case of it (carried from body to body for two hundred years, never lost, a crowd in his body) are one thing in two pieces told in Chapter 12, but the rite is known by few and Hyuck's survival by nobody.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.never-lost, hyuck.unnamed-until-12): The same Chapter 12 beat (hyuck-unmasked), with the same flags, but two separate claims (how he is alive; that the test pilot is Hyuck and the statue was always him), kept apart. The second also restates the decided hyuck.test-pilot and hyuck.statue-truth, adding only when the table learns it.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.bonebox, hyuck.the-carrying): bonebox's last sentence (gnolls carry their great dead's skulls to keep their spirits close) is the Carrying's first sentence again, but bonebox is told later and dated about 200 years ago, the Carrying is told in Chapter 12.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.bonebox, hyuck.fratricide): Both are about the elder brother's skull he carried in the old campaign; bonebox is known by few and dated, fratricide is known by nobody.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.hunts-his-father, hyuck.old-ways): Both come from Hyuck Bonebox's old character sheet and both mention his trunk of ancestral bones, but one is his quest (feeding the open hyuck.fathers-portrait) and the other his superstitions; kept apart.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.abadars-mark, hyuck.haruspex-stone): Two more episodes from the same old sheet with the same flags (a divine curse and his guilt; a haruspex's reading and a stone), but unrelated to each other.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.catchphrases, hyuck.how-to-play): His catchphrases are an example of the voice that how-to-play describes, with the same flags, but added to the merged how-to-play (which already holds never-quite-falls) the item would pass 150 words.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.statue-an-earlier-body, hyuck.unnamed-until-12): Both are the players putting the statue, the plate and the test pilot together; one is told later and may happen in any scene, the other is the Chapter 12 reveal.

- **directorate-episode-two** (idea--speak-for-the-comms-officer, idea--a-doctor-without-an-appointment): Two different moments of Episode Two's debrief (what the party says about the Comms Officer; the corridor wait and Shucks's access) with different threads: each can be taken without the other.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--the-mirror-of-two-institutions, idea--the-institution-absorbs-reform, idea--nobody-to-unmask, idea--no-side-to-pick): Four facets of one stance (the Directorate and Cicada are the same sincere failure, not a villain and a hero): the mirror is the thesis, absorbed reform is a prediction for play, and the merged note is the rule for villains and sides; together they run far over the 170-word cap, and each is its own thing to accept.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--the-core-theme, idea--enduring-not-restoring): Two statements of the campaign's throughline (a group winning its own independence from a system that is never satisfied; a dire trajectory in which nothing is restored): they may be complementary or alternatives, which is hers to say.

- **misc-antagonists** (idea--the-dig-is-the-danger, idea--something-worse-beyond): Two different fillings for the reserved late-campaign 'real danger' reveal (the dig wakes the ship; something worse lies beyond), in different states (live, parked): alternatives, never merged.

- **misc-clues** (idea--censored-papers, idea--glamoured-paper, idea--recruitment-manga): Three different Directorate-document devices (clearance-censored versions of one paper; per-player copies that spell a hidden message when laid side by side; a recruitment manga that undercuts itself): different mechanisms, each its own decision.

- **misc-hyuck-origin** (idea--hyuck-offended-a-god, idea--hyuck-brothers-skull, idea--hyuck-contained-asset): Three parked pitches for what Hyuck was and what became of him (he offended a god and was thrown forward in time; he carries a construct of his idealised brother; the Directorate keeps him contained): alternatives, and the file itself says nothing in the brainstorm was decided.

- **misc-hyuck-origin** (idea--hyuck-mad-scientist, idea--hyuck-psychic-cockpit): Two live angles on Hyuck and a Frame (someone leads him into trying to put his brother's soul in one; his beliefs latch onto a Gen 2's living core): near each other, but one is a book iii plot event and the other a standing cockpit condition, so left for her to pick or combine.

- **people-founders** (idea--the-carrying-in-detail, idea--a-seat-that-cannot-settle): The seat is a consequence for Gen 2 of the crowd inside Hyuck, a separate thing to accept; joined to the Carrying it would also run well over the 170-word cap.

- **people-founders** (idea--the-carrying-in-detail, idea--a-carrying-seen): a-carrying-seen is parked (whether the party sees a Carrying, and whether Steve's offended-god pitch comes in) while the rest are live: different states are never merged.

- **people-founders** (idea--mothers-pieces, idea--flashback-two-moves-to-anabala): Both come from Anabala building Gen 2, but one is a claim about where the cores came from and the other a flashback scene: two separate decisions.

- **places-hooks** (idea--the-rock-visit, idea--the-rock-standing, idea--nine-and-nine): Three ideas from the one place record of The Rock: a visit scene, a proposed new kind of standing tracked apart from Clearance (a mechanic, not a scene), and a link between the Rock's nine cells and the Hangar's nine bays: three separate decisions.

- **places-hooks** (idea--deep-storage-is-the-vaults, idea--deep-storage-requisitions, idea--deep-storage-custodian-cast): Deep Storage three ways: what it is (the vaults, or only an attic), scene hooks, and whether the custodian becomes a named recurring character: three separate decisions.

- **places-hooks** (idea--vermilion-posting, idea--the-director-knows-more): A set of scene hooks for the Vermilion Block and the open question of what its director knows; the question is not a hook and would sit better as an open fact.

- **sweep-ledger** (idea--the-sorters, idea--the-charters-black-bar): Both are rejected, so their wording is retired and they are never merged with anything.

- **sweep-ledger** (idea--four-portraits-confuse, idea--founders-letterhead): Both use the founders' approved picture, but one is live and one parked, and every idea in this file is Ingram's own words, which a merge may only join exactly: left apart.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--everything-becomes-what-it-was-mistaken-for, idea--a-thing-in-the-wrong-container): Two different thematic keys from the old bible (the mechanism: everything becomes what it was mistaken for; the image: everything is a thing in the wrong container), each over 100 words and each a separate claim to accept or drop.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--the-campus-in-daylight, idea--memorial-assembly, idea--heroes-and-they-hate-it): Three successive campus beats (an attack in daylight, the memorial assembly, the public shelter with the party as heroes): a run of separate scenes, not one idea.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--five-trails, idea--corroboration-from-the-wrong-direction, idea--contradictory-testimony, idea--pre-dated-graffiti): Four different devices for building the investigation (five trails, an uncredited second witness, contradictory testimony, a pre-dated clue): each is its own decision.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--one-death-precisely-placed, idea--what-hand-sized-threats-take): Both are about what the Watcher's children first take from the kids, but one is a single accidental death and the other says it is trust and time, not a life: kept apart (and see the conflict with nobody-has-to-die).

- **sweep-v4** (idea--the-three-modes): Parked, and at 172 words already over the 170-word cap on its own, so it cannot join anything; its same-hole-in-every-Gen-2 premise is the plant in idea--the-hull-breach (unplanted-clues).

- **unplanted-clues** (idea--the-hull-breach, idea--the-stores-cupboard, idea--the-gentle-frame): Three different plants with three different payoffs (chapters 5, 9 and 7), all parked: the pieces carry different dates, so they are never merged.

- **watcher-mitflits** (idea--the-slow-reveal, idea--the-first-mitflit): Two different starts for the party's first mitflit (glimpsed salvaging at a wreck and not followed up; caught in a wall trap, furious, and the choice to free it): alternatives for one moment, in different states (live, parked).

- **watcher-mitflits** (idea--why-gen-2-unsettles, idea--mother-in-the-cores): Two live ideas on the Gen 2 and the mitflits' ship (the wild mitflits' unease; Mother's pieces in every core): related and compatible, but each is its own claim, and Mother's pieces are conditional ('if').

- **watcher-the-swarm** (idea--claimants-stake-claims, idea--escheat): Both read the setting's property-law words as accidentally literal and both come from the one old claimant-guidelines note, but one is about the Watcher's children and the other about the title, the ships' heirs and the dig: two readings, left for her.

- **inicrium** (inicrium.shadow-plane, inicrium.unstable): shadow-plane is about any great concentration of magical energy meeting the Shadow Plane (it happened at Baldwin Manor through an Inicrium battery), not stated to be Inicrium's own behaviour; unsure it belongs in the same item.

- **kiri** (kiri.illusionist, kiri.the-card): Both told in Chapter 3 and both about her private shows, but illusionist is known by some and the-card by few.

- **kiri** (kiri.the-card, kiri.recruits): The card is the way into her Chapter 6 show where Cicada recruits, but the-card is told in Chapter 3 and recruits in Chapter 6.

- **kiri** (kiri.succession, kiri.loves-manako): Her succession plan is the same loyalty that makes her love him, but succession is told in Chapter 12 and loves-manako later.

- **kiri** (kiri.how-she-met-him, kiri.loves-manako): How she met Manako (shown the lens, asked to look) is the start of the devotion; same known/told, but a separate backstory fact that also carries the 'witness, not believer' line, so I left it apart (unsure).

- **kiri** (kiri.wants-good-shows, kiri.shares-a-teacher): Both are about her craft (she is the only one in Cicada who cares about it; she shares a craft and possibly a teacher with Rupiah), but they are two different claims, left apart (unsure).

- **kudo** (kudo.the-lawyer, kudo.the-speech): Same man, same known and told, and the speech is the 'very loud' of the first fact; left apart because the speech says she has been in a cell for four months (the first detention) while the-lawyer says he fought her out once and will do it louder the second time, so which fight the speech belongs to is not settled and a merge would settle it by accident. The checker also finds the wording unrelated. Unsure.

- **kudo** (kudo.the-lawyer, kudo.beat-the-commander): He fought her out of military detention once and he beat the Commander in tribunal may be the same tribunal, but the-lawyer is known by some and beat-the-commander by few. Ingram may want to say whether it is one event.

- **kurose-police** (kurose-police.distrust-directorate, kurose-police.jurisdiction-quarrel): Her distrust items and Claude's jurisdiction items are the same quarrel (jurisdiction is the reason for the distrust), but they differ in author (by), so they cannot be joined.

- **kurose-police** (kurose-police.jurisdiction-quarrel, kurose-police.the-file): 'Ask them for anything else and you get nowhere' and 'getting the file means persuading the police to trust the party' go together, but jurisdiction-quarrel (which holds raids-only) is known by some and told later, the-file by few and told in Chapter 11.

- **kurose** (kurose.showy-magic-frowned-on, kurose.magic-shows): Claude's eight-word rule is the first clause of her magic-shows (showy magic is frowned on, except in an emergency); same known and told, blocked only by `by`. Her version stands and Claude's adds nothing.

- **kurose** (kurose.showy-magic-frowned-on, kurose.small-signs): Same metadata, and the first two small signs (a cantrip mended with an apology, a shopkeeper blaming a caster) are examples of the rule; but small-signs is mostly other colour (traders beside coal cranes, red tile, the mitflit notice, draft paperwork), so it is not a rule and its example. Left apart.

- **kurose** (kurose.cicadas-revered, kurose.cicada-link): The same source (the cicada motif); the open question of when the civic reverence and the cult's emblem get connected is never merged into a pencil fact.

- **lens** (lens.degrees, lens.always-works): The table of what a look shows by degree of success belongs to the same check (DC 18 plus a hidden modifier), and I first joined it in; the two share almost no words and the table is a separate design she may want to decide on its own, so I kept it apart. Easy to join if you prefer one 'lens check' item.

- **lens** (lens.treated-with-sap, lens.what-it-shows): 'Treated with a chemical' and 'the chemical is Sap' are one description, but what-it-shows is told in chapter 6 and treated-with-sap later, so the rules keep them apart.

- **lens** (lens.never-without, lens.nobody-sells-one): Both say that after the first sight the party cannot see the Watcher again without making a lens, understanding the chemical; never-without is known by nobody and told later, nobody-sells-one is known by some and told in chapter 6.

- **lens** (lens.never-without, lens.what-it-shows): what-it-shows's last sentence ('never visible any other way, except where the chemical touches it') is the same claim as never-without; known few and told in chapter 6 versus known nobody and told later.

- **lens** (lens.nobody-sells-one, lens.three-ways): A chain (nobody sells one, so there are three ways to get one), but known by some versus few and told in chapter 6 versus chapter 7.

- **lens** (lens.feeds-it, lens.treated-with-sap): Both say a lens is Sap (and so feeds the Watcher); feeds-it is known by nobody and treated-with-sap by few.

- **main-mech-hangar** (main-mech-hangar.nine-bays, main-mech-hangar.three-empty): Two halves of one image (nine bays in a row; six hold Frames and three are swept, lit, numbered and empty), blocked only by `known`: the bays are known by everyone and the three empty ones by some.

- **main-school-complex** (main-school-complex.what-it-is, main-school-complex.two-schools-joined): Both say the school and the technical college are stacked into one block because the island ran out of room to spread; blocked because what-it-is is known by everyone and two-schools-joined by some.

- **main-school-complex** (main-school-complex.the-real-archive, main-school-complex.nobody-compares): A claim and its consequence (the registrar's real archive of placement data; nobody there compares notes across sections, so several unusual results would first show there); blocked because the archive is known by few and nobody-compares by nobody.

- **main-school-complex** (main-school-complex.what-it-is, main-school-complex.word-travels): Same metadata, but word-travels is a social consequence (everyone's children pass through the same building, so news reaches every family within the week), not the same thing as the building's description; left apart.

- **manako** (manako.ostracised, manako.was-warning, manako.suspected-right): These three are one sentence of hers (D068) split three ways. was-warning and suspected-right are joined (both known by few); ostracised alone is tagged known: some, so it stays apart. If you agree it is 'few' too, the three would be one item.

- **manako** (manako.the-failed-demonstration, manako.checks-first): The same room where nothing happened and the lesson he took from it, but known by few against known by nobody.

- **manako** (manako.the-failed-demonstration, manako.the-conference): He failed to show his colleagues before the conference and succeeded at it: two events in sequence, not one.

- **manako** (manako.the-circle, manako.the-conference): The circle forms after the conference with the same date and knowers, but the-circle is a clue road in two questions (what-the-envoy-was and aois-name), so it stays its own fact.

- **manako** (manako.the-bill, manako.the-papers-are-his, manako.revenge-list): The three separate roads of 'Who wrote Cicada?' (questions/who-wrote-cicada.md); merging them would collapse three independent clues into one fact.

- **manako** (manako.the-papers, manako.the-papers-are-his): The same papers: an anonymous discredited study told in Chapter 4 (known by few) and their authorship told in Chapter 12 (known by nobody).

- **manako** (manako.revenge-list, manako.list-worked-by-inner-circle): The same list, but told in Chapter 12 against later, and revenge-list is a clue road.

- **manako** (manako.the-only-rope, manako.killing-him-is-the-wrong-choice): Same idea (only he can steer the Watcher, so killing him is wrong) but Claude's words against hers, told in Chapter 12 against later, and the-only-rope is a clue road.

- **manako** (manako.underworld-dealings, manako.ways-to-be-noticed): ways-to-be-noticed spells out the second half of her sentence, but it is Claude's wording and known by few against her by-ingram item.

- **manako** (manako.petty-crime-chemist, manako.ways-to-be-noticed): Both are about the criminals with the same details, but the early petty jobs and the later arrangement are different phases, and 'in return' needs the arm's-length context.

- **manako** (manako.slide-and-movements, manako.arms-length, manako.kind-to-strangers): slide-and-movements restates the arm's-length work and the kindness and then maps them onto the movements; merged with either it would pass the 170-word cap.

- **manako** (manako.gifted-chemist, manako.underworld-dealings): Both hers and both about his slide, but two separate statements (D031 and D028); left apart.

- **manako** (manako.his-number, manako.appearances): his-number is the full rule behind 'one of them may have his number' in the merged appearances item, but it is known by few (the GM and that player) against known by nobody.

- **manako** (manako.half-suspects, manako.frightened): reviewed and left apart: Half-suspects is a standing belief kept up for forty years, while the Frightened Man is the fourth movement of his arc (it belongs with the movements); they share the keep-the-question-unaskable motive but are a standing state and a late stage, told at different points.

- **manako** (manako.moods, manako.voice-by-movement): reviewed and left apart: What each mood does to his children and how each movement changes his voice answer different questions and serve different scenes (a fight, a conversation); they share only the movements, and the join needed a connective the pieces do not have ('In how he speaks').

- **manako** (manako.list-worked-by-inner-circle, manako.suspects-nobu): reviewed and left apart: The revenge list the inner circle works through and the suspicion that Nobu funds the column (a separate reveal held in reserve, which belongs beside nobu.funds-the-column) share only Nobu.

- **mayor** (mayor.fan-of-directorate, mayor.glad-to-say-so): The same claim: the city has done well commercially out of the Directorate and the mayor is glad of it. Same known (everyone) and told, but fan-of-directorate is her words (D068) and glad-to-say-so is Claude's restatement, so they differ in author and cannot be joined; once she has inked her sentence, glad-to-say-so is redundant and could be cut.

- **mayor** (mayor.fan-of-directorate, mayor.support-forces-cooperation, mayor.own-agent): All three are her words on the mayor (D068) and follow from one another (a fan because of the money; so strong a backer that police and Directorate are forced to cooperate; still their own agent), but they differ in who knows them (everyone / some / few), so they cannot be joined.

- **mitflit-home-world** (mitflit-home-world.why-ships-fall, mitflit-home-world.who-fells-ships): The pencil says the logs of the derelicts explain why ships fall at Golarion (Chapter 17); the open decision asks why they fall at all (the gods, something else, or never explained). One is the plan for when the table learns it, the other the answer it waits for; an open decision is never merged.

- **mitflit-home-world** (mitflit-home-world.seeds, mitflit-home-world.the-problem): seeds is a list of possible answers to the open the-problem (the home world lost ships too; the High mitflits don't want the wild ones back; the gods' reason for felling ships; a ship's mind has a name at home), which makes it an idea more than a fact; kept as it stands.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.degraded, mitflits.muddled): Both are her accounts of the crew's descendants (alien and robotic, became weaker and the least of races; star-faring robots whose biology, magic and cybernetics became muddled, a joke), but degraded is known by few and muddled by nobody.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.descended-from-crew, mitflits.degraded): Claude's 'descended from the generation ship's crew' is said again in her degraded; both known by few, but they differ in author, so they cannot be joined.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.young-pilots, mitflits.freedom): The same generational split (the young volunteer for the machines; the elders refuse); young-pilots is her words and the-trap-repeats is Claude's elaboration, so they differ in author.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.avoid-gen-2, mitflits.dont-know-gen-2): The same discomfort around Gen 2; avoid-gen-2 is Claude's plant in Chapter 5 and dont-know-gen-2 is her rule that the mitflits do not instantly understand where Gen 2 comes from, so author and told differ.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.gauntlight, mitflits.gauntlight-council): Two parts of the same Gauntlight Keep story (the founders befriending the band and giving them the keep; the council and its marketplace), but gauntlight is known by few and the council by nobody.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.the-gathering, mitflits.rare-at-first): The same gathering seen two ways (mitflits converge on Kurose; they are rare at first and grow commoner because they are gathering on purpose), same author and known, but the-gathering is told in Chapter 6 and rare-at-first at the start.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.the-gathering, mitflits.going-missing): Both are the Chapter 6 gathering, but the gathering is known by everyone and the missing mitflits by few.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.look, mitflits.high-mitflits): high-mitflits repeats part of her look (elegant, unblemished and aloof) and adds that the party meets them in Book V; author, known and told all differ.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.rude, mitflits.wild): never-vermin ends 'and thought rude', which is her rude again; they differ in author (her words / Claude).

- **mitflits** (mitflits.keen, mitflits.degraded): Both are her words from the same section about what mitflits remember of where they came from (some communities kept the memory; many would not believe it; all are keen for the adventure), with the same flags, but one is history and the other present attitude; left apart.

- **mother** (mother.old-campaign, mother.met-by-the-four): The same old-campaign meeting told twice (Mother as a level-15 incorporeal construct, in the mitflit village and on the cargo deck), but old-campaign is known by nobody and met-by-the-four by few; they also disagree about where the village is (see conflicts).

- **mother** (mother.ships-mind, mother.never-pulverised): Both have Mother weakened at First Impact, but the dates read '4721 AR' and 'about 200 years ago', which are the same year in different words and which the rules treat as different.

- **mother** (mother.faint-voice, mother.speaks-clearly): The fragment's voice at the museum case in chapter 6 and again, clearly, in chapter 11: one continuing fact, but told in different chapters (and mother.voice, open, decides whether it speaks at all).

- **mother** (mother.the-fragment, mother.proof): The fragment as the party's proof on the mitflit home world is its use in Book V, a different job from what it is, so I kept it apart.

- **nao** (nao.faithful, nao.not-a-spy): The same dossier from two angles (among Cicada's files is one for Nao; Nao's dossier is the one that lands), both told in chapter 9, but faithful is known by few and not-a-spy by nobody.

- **nao** (nao.classmate, nao.faithful): Nao is a classmate and one of the quiet Faithful, as the chapter 9 beat puts it in one sentence, but classmate is known by some and faithful by few (and told later against chapter 9).

- **nao** (nao.faithful, nao.peril): Peril is what comes of the dossier being found (turned in, disappearing into detention); same known (few) but faithful is told in chapter 9 and peril later.

- **night-regulars** (night-regulars.tsubaki, night-regulars.imai, night-regulars.aucourt, night-regulars.gennai, night-regulars.kuroki, night-regulars.mori): Ward Four's six, the General Hospital rest. Same metadata, so the checker would pass any join, but these are six people (the night sister, two long-stay patients, the soldier, the porter, the cleaner), each with an NPC file, and 682 words together against a 170-word cap. Imai and Kuroki lie in neighbouring beds and refer to each other; that links two people, it does not make one description.

- **night-regulars** (night-regulars.kiku, night-regulars.sanzo, night-regulars.tama): The Paper Lantern's counter. Chapter One's plan names these three in one line, so they travel together, but they are three people with an NPC file each and 271 words together. Left apart so she can ink or cut each one.

- **night-regulars** (night-regulars.michiru, night-regulars.lucan, night-regulars.chiyo): The Card Game's dealer, cardsharp and player who never wins. Lucan cheats for Chiyo in the scene: a link between two people, not one description. 261 words together.

- **night-regulars** (night-regulars.genzo, night-regulars.nadi): The Met Office night crew. The one pair short enough to join under the cap (157 words), and Nadi is written as the observer's assistant, so it is the likeliest join if she wants fewer items. Left apart because they are two people, each readable alone, and joining them is her call.

- **night-regulars** (night-regulars.tokuji, night-regulars.mira): The Shrine Gate: the keeper and a switchboard operator who prays there on her way home. Two people, 170 words together, exactly at the cap.

- **night-regulars** (night-regulars.morishita, night-regulars.mrs-ueda, night-regulars.isamu): Three block wardens (Row C Block 3, Row B Block 8, Row E) in three different haunts. Morishita and Mrs Ueda do nearly the same job in nearly the same way (counting people in at night with a torch): the one pair here whose characters resemble each other, so one may want sharpening or cutting, but that is a decision about two people, not a merge.

- **night-regulars** (night-regulars.imai, night-regulars.mira): A retired and a working telephone-exchange operator, in two different haunts (the hospital and the shrine). A pairing of two people across haunts, not one description.

- **night-regulars** (night-regulars.kazue, night-regulars.hachi, night-regulars.sigrun, night-regulars.tomoe, night-regulars.petr, night-regulars.ren, night-regulars.kaede, night-regulars.joji, night-regulars.nkiru, night-regulars.morishita, night-regulars.haru, night-regulars.grenna, night-regulars.hana, night-regulars.obi, night-regulars.mrs-ueda, night-regulars.sayo, night-regulars.faruq, night-regulars.isamu, night-regulars.tamsin, night-regulars.daisuke, night-regulars.mrs-handa): Seven more trios of three people met in one scene: Bathhouse (kazue, hachi, sigrun; 297 words), Crew Mess (tomoe, petr, ren; 230), Night Shift (kaede, joji, nkiru; 242), Early Night (morishita, haru, grenna; 271), Rumour Mill (hana, obi, mrs-ueda; 238), Roof (sayo, faruq, isamu; 230), Magic Show (tamsin, daisuke, mrs-handa; 265). Nothing joins each trio but the place, each has an NPC file per person, and every trio is over the 170-word cap.

- **nirvana** (nirvana.the-golden-key, nirvana.the-locks, nirvana.the-ancient-alien-god): The Auros zodiac key (to escape an extra-dimensional prison, unlocking the Gates of Dawn) sits in the same stretch of the old adventure as the locks and the boss, probably between them, but no fact says it is part of the way back, so I kept it apart; the tool also finds it shares almost no words with them.

- **nirvana** (nirvana.the-relic, nirvana.the-locks): The relic's errand (carry it to the fissure) and the locks on the way back are the same journey, but one is Sarenrae's charge and the other the obstacle course; kept as two items.

- **nirvana** (nirvana.what-it-is, nirvana.luminara): reviewed and left apart: A plane and the named city inside it are two places, each with its own description; a scene in Luminara need not retell the plane, and the old campaign notes describe them separately.

- **nirvana** (nirvana.the-relic, nirvana.djarvik): reviewed and left apart: The relic that seals the breach (and the way home to Gauntlight Keep) and the charge to Hyuck and Djarvik over their shared past are two separate matters that only share Sarenrae and the audience; they feed different later uses (the closing of the Kortos rift versus Hyuck's backstory).

- **nobu** (nobu.paying-a-debt, nobu.quartermaster): Both say he runs the budgets and is who the party argues with about repairs, but paying-a-debt is known by nobody (it carries the secret) and quartermaster by some.

- **nobu** (nobu.lab-assistant, nobu.kept-his-job): Wouldn't back Manako up and said nothing when it mattered are the same event told twice; left apart because lab-assistant is the one dated event (about 44 years ago) and kept-his-job (merged into nobu.the-debt in this proposal) describes the present, so merging would hang today's post on the date. Unsure.

- **nobu** (nobu.hides-well, nobu.guilty-conscience): How he hides the secret versus what the secret is; same known and told, left apart because one is an acting note and the other a fact about the man.

- **nobu** (nobu.opinions-on-gear, nobu.quartermaster): Same counter (Kit Issue) and same known and told; all three would run to about 150 words, and the gear opinions are a separate play note on how to be served.

- **nobu** (nobu.funds-the-column, nobu.paying-a-debt): The debt is what the funding pays; funding is told in chapter 9 and paying-a-debt is not scheduled.

- **noodle-stand-owner** (noodle-stand-owner.repeats-nothing, noodle-stand-owner.the-late-stand): She hears everything the off-duty staff say and repeats none of it; same known and told, but it is a rule for the GM (not a source, not a hook) rather than part of what the stand is, and would take the merged item to about 150 words. Unsure.

- **nozomi** (nozomi.the-show, nozomi.shown-the-watcher, nozomi.stayed): The same night told twice: the compact version the table hears in Chapter 2 and the full backstory told later, so the two are kept as two items.

- **nozomi** (nozomi.name-on-the-board, nozomi.locker): Both are about the state of her name (struck through on the Hangar Two board against still on the roster with her locker assigned), but told in Episode Two against later and name-on-the-board is a clue road in why-the-intake-came-early.

- **nozomi** (nozomi.a-fragment-only, nozomi.not-a-traitor, nozomi.the-ask): a-fragment-only is from the same tone section (what not to do) as the other two, but it is known by nobody and is a rule about what the party can overhear in the intake queue, not about how to play her.

- **nozomi** (nozomi.shown-the-watcher, nozomi.stayed, nozomi.not-a-traitor): not-a-traitor belongs with the same decision, but joining it would pass the 170-word cap.

- **party** (party.worst-jobs, party.marker-by-coincidence): Nothing in common beyond the section: worst-jobs is known by some, marker-by-coincidence by nobody, and they are about different things (the section's duties, the screening).

- **pip** (pip.the-volunteer, pip.peril): Both portray Pip as young and desperate to belong in a cockpit (brilliant and delighted by the machines; reckless and desperate to prove mitflits belong), and the jeopardy is the trap the volunteer walks into, but the-volunteer is known by some and peril by few. peril is also one of four peril-roster entries (pip, nao, shinobu, bobbin) written in the same shape.

- **prophecy** (prophecy.divination-fails, prophecy.conditions-not-right): Both hers and one subject (why divination gives no answer about the next rift), but the observation is known by some and its explanation by nobody, so they are two items by her own tagging.

- **rift** (rift.arrived-late, rift.flung-to-nirvana): The same return from Nirvana to a much larger rift, but arrived-late is her own working account (marked 'presumably') and the other is Claude's wording, so her item is left whole.

- **rika** (rika.insufficient-persistence, rika.believed-by-nobody): Same woman and the same thirty-eight years of investigating, but insufficient-persistence is known by nobody (it is the private sin) and the other two by few. It also pulls the other way: she almost believed Manako, asked one good question and let it drop, where the others have her refuse the explanation and investigate alone. Both can hold.

- **rupiah** (rupiah.star-singer, rupiah.asked-the-gods): star-singer (Claude: an elf noble, the Star Singer who asked the gods and got the prophecy) restates her asked-the-gods and her elf, and adds 'noble', the title Star Singer and that he got the prophecy; the first two are her words and star-singer is Claude's, so they differ in author (by) and cannot be joined.

- **rupiah** (rupiah.star-singer, rupiah.as-he-was): Both give the Star Singer title and his elf nature, but star-singer is the present man (known by few) and as-he-was the old campaign's sheet (known by nobody, dated 4721 AR).

- **rupiah** (rupiah.public-face, rupiah.shelters-the-kids): Both are the v4 notes on Rupiah now, but public-face is known by everyone and shelters-the-kids by few.

- **rupiah** (rupiah.star-singer, rupiah.shelters-the-kids): Both known by few and written by Claude, but the Star Singer (who he is, what he asked the gods) and the shelter he gives the young (how he behaves) are separate things that share no wording.

- **sap** (sap.catalogued-at-kortos, sap.last-sample): Consecutive parts of one story (the sample catalogued at Kortos, then handed to Manako, then walking out in his pocket), but dated about 120 years ago and about 44 years ago, which the rules treat as a conflict, not a merge.

- **sap** (sap.catalogued-at-kortos, sap.inert-12): Both are the shelved Inert 12 sample, known by few; catalogued-at-kortos is told later and dated, inert-12 is told in chapter 7.

- **sap** (sap.grows-the-watcher, sap.cost-of-seeing): Both are permanent costs of Sap use (the Watcher grows; the exposed person is easier to see), same known and told, but they are two different rules, not one thing.

- **sap** (sap.what-it-is, sap.gauge): Same known and told (nobody, chapter 9), and the gauge follows from knowing what Sap is, but it is a separate rule the party can use, so I left it as its own item.

- **sap** (sap.grows-the-watcher, sap.released-where-children-form): Both mention Cicada's demonstrations and Sap, but one is the Watcher growing and the other where its children can form: two rules.

- **school-civil-defence-instructor** (school-civil-defence-instructor.drills, school-civil-defence-instructor.refused-four-times): One woman and one old record, and the second follows from the first (nothing she teaches has ever been used; she has asked four times for a real exercise), but drills is known by some and refused-four-times by few.

- **school-civil-defence-instructor** (school-civil-defence-instructor.refused-four-times, school-civil-defence-instructor.pays-off): pays-off is what her four refusals lead to (the siren test in the exercise is the drill she asked for), but it is known by nobody and refused-four-times by few.

- **school-natural-history-teacher** (school-natural-history-teacher.true-answers, school-natural-history-teacher.the-specimen): The unclassifiable specimen is an example of her cheerful not-knowing, but it carries its own ruling (a plain plant, never explained in Episodes One or Two, not tied to Frame technology or First Impact), which Ingram should be able to accept or cut separately.

- **search-ship** (search-ship.still-calling, search-ship.digging-louder): Would merge cleanly (the call, and the dig makes it louder; same known and told), but still-calling is the answer of the question 'Who is doing it?' and digging-louder is one of its roads, so merging would make a road point at its own answer.

- **search-ship** (search-ship.crashed, search-ship.not-the-same-wreck): Both give the thousand years between the ships; crashed carries the anchored date (about 1000 years after generation-ship.crashed), and joining it would date the Directorate's dig that far back.

- **search-ship** (search-ship.second-excavation, search-ship.found-while-digging): The second excavation follows the hull found in the first dig, but one is known by some and told in chapter 8, the other known by few and told later.

- **second-rift** (second-rift.cause, second-rift.in-their-time, second-rift.starship): All three are hers, but second-rift.cause is the answer of the question what-it-is-building and stays atomic.

- **second-rift** (second-rift.cause, second-rift.doesnt-understand): doesnt-understand restates her cause (a weapon built from exotic matter without understanding what it can do) and adds what it could destroy; hers against Claude's.

- **second-rift** (second-rift.learns-the-worth, second-rift.can-build, second-rift.doesnt-understand): learns-the-worth is a step of the Second Impact clock in threads/the-watcher.md; the other two are standing conditions, so the clock step stays its own fact.

- **second-rift** (second-rift.learns-the-worth, second-rift.takes-the-matter): Consecutive steps of the same clock, but told later against Chapter 13.

- **second-rift** (second-rift.the-violet-knot, second-rift.opens): Consecutive steps of the same clock and question road, but known by few and told in Chapter 14 against known by everyone and told in Chapter 15.

- **second-rift** (second-rift.gap-is-after, second-rift.in-their-time): Both hers and both known by nobody, but the Gap is a different event (after the adventure) from the second rift.

- **section-officer** (section-officer.has-it-in-for-them, section-officer.back-and-forth): Two halves of the same feud (he keeps ramping up the duties because he has it in for them, so every assignment is a repeated chance to score a point back), same known and told; left apart because one is his private motive and the other a rule for how the exchange runs (always full-party work), which Ingram may want to accept or cut separately. The checker also finds the wording unrelated. Unsure.

- **section-officer** (section-officer.middling, section-officer.not-dangerous): A claim and its consequence (total authority over the small and none over anything that matters, so the worst he can do is a tedious fortnight), same known and told; left apart because not-dangerous is a guardrail that clashes with the Episode One drains fight (see conflicts), and should not be hidden inside a description of who he is. Unsure.

- **section-officer** (section-officer.not-dangerous, section-officer.not-cruel): Both say he cannot or will not really hurt them (never cruelty / never harms them), but not-dangerous is known by some and not-cruel by nobody, so the table meets them as different facts.

- **section-officer** (section-officer.ongoing-problem, section-officer.has-it-in-for-them): Same hazing arc (he stays the party's contact and keeps escalating), but ongoing-problem is known by some and has-it-in-for-them by few.

- **section-officer** (section-officer.takes-the-credit, section-officer.not-dangerous): Both end on a fortnight of legitimate, miserable work after the errand, but takes-the-credit is told in chapter 2 and not-dangerous later.

- **section-officer** (section-officer.never-wrong, section-officer.voice): The same trait seen from two sides (never wrong in his own account; the passive-voice-for-failure, first-person-for-success habit that shows it); same known and told, left apart because one is what he does and the other how he sounds. Unsure.

- **section-officer** (section-officer.promoted-out, section-officer.unredeemed): Both end with him not removed, corrected or redeemed, but promoted-out is an event (the win in front of someone senior) and unredeemed is a rule about tone.

- **section-officer** (section-officer.has-done-every-job, section-officer.back-and-forth): A correct written appeal is the 'use his own procedure against him' lever, but has-done-every-job is a play note on how to deal with him and the other is the arc. Unsure.

- **section-officer** (section-officer.out-of-his-depth, section-officer.watched-from-above): Both are about Vetch being out of his depth in front of the real thing, but one is a late quiet scene and the other a standing condition (somebody senior has noticed his section).

- **shinobu** (shinobu.the-splash, shinobu.nanites): nanites restates the splash (splashed point-blank with Sap, carries a colony), but the-splash is known by few and nanites by nobody.

- **shinobu** (shinobu.the-cell, shinobu.watched-in-her-cell): Both say the Watcher finds its way into her glass cell deep underground to watch her; the-cell is known by few and watched-in-her-cell by nobody.

- **shinobu** (shinobu.willing-to-talk, shinobu.most-witnessed): Both say she is entirely willing to talk; willing-to-talk is known by few, most-witnessed by nobody, so the sentence stays in two merged items.

- **shinobu** (shinobu.peril, shinobu.the-colony): The jeopardy is the colony being mishandled; same known and told. Left apart because peril is a roster entry about how she can be lost and saved, the same shape as pip.peril, nao.peril and bobbin.peril. Unsure.

- **shinobu** (shinobu.detained, shinobu.the-splash): Her detention follows from the splash, but the-splash is known by few and detained by some.

- **shucks** (shucks.skeleton, shucks.processed-grandparents): Both say his two hundred years are literal and unremarkable to him, and the checker passes them as one 105-word item, but held back as doubtful: one is what he looks like, the other an anecdote he tells at Intake Medicals, so a scene could tell one without the other. Optional merge, ready in merge-optional/shucks.json.

- **shucks** (shucks.shuck-mord, shucks.rand-old-campaign): Both are 'in the old campaign' notes (Shucks Mord; Shuck's Right Hand), pass the checker, and are known by nobody; held back as doubtful because they are about two characters (he, and his hand) and the hand has its own cluster of facts. Optional merge, ready in merge-optional/shucks.json.

- **shucks** (shucks.skeleton, shucks.as-he-was, shucks.shuck-mord): Three versions of the same fact (a tomb-born skeleton rogue who became a doctor and is really two hundred years old) with three different known levels: some, few and nobody. If Ingram aligns their known, they merge into one.

- **shucks** (shucks.rand-old-campaign, shucks.the-hand-then): The old-campaign hand (a scout and an agent of chaos, a character of its own) said twice, known by nobody and by few, and the second carries a date (4721 AR) while the first carries none.

- **shucks** (shucks.tests-the-blood, shucks.found-the-marker, shucks.screens-every-candidate, shucks.surprised-by-six): All five facts from Shucks's testing programme are one story (a theory about the blood, the original Gen 2 trial pilots, the screen run on every candidate, six carriers in one intake), but tests-the-blood is Ingram's own words (by ingram, known by few) and the others are Claude's, with known split between nobody and few; the checker keeps them apart. found-the-marker (nobody) would join the screen-and-silence item if its known were few.

- **shucks** (shucks.supervised-the-disgrace, shucks.on-the-list): The same claim (Shucks supervised Manako's disgrace and so is on his revenge list) told twice, but one is told later and carries a date (about 44 years ago) and the other is told in chapter 12.

- **shucks** (shucks.voice, shucks.forbidden-to-explain): voice also says he won't explain what the blood flag means and thinks the rule stupid, but voice is known by some and the forbidden-to-explain facts by few.

- **shucks** (shucks.why-he-works, shucks.became-the-machinery): Both say what Shucks is for in the story (the person inside a two-hundred-year-old institution; the man who never left and has become the machinery), but known by nobody and by few.

- **shucks** (shucks.as-he-was, shucks.at-first-impact): Both are about Shucks two hundred years ago, but one is dated 4721 AR and the other about 200 years ago (the same date written two ways, which the checker treats as different).

- **shucks** (shucks.hand-detached, shucks.the-hand-then): Both say the hand is its own creature, but hand-detached says only 'some while ago' (a beam of necromantic energy) and the-hand-then dates it to 4721 AR; a merged item would silently date the beam incident, which is what the second conflict below is about.

- **shucks** (shucks.tells-the-deputy, shucks.not-a-guardian-angel): The scene (Shucks walks into the Deputy Director's office unannounced) and the rule that it must not make him a protector go together, but one is plot and one a note on how to play it; kept as two.

- **shucks** (shucks.rand-not-a-rescuer, shucks.rand-the-override): The tone rule (warm comedy, never a rescuer or patron, its help costs them) and the act it governs (the override put them on the machines) are cause and consequence, but a note on tone and an event.

- **stray** (stray.where-it-comes-from, stray.what-it-is): Where it comes from (a crumb that follows one of them home after the Chapter 5 fight and waits to be fed) is the first meeting; known by few and told in Chapter 5, against nobody and later.

- **stray** (stray.raised, stray.where-it-comes-from): Both are Chapter 5 beats (the stray appears; feed it or not), but raised is known by nobody and told later, and where-it-comes-from by few and in Chapter 5.

- **stray** (stray.works, stray.visible): Two open decisions on the stray (whether it works at the table; whether it can be seen); open facts are never merged, but both turn on its first appearance in play.

- **taka** (taka.a-year-ahead, taka.what-he-knows): The six o'clock morning scene is the same moment as the drill briefing, but a-year-ahead is told in the Zero Six Hundred scene and the others later, so the table meets them at different times.

- **taka** (taka.a-year-ahead, taka.flies-gen-1): a-year-ahead already says he is a year ahead and flies a Gen 1, which flies-gen-1 and peer repeat; blocked by the told difference (the Zero Six Hundred scene against later).

- **taka** (taka.a-year-ahead, taka.claims-the-table): Both have him at the canteen (a-year-ahead: he sits with them); blocked by the told difference.

- **taka** (taka.claims-the-table, taka.peer): peer reads the instant claiming as a peer's bonding move, so the two lean on each other, but claiming is a scene beat (and is in the ink canteen scene) while peer is a standing fact about who he is and belongs beside the open question of his age and rank. Unsure.

- **taka** (taka.peer, taka.what-he-knows): A peer a year further in, who knows it in his hands: close, but peer is a guess about his status that the open question taka.particulars may overturn, so it is kept separate from what he knows. Unsure.

- **taka** (taka.warm-because-free, taka.hydraulic-compensation): Both are rules for playing him (warmth costs him nothing; competence surprises and the goofiness is played straight) from the same tone section of the old file; left apart as two separate rules, same known and told. Unsure.

- **the-administrative-block** (the-administrative-block.the-ladder, the-administrative-block.decided-upstairs): Same building and the same top floor, but the-ladder is known by some and decided-upstairs (decisions arriving downstairs as an unexplained duty change, read on the muster yard's board) by few.

- **the-administrative-block** (the-administrative-block.the-ladder, the-administrative-block.the-establishment): Same metadata, but the Establishment Offices are a different building (five storeys beside the Block, subordinate to it) with their own subject, the list of who exists to the Directorate; left apart.

- **the-drain-head** (the-drain-head.the-cover, the-drain-head.not-the-old-town): Same place, but not-the-old-town (it is the island's own drainage, a separate problem from the drains under the old town; no civilian knows it is there) is known by few and the-cover by some.

- **the-fixer** (the-fixer.logistics, the-fixer.keeps-it-working): Both say he is Cicada's logistics man (venues, crates, permits, timing), but logistics is known by some and told in Chapter 4, keeps-it-working by nobody and later.

- **the-fixer** (the-fixer.logistics, the-fixer.how-he-fights, the-fixer.voice): logistics repeats the hook, chain and crowbar (how-he-fights) and the line 'It's already been signed for' (voice); known by some and told in Chapter 4 against nobody and later.

- **the-fixer** (the-fixer.books, the-fixer.audit): The books are what the audit finds (the money runs through the crime lords and back to Nobu), both in Chapter 9, but books is known by few and audit by nobody.

- **the-fixer** (the-fixer.books, the-fixer.embezzles): Both are about where Cicada's money goes through the Fixer's hands (to the crime lords and on to Nobu; to the families of his dead crew), but few and Chapter 9 against nobody and later.

- **the-fixer** (the-fixer.how-he-fights, the-fixer.exits): reviewed and left apart: How he fights and how he leaves are separate design slots (gimmick and escape) that only share his knowledge of the ground: neither piece depends on the other, the fight is not where 'He left an hour ago' happens, and the checker found no shared content.

- **the-muster-yard** (the-muster-yard.the-board, the-muster-yard.the-hatch): Same metadata and the clerk keeps the board, but the hatch is a person to handle ('pressure doesn't work, paperwork does') and she may want to ink it separately from the board; left apart.

- **the-outer-sea-wall** (the-outer-sea-wall.the-tide-decides, the-outer-sea-wall.the-weed-line): Same metadata and the weed line is the high-water mark that explains the tide table, but the weed line is a separate physical image (and the tide-decides figure is in question, see the conflict), so left apart.

- **the-outer-sea-wall** (the-outer-sea-wall.the-tide-decides, the-outer-sea-wall.nobody-comes): Would be a claim and its consequence (the walk is open only at low water, so nobody sees who is there), but their hours do not agree, see the conflict.

- **the-plot-room** (the-plot-room.what-it-is, the-plot-room.plotters-talk): The plotters (who hear everything and are junior enough that nobody manages what they say off duty) are known by few, the room by some.

- **the-registry** (the-registry.what-it-is, the-registry.typed-per-clearance): Same place, but what-it-is (forty typewriters, the print room, nothing handed over without a signature) is known by some, and typed-per-clearance by few.

- **the-rock** (the-rock.on-the-surface, the-rock.nine-doors): Both describe The Rock seen from above ground, but the surface (a carpark and bare reinforced ground) is known by some and the nine doors and what lies under them by few.

- **the-sceptics** (the-sceptics.who, the-sceptics.unconnected): Both are Chapter 11 (the sceptics who investigated privately; they have no name or organisation and the party assembles them), but who is known by few and unconnected by nobody.

- **the-sceptics** (the-sceptics.cranks, the-sceptics.price): Both are about the sceptics' standing with the Directorate (treated as cranks; allying costs credibility), both told later, but known by some and by nobody.

- **the-soldier** (the-soldier.the-squad, the-soldier.never-kills, the-soldier.wont-fire-on-children, the-soldier.withdraws-by-the-book): the-squad is a one-line summary of the code (non-lethal, won't fire on anyone young, withdraws by the book, won't finish anyone off) that the other three give in detail, but it is known by some and the others by nobody, and never-kills is told in Chapter 3 against 'later'.

- **the-soldier** (the-soldier.knows-section-nine, the-soldier.how-he-knows): A claim and its explanation (they call the party 'Section Nine'; the Young One told them), but known by few and told in Chapter 3 against known by nobody and told later: the table is meant to notice it long before it learns why.

- **the-soldier** (the-soldier.wants-to-be-caught, the-soldier.serving-as-penance): Both say he wants to be caught (serving-as-penance is now inside the merged what-he-wants), but wants-to-be-caught is told in Chapter 11 (he arranges his arrest) and the other later.

- **the-soldier** (the-soldier.his-side, the-soldier.on-the-cordon): his-side is the account he gives in custody in Chapter 11 (known by few), the cordon facts are what actually happened (known by nobody); the same day told from two sides, so they stay two facts.

- **the-soldier** (the-soldier.wants-to-be-caught, the-soldier.his-side): He arranges his arrest, then tells his side in custody: both Chapter 11, but nobody versus few; and the arrest is a plan beat (the-soldiers-arrest) as well.

- **the-soldier** (the-soldier.wont-fire-on-children, the-soldier.never-kills): His counter (he will not fire on children) and the behaviour it produces (every Strike is nonlethal); both nobody, but later versus Chapter 3.

- **the-soldier** (the-soldier.voice, the-soldier.knows-section-nine): The voice piece repeats that he calls them 'Section Nine' and nobody should explain; known by nobody and told later versus known by few and told in Chapter 3.

- **the-soldier** (the-soldier.keeps-the-count-at-zero, the-soldier.serving-as-penance): reviewed and left apart: Two parallel motives, the stated want (a zero count) and the hidden ulterior motive (penance, wanting to be caught), that neither piece builds on; 'the penance behind the doctrine' is the drafter's link, not the pieces'.

- **the-telephone-exchange** (the-telephone-exchange.every-call, the-telephone-exchange.guarded): Same building, but guarded (unremarkable outside, guarded rather more carefully than it looks) is known by few and every-call by some.

- **the-works** (the-works.the-strip, the-works.erecting-shop): The strip ends 'every shop on the strip exists to feed the Erecting Shop', but the strip is known by everyone and the shop by some.

- **the-works** (the-works.erecting-shop, the-works.the-canteen): Same metadata, but the canteen (seventy metres of tables in three sittings, where rank is a suggestion and the fitters are right) is a different building and a different point; left apart.

- **the-young-one** (the-young-one.the-killing, the-young-one.doctrine-afterwards): The killing in a heist and the doctrine built backwards afterwards are the same event told twice, but the-killing is known by few and told in Chapter 10, doctrine-afterwards by nobody and later.

- **the-young-one** (the-young-one.masked-fighter, the-young-one.the-killing, the-young-one.unmasked): The masked fighter's three appearances (the gang weapon in Chapter 4; the killing in Chapter 10; unmasked in Chapter 12) are one arc, but known/told differ at every step (some and Chapter 4; few and Chapter 10; few and Chapter 12).

- **the-young-one** (the-young-one.cicadas-source, the-young-one.intelligence): intelligence says what Cicada gets from the source (movements, rosters, deployments), but cicadas-source is told in Chapter 12 and intelligence later.

- **the-young-one** (the-young-one.went-to-a-show, the-young-one.why-he-stayed): why-he-stayed continues the story (he stayed because he was useful, told himself he could do more from inside, and was frightened), but went-to-a-show is told in Chapter 12 and why-he-stayed later.

- **the-young-one** (the-young-one.why-he-stayed, the-young-one.wants-permission): Both are his inner life (never worked out why he stayed; wants someone to tell him it was all right), but two different claims, left apart (unsure).

- **the-young-one** (the-young-one.misquotes, the-young-one.voice): Both say he quotes Manako slightly wrong, one as a road to Cicada's author and one as his voice, but told in Chapter 12 versus later.

- **the-young-one** (the-young-one.how-he-fights, the-young-one.has-a-frame): has-a-frame is a card held in reserve ('if the campaign wants it'), not a settled part of how he fights, so I kept it apart (unsure).

- **the-young-one** (the-young-one.competitive, the-young-one.canteen): competitive repeats the canteen line (eats in the party's canteen, has an opinion about their assignment), but is known by nobody while canteen is known by some.

- **the-young-one** (the-young-one.how-he-fights, the-young-one.alone): reviewed and left apart: How he fights and the no-escape/counter are separate design slots with no dependence between the pieces (the checker found no shared content); merging mixes what he does with how he is beaten.

- **the-young-one** (the-young-one.doctrine-afterwards, the-young-one.competitive): reviewed and left apart: The 'competitive' piece is a hybrid: its character-and-canteen lines belong with how the party meets him, and only its thesis sentence repeats the doctrine piece, so merging ties his Episode One demeanour to the Chapter 10 killing (it is also marked known: nobody though its canteen line is known: some elsewhere).

- **transparency-advocate** (transparency-advocate.radicalised, transparency-advocate.satoru): Why Satoru believes it (years of failing to get Shinobu released) is known by nobody; his position itself is known by some.

- **transparency-advocate** (transparency-advocate.refused-three-times, transparency-advocate.arms-them): The three refused requests for the deep-storage inventory are known by few, his habit of telling trainees their rights by some.

- **vermilion-block** (vermilion-block.the-silos, vermilion-block.silos-came-later): Silos-came-later (the silos came after the building; long-serving staff remember when there were only two; the Block was built when the magic users outgrew an informal arrangement) is known by some, the silos by few, and half of it is the Block's own history.

- **vermilion-block** (vermilion-block.what-it-is, vermilion-block.how-it-looks): Same building, but how-it-looks (black walls, red trim, narrow windows, a red diamond on the roof) is known by everyone, what-it-is by some.

- **vermilion-block** (vermilion-block.what-it-is, vermilion-block.just-a-department): just-a-department restates that the Block is a plain Directorate department, and adds a tone note (its mystery is what a department is stockpiling), but it is known by nobody and the others by some.

- **vermilion-block** (vermilion-block.silos-came-later, vermilion-block.what-it-is): Silos-came-later says the Block itself was built when the magic users outgrew an informal arrangement, which belongs with what-it-is; the same metadata, but the rest of it is about the silos, so left apart.

- **watcher** (watcher.the-mirror, watcher.mirror-lean): Both say how the party fights teaches the Watcher its method and never its purpose, but the-mirror is told in chapter 11 and mirror-lean is not scheduled, so the rules keep them apart.

- **watcher** (watcher.reaches-down, watcher.in-the-ground): Both put the Watcher in the rock around the buried ship; reaches-down is told later, in-the-ground in chapter 8.

- **watcher** (watcher.what-it-is, watcher.built-for-the-void, watcher.no-organising-layer): what-it-is (the ship's eyes without its mind, torn out and broken into a swarm) is the summary of the detail in the other two, but it is told in chapter 11 and the others later.

- **watcher** (watcher.drawn-by-the-call, watcher.drifted-to-kurose): Both say the Watcher came to the buried ship's call; drawn-by-the-call is told in chapter 11 with no date, drifted-to-kurose is dated about 200 to 150 years ago and told later.

- **watcher** (watcher.g3, watcher.recognises-gen-2): g3's last sentence (the Seekers prefer Gen 2 because it is built from their ship's technology) restates recognises-gen-2; known some versus nobody, told chapter 5 versus later.

- **watcher** (watcher.the-runaway, watcher.g8): Both are the children making Sap (the runaway); the-runaway is known by some and g8 by nobody, both told in chapter 14.

- **watcher** (watcher.loud-places, watcher.feeds-on-alarm): Both are the Watcher going for noise (loud places; it feeds on sirens); loud-places is told later, feeds-on-alarm in chapter 10.

- **watcher** (watcher.nothing-dies, watcher.drains-to-the-dig): What the table sees in chapter 2 (a beaten child comes apart and drains away, known by some) and the hidden fact that it drains to the dig (known by nobody, told later) are one event with different known and told; I put drains-to-the-dig with the children instead.

- **watcher** (watcher.mass-clock, watcher.hidden-numbers, watcher.mirror-lean): The three hidden dials of the Observer dossier (the Mass Clock, the Mirror's lean, the lens modifier) read as one thing, but mass-clock says to show the clock to the party and hidden-numbers says its position is never confirmed to them, so I left all three apart (see conflicts).

- **watcher** (watcher.children-are-condensations, watcher.what-children-are-for): what-children-are-for (an eye first, then defenders, claim-stakers, makers) is the same children described by role; adding it would make a 135-word item, so I kept it apart. Unsure: easy to add if you prefer one 'children' item.

- **watcher** (watcher.learns, watcher.mirror-lean): Two views of the adversary loop (it learns from the party; how they fight leans which Watcher they train), but they are separate rules and learns is the general claim the whole dossier hangs on, so I did not call them one thing.

- **watcher** (watcher.exists-to-observe, watcher.played-by-claude): played-by-claude (Ingram's words) restates the objective in wants and exists-to-observe (multiply, expand, continue observing); her words and Claude's are never mixed, so they stay apart.

- **watcher** (watcher.built-for-the-void, watcher.no-organising-layer): reviewed and left apart: What the navigation and sensor system was built to do and the state of the swarm after First Impact are two different stages, not an elaboration of one another (the source keeps them in separate sections, 'What it was' and 'What happened to it'); the first is also what Mother's fragment remembers, the second what the party sees of the swarm.

- **watcher** (watcher.cannot-read-paper, watcher.files-on-the-six): reviewed and left apart: A limit on what the Watcher can read (which Rand's paper trail depends on) and its noticing the six people it can't explain are separate claims from different chapters of the plan; opening files on the six is not a consequence of being unable to read paper.

## Conflicts

- **anabala** (anabala.wakes-the-ship, anabala.way-home): wakes-the-ship (pencil) has Anabala wake the search ship in Chapter 14 to take her people home, so that it lifts in Chapter 15; the open decision anabala.way-home still asks whether her way home is the search ship under Kurose or the shuttle under Kortos.

- **anabala** (anabala.recruited-the-four, mitflits.gauntlight): recruited-the-four has Anabala, already one of the four, bring Hyuck, Shucks and Rupiah to intervene against Baldwin; mitflits.gauntlight has the four founders befriending a band of mitflits at Gauntlight Keep, with Anabala put in charge of their government afterwards. The two tellings of how the four and the mitflits came together do not fit.

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.volkheed, baldwin-manor.mitflit-clan): volkheed says Inicrium mining began at Volkheed around 4171 AR; mitflit-clan has a clan arriving in 4147 AR and lending its skills to the Inicrium trade, twenty-four years before the mining began (unless the 'trade' is older than the mining).

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.croglang): This one fact contradicts itself, as it admits: the old history has the castle built in 4221 AR and renamed in 4201 AR, twenty years before it was built.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.alive, anabala.knows-baldwin-lives): baldwin.alive ends 'Nobody knows' (known: nobody), which settles in advance what the open anabala.knows-baldwin-lives still asks (candidates: she knows, or she doesn't); baldwin.nobu-knows is the same question for Nobu and the Fixer.

- **cicada** (cicada.not-the-first-fight, the-soldier.the-squad): not-the-first-fight (told Chapter 9) calls the column raid 'the first real Cadre fight', while the-soldier.the-squad and the-soldier.never-kills (told Chapter 3) and the plan (beat--meet-the-cadre) have the party meet and fight an armed Cadre squad in Chapter 3; they agree only if 'real' means a fight where the Cadre fights to kill, as column.cadre-defends says of Chapter 9.

- **cicada** (cicada.witness-is-the-goal, cicada.nobody-dies): witness-is-the-goal says some of what Cicada does is 'now and then fatal in its consequences', while nobody-dies (and the-soldier.keeps-the-count-at-zero) say nobody has died at a demonstration; they agree only if 'what they do' means more than the demonstrations (the Cadre's work, the heists).

- **cicada** (cicada.no-leader-in-the-file, cicada.dangerous-cultists): no-leader-in-the-file says nobody in the Directorate is assigned to Cicada and its file holds only private showings, a mark or two and the odd arrest for a public nuisance, while dangerous-cultists (and the mayor's joint-raid arrangement, and the armed Cadre squad the party meets in Chapter 3) have the Directorate raiding 'dangerous cultists' beside the police; they fit only if the raids are never tied to a file.

- **comms-officer** (comms-officer.afraid-for-them, hulda-brack.no-secrets): afraid-for-them calls her the first adult in the campaign whose instinct is to protect the party; hulda-brack.no-secrets (Brack uses the machinery for them) and directorate.decent-adults (Shucks, Nobu and Taka are warm and on the children's side) already put kind adults in Episode One. Whether 'first' means the first to put herself between them and danger is for Ingram. A soft tension, not a flat contradiction.

- **directorate** (directorate.two-ends, directorate.first-look-mix): Both are Ingram's words. two-ends says the school and the base are one island with two ends, 'not one blended site', with the arrival end (causeway, Roadgate, hangar, checkpoints) pure military; first-look-mix says that on first arrival the campus reads as a genuine mix of military and school with neither dominating. Possibly reconcilable (a first look from the causeway taking in both ends), but each seems to deny the other until she says how.

- **directorate** (directorate.reads-the-messages, directorate.consent-in-the-forms): reads-the-messages puts the consent in a clause of FORM 1-B, the Service Agreement signed at induction; consent-in-the-forms puts it in the intake paperwork, 'the same forms' that ask for mother's maiden name twice, which directorate.form-12b (decided) says is Form 12-B, the intake screening form. Either they are two different forms or one fact has two homes.

- **envoy** (envoy.first-hello, envoy.swarm-felt-nothing, hori.peril): first-hello dates the Envoy 'about 15 years ago | about 1 year ago' (the open decision cicada.envoy-when), while swarm-felt-nothing says about 15 years and hori.peril says 'fifteen years of guilt'.

- **era** (era.dug-up-not-invented, era.tech-trickle, era.electronics-source): dug-up-not-invented says the gate is hard yet ship technology 'still leaks outwards from Kortos' and the phones run on the ship-derived substrate, which pre-answers the open decisions era.tech-trickle (does it reach civilian life at all?) and era.electronics-source (the generation ship's leak, or the search ship?).

- **founders** (founders.pictures-are-the-lie, founders.page-forty, founders.the-tidying): pictures-are-the-lie calls the founders' official pictures 'the lie'; page-forty says the approved history is 'not a lie anybody is currently telling' and the-tidying says nobody is keeping the faces secret, only a two-century tidying. Whether the official portrayal is a lie or an unexamined tidying is her call.

- **founders** (founders.what-they-were, founders.as-they-were): Pencil against ink (the ink one would win): founders.what-they-were calls Shucks 'a man who is now a skeleton', which reads as a man who later became one, while as-they-were has him 'a skeleton rogue' in the old campaign's adventuring days, as do shucks.shuck-mord ('two hundred years ago Shucks was a skeleton rogue, tomb-born') and D043's memorial plate ('a skeleton'). Worth her correcting the ink wording if it is a slip.

- **general-hospital** (general-hospital.who-oversees-it, directorate-posts.hospital-chief-of-staff): who-oversees-it is an open question (Claude proposed Shucks Mordant; Ingram has not confirmed it; nobody in particular or someone else are live), but directorate-posts.hospital-chief-of-staff states as fact that the hospital nominally answers to Shucks Mordant as the Directorate's Chief Medical Officer, who is never there in person.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.statue-as-described, hyuck.statue-material): statue-pose says 'if the stone were flesh', which presumes stone, while the open decision hyuck.statue-material still asks whether the statue is stone or bronze.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--the-watchers-early-voice, idea--the-voice-changes): Both give the Watcher's later voice, but this one is a legalistic claim-filer who says 'Survive all of it, and I'm done', while the other (and the Voice drafted in world/watcher.md) is a calm observer, and the fact watcher.stays has it remain over Kurose whatever the party survives.

- **misc-clues** (idea--censored-papers, the-registry.typed-per-clearance): The idea has each document censored to a clearance, with the censor's cuts telling the reader something; the fact has every clearance level typed separately, 'never censored afterwards', so there are no cuts to read.

- **misc-clues** (idea--censored-papers, idea--the-charters-black-bar): Ingram cut a publicly censored paragraph of the charter as 'a secret that is flaunted'; censoring every Directorate paper in view is the same flaunting made standard.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--nobody-has-to-die, idea--one-death-precisely-placed): The first says every death in the campaign is one a human caused, with no corpses on the enemy side; the second has an early Claimant kill a man by accident.

- **the-mayor** (idea--mayor-first-appearance, mayor.own-agent): The idea has the party learn which of the city and the mayor's own position the mayor protects first; Ingram's fact has the mayor protect both equally.

- **manako** (manako.disgraced, manako.disgraced-after-the-spill): Ink says he was expelled after a 'breakdown' at a science conference; the pencil spill piece says he lost his career 44 years ago after the lab spill (shucks.supervised-the-disgrace agrees), six years before the conference: which event is the disgrace?

- **manako** (manako.disgraced-after-the-spill, manako.the-papers-are-his, manako.checks-first): The discredited papers are a forty-year-old study 'from before his disgrace', and checks-first has him develop their technique after the failed demonstration, but the disgrace is placed 44 years ago, before all of that.

- **mitflit-home-world** (mitflit-home-world.why-ships-fall, mitflit-home-world.who-fells-ships): why-ships-fall has the derelicts' logs in Chapter 17 explain why ships fall; one candidate of the open who-fells-ships is that it is never explained in the campaign.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.wild, baldwin.the-hunters): never-vermin says mitflits have never been hunted at any point in this world's history (her confirmation, D014: 'never hunted, never bountied'); the Baldwin items have his hunters capturing mitflit savants for their minds, and an army of hunters set against the mitflits at Gauntlight, which by the word is hunting them.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.wild, kurose.small-signs): never-vermin says mitflits are never treated as vermin; kurose.small-signs has a hand-lettered notice asking anyone who spots a mitflit to report it to the Harbour Office and not handle it themselves, which reads as a pest notice.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.wild, mitflits.rare-at-first): wild has the wild mitflits living around the city and the hangars; rare-at-first has mitflits extremely rare in Kurose at the start of the campaign. How many are about, and since when, is not the same in the two.

- **mitflits** (mitflits.gauntlight, mitflits.gauntlight-council): gauntlight has the four helping the mitflits build a government 'with Anabala in charge'; gauntlight-council says the council was led by a triumvirate of Comhphas, Scrawng and Anabala.

- **mother** (mother.old-campaign, mother.met-by-the-four, generation-ship.mitflit-village): old-campaign puts the mitflit village inside the jungle ship; met-by-the-four and generation-ship.mitflit-village put it beside the generation ship (while generation-ship.inside has two mitflit quarters within the ship).

- **nirvana** (nirvana.the-relic, rift.sarenrae-sent): the-relic says Sarenrae gave the four a relic to seal the breach between planes; rift.sarenrae-sent (open) says what exactly she sent them back with to close Kortos is not decided.

- **nobu** (nobu.looks-away, nobu.hides-well): looks-away says he reads as a man compensating for something and will not meet your eye when generous; hides-well says he is never nervous, evasive or cagey and nothing in his manner gives his secret away. Both hold only if the compensating is a different tell from the secret. (looks-away is merged into nobu.quartermaster in this wave.)

- **noodle-stand-owner** (noodle-stand-owner.proof-of-an-outside, rest--the-paper-lantern): proof-of-an-outside makes her the campaign's only civilian fixture the Directorate does not own, a place where nobody is anybody's section; the haunt scene The Paper Lantern (sited by Ingram on a real building of The Slope) is a civilian izakaya off Directorate ground where nobody's rank matters, run by Kiku Enomoto, who runs the place exactly her own way.

- **rift** (rift.the-fumble, founders.the-plea): the-fumble says a planar breach flung the four across the planes to Nirvana; founders.the-plea says Comhphas's plea reached them as the breach opened and they went through a counterpart portal and came out in Nirvana: flung by the breach, or went through on purpose?

- **rupiah** (rupiah.elf, founders.what-they-were): rupiah.elf has it known by everyone that Rupiah is an elf; founders.what-they-were (decided) has the real four, the elf included, known by few, and founders.pictures has every approved picture show four tall humans, which an elf who cuts the ribbon in person each Founding Day would give away.

- **sap** (sap.last-sample, sap.inert-12, lens.three-ways): last-sample says the last remaining sample walked out in Manako's pocket about 44 years ago and nobody reported it; inert-12 and lens.three-ways have a shelved sample still in the Directorate's catalogue for the party to find in chapter 7.

- **sap** (sap.last-sample, manako.first-supplier): last-sample says Manako reverse-engineered everything from the one sample he took; manako.first-supplier says a fragment of the Watcher stayed near him and helped him work out how to make more Sap (they may both be true).

- **sap** (sap.inert-12, sap.catalogued-at-kortos, section-officer.the-transmission-chain): Three ages for one sample: inert-12 says it is about a century old; catalogued-at-kortos dates the first trace about 120 years ago; section-officer.the-transmission-chain has Manako handed a century-old sample about forty years ago, which would make it about 140 now (probably only rounding).

- **school-history-teacher** (school-history-teacher.taught-straight, school-history-teacher.two-teachers): taught-straight says a student comes out of his class having learned something true about how the island got here and leaves it a GM call whether he knows more; two-teachers (known by nobody, so also not mergeable) says the history syllabus is an approved text that has been edited without his knowing, so what he teaches is not simply true and he does not know more.

- **school-natural-history-teacher** (school-natural-history-teacher.true-answers, school-history-teacher.says-i-dont-know): true-answers makes her the only teacher in the building who will happily tell a class she doesn't know; school-history-teacher.says-i-dont-know has the history teacher say I don't know plainly with no embarrassment, as does the Episode One first-period scene (Ota 'says I don't know plainly and doesn't mind').

- **section-officer** (section-officer.not-dangerous, section-officer.the-drains): not-dangerous says Vetch cannot get the party hurt and the worst he can do is a tedious fortnight; the Episode One scenes have him order them down the storm drains on the first night, and Flood Tunnel Clearance makes that the first real fight (six giant centipedes).

- **section-officer** (section-officer.unredeemed, section-officer.in-a-frame): unredeemed has Vetch still at his post at the end of the campaign, doing the same to the next intake; the open question in-a-frame lists 'he disappears from the story after the promotion' as a live candidate, so settling it that way would undo unredeemed unless he stays offstage.

- **shinobu** (shinobu.detained, transparency-advocate.radicalised): detained says months of nothing followed and released says she was freed under her father's pressure (kudo.the-speech has four months in a cell); transparency-advocate.radicalised says Satoru spent years trying to get Shinobu released and failing.

- **shinobu** (shinobu.the-colony, shinobu.the-cell): the-colony says part of the Watcher lives in her, so she is held in its attention more or less continuously and not glimpsed occasionally; the-cell and watched-in-her-cell say the Watcher finds its way into her cell and call what it does visits. A tension, not a flat contradiction: both hold if the main swarm visits and the colony is always there.
