- **shucks** (shucks.knows-the-marker, shucks.found-the-marker): knows-the-marker says Shucks has known what the marker means since the party's intake; found-the-marker says he found the marker years ago, going back through the notes of the original Gen 2 trial pilots when the programme nearly folded.

- **shucks** (shucks.the-hand-then, shucks.rand-the-earlier-man, shucks.hand-detached, shucks.old-handwriting): When the hand became its own creature: the-hand-then dates it to 4721 AR ('already a creature of its own in the old days') and rand-the-earlier-man says it detached 'before any of this'; hand-detached says only 'some while ago', after Shucks put it in a necromantic beam; and the ink fact shucks.old-handwriting has Rand writing in Shucks's handwriting from before the hand came off, so that anything the founders signed two hundred years ago (the charter) shows it. If it was already loose in 4721 AR, it is unclear how the charter (presumably signed at the Directorate's founding, about five years after First Impact) carries its handwriting.

- **shucks** (shucks.on-the-list, shucks.supervised-the-disgrace, shucks.knows-hes-a-target): Pencil against an open decision: on-the-list and supervised-the-disgrace state that Shucks supervised Manako's disgrace personally and may feel his conscience salved; the open decision knows-hes-a-target says it is not decided whether he remembers Manako at all or what his role actually was (a direct decision, or a signature on someone else's).

- **taka** (taka.a-year-ahead, episode-c1e1--canteen): a-year-ahead says Taka sends them off to the drains; in the Episode One canteen scene Vetch gives Section Nine the drains and Taka only gets the last word (wear the waders).

- **taka** (taka.old-drill-briefing, episode-c1e2--zero-six-hundred): old-drill-briefing has Taka brief the Frame drill the next morning (the morning after the first evening), but the chapter plan puts his 0600 scene and the Frame drill on Day 3, two mornings after the canteen. It may only mean the next morning after the night off.

- **the-outer-sea-wall** (the-outer-sea-wall.the-tide-decides, the-outer-sea-wall.nobody-comes): the-tide-decides says the walk out is dry only for about four hours either side of low water (a window of up to eight hours each tide); nobody-comes says nobody will see who is there for four hours twice a day (four hours each tide). Either 'four hours either side' means four hours in all, or the two windows are different lengths.

- **the-rock** (the-rock.on-the-surface, the-rock.nine-doors): on-the-surface says the south-east side is bare reinforced ground with a few low vents; nine-doors puts nine round metal doors, each several metres across and ringed in hazard paint, flush with the ground on that same south-east side. Possible tension (the doors may simply be unnoticed or shut by the 'some' who have seen it), for Ingram to settle.

- **the-rock** (the-rock.what-it-is, the-rock.nine-doors): what-it-is (merged above into secret-prison-and-laboratory) says The Rock is reached only by a tunnel from Central, 'with no door of its own'; nine-doors says nine round doors lie flush with the ground and open upward onto the cells. Probably cell hatches rather than ways in, but the wording clashes until someone says so.

- **vermilion-block** (vermilion-block.just-a-department, vermilion-block.what-the-reagents-are-for): just-a-department says the Block has nothing to do with the Watcher, the magic shows or the Kortos rift, while the open question what-the-reagents-are-for asks whether the silo stock connects to anything else in the campaign; the pencil fact would answer the open question before she has.

- **watcher** (watcher.mass-clock, watcher.hidden-numbers): mass-clock says to show the Mass Clock to the party once they understand Sap, while hidden-numbers says the Mass Clock's position is never confirmed to them; sap.gauge's 'a rough gauge, never an exact number' may reconcile them, but it is her call.

- **watcher** (watcher.why-eyes, watcher.drowning-instrument, watcher.shaped-by-manako): The first two say the Watcher's eyes and ears are a sensor's reflex to perceive harder; shaped-by-manako says it has eyes and ears because Manako wanted to be seen and heard (they may be two layers: the reflex first, his portrait later).

- **watcher** (watcher.stays, watcher.fate): stays calls the Watcher a 'perpetual threat' over Kurose after Second Impact, while fate (open) leaves its ending to be chosen (it disperses for good, becomes harmless, or is taken home); 'perpetual' may pre-empt that.

- **watcher** (watcher.native-to-the-phones, era.electronics-source): native-to-the-phones says the Directorate's own electronics are ship technology that leaked out of Kortos; era.electronics-source (open) leaves undecided whether they come from the Kortos leak or the search ship under Kurose.

## Possible overlaps across files

- **abraxas** (abraxas.host-and-alliance, anabala.lived-through-the-ages): Both say Anabala lived through the ages because of her connection to Abraxas (both her words, both nobody/later). abraxas.host-and-alliance (the-host) is the better home, since it says how the host bond began; anabala.lived-through-the-ages could shrink to the longevity consequence, or join it.

- **anabala** (anabala.gen-2-price, frames.gen-2-living): frames.gen-2-living says Gen 2 Frames are Anabala's, living flesh melded with the generation ship's technology. frames.md is the better home for what a Gen 2 is; anabala.gen-2-price keeps the bargain (her price for access).

- **anabala** (anabala.gen-2-price, frames.gen-2-core): frames.gen-2-core (her words) already says the core is biomechanical mitflit technology; frames.md is the better home for the core, and the only thing this item adds is that she built it and so knows what it is.

- **anabala** (anabala.flew-it, frames.small-programme): frames.small-programme (her words) says almost nobody could fly a Gen 2, so it stayed a small programme mothballed once; frames.md is the better home for the programme, and flew-it adds only that Anabala flew it, suffered Frame Sickness, and that the Directorate's record is thin.

- **anabala** (anabala.free-agent, founders.alive): founders.alive (decided, ink) already says Anabala is a free agent; the pencil adds that she is not part of the Directorate yet works with it.

- **anabala** (anabala.the-gathering-is-her-plan, mitflits.the-gathering, mitflits.rare-at-first): All three say the mitflits are gathering in Kurose on purpose to be in one place when they leave. mitflits.the-gathering (what the table sees, Chapter 6) is the better home for the event; the Anabala item should keep her motive and plan.

- **anabala** (anabala.the-gathering-is-her-plan, rupiah.diplomacy-with-mitflits): Rupiah's use of diplomacy with the mitflits is said in both; rupiah.diplomacy-with-mitflits (her words) is the better home for his part.

- **anabala** (anabala.way-home, generation-ship.shuttle-matters): Two open decisions asking nearly the same thing: whether Anabala's way home is the search ship under Kurose or the shuttle under Kortos (anabala.way-home), and whether the shuttle under Kortos matters again, perhaps as Anabala's way home (generation-ship.shuttle-matters). Both are due before Book IV; one question would do.

- **anabala** (anabala.knows-what-the-eyes-were, generation-ship.mind-and-eyes): generation-ship.mind-and-eyes (its eyes, the navigation and sensor system, are the Watcher) is the better home for what the eyes are; the Anabala fact only says what she knows of them (and that she does not know they survived).

- **aoi** (aoi.manakos-wife, envoy.shot, hori.tried-to-save-her): One of Hori's rounds killing Aoi, Manako's wife, is told again in the envoy and hori files; envoy.shot is the better home for the event, aoi.manakos-wife for who she was.

- **aoi** (aoi.unidentified, envoy.two-institutions, directorate.paperwork-disappears-people, the-soldier.on-the-cordon): The Directorate filing her as 'one deceased, unidentified' is told again in the envoy, directorate and the-soldier files.

- **aoi** (aoi.nobody-named-her, envoy.manako-broke-down): envoy.manako-broke-down (her words) already says the circle scattered in the confusion, Manako with them; aoi.nobody-named-her adds the consequence for her name.

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.croglang, baldwin.croglang, column.croglang): Croglang is the castle's old name in baldwin-manor.croglang and again in baldwin.croglang ('the old name of Baldwin's castle'); baldwin-manor.croglang is the better home for the castle, baldwin.croglang for the front company, and column.croglang for the client in the plant's books.

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.the-siege, baldwin.the-grudge, founders.baldwin-manor-fight): The shadow siege, the Baldwin family dying by accident and Baldwin blaming the four are told in all three; baldwin-manor.the-siege (with her the-curious-child) is the better home for the event, baldwin.the-grudge should keep only his blame and the horse, and founders.baldwin-manor-fight the four's night there.

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.manufactory, baldwin-manor.volkheed, baldwin.the-hunters): baldwin.the-hunters repeats that Baldwin ran the family's magical-weapons manufactory at Baldwin Manor, once Croglang Castle, at Volkheed on Kortos; baldwin-manor is the better home for the place, baldwin.the-hunters for the hunters and the WCB note.

- **baldwin-manor** (baldwin-manor.far-from-the-ship, generation-ship.buried): The hidden, jungle-like valley on Kortos where the ship lies is in both; generation-ship.buried is the better home for the valley, and far-from-the-ship's own job is to keep the manor and First Impact apart.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.workshops, mitflits.going-missing): Both say the missing mitflits of the gathering are taken for Baldwin's workshops; mitflits.going-missing (known by few, told in Chapter 6) is what the table sees, baldwin.workshops (nobody, later) the truth behind it, so the sentence could live in one and be cited from the other.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.why-mitflits, mitflits.helpful): Near-identical sentences in her words (mitflits have a horrible characteristic: extremely helpful when given the right tasks, fascinated by the production of technology, which makes them easy to use). mitflits.helpful is the better home (it adds 'never servility'); baldwin.why-mitflits would keep only 'Baldwin knows it better than anyone alive'.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.the-grudge, founders.the-horse): Hyuck paying a thief to steal a hunter's horse and having the hide made into armour is in both; founders.the-horse is the better home for the event, and baldwin.the-grudge for Baldwin's accusation.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.hunter-band, founders.the-nuns): Regina Key and the hunters who killed a gnoll appear in both; founders.the-nuns is the better home for the nuns and Regina's arrest, baldwin.hunter-band for who the hunters were.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.the-organisation-in-kurose, manako.on-paper, manako.arms-length, manako.never-met-the-top): That Manako takes the crime lords' problems on paper and never sees what they become is in all of them; the manako files are the better home for his side, baldwin for who the crime lord turns out to be.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.the-organisation-in-kurose, nobu.whose-money): Nobu's laundering through the crime lords' organisation is said in both (nobu.whose-money adds that the organisation is the oldest enemy of the four who founded the Directorate); nobu.md is the better home for Nobu's side.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.workshops, cicada.heist-loop): The heist crews and what the party can learn from them are in both; cicada.heist-loop is the better home for the loop, baldwin.heist-crews for whose crews they are.

- **baldwin** (baldwin.the-organisation-in-kurose, kurose.crime-trail): kurose.crime-trail (her words, D068) already says that followed far enough Kurose's organised crime leads to Baldwin and to Manako's dealings with the underworld; the Baldwin items add what the trail finds.

- **bobbin** (bobbin.argues-with-anabala, bobbin.refuses-to-leave, mitflits.split): 'Anabala's answer is to leave; Bobbin's is to refuse' is said in mitflits.split and again in bobbin.argues-with-anabala, with the refusal a third time in bobbin.refuses-to-leave; mitflits.split is the better home for the Chapter 13 event, bobbin.argues-with-anabala for the two-century argument behind it.

- **bobbin** (bobbin.remembers-him, mitflits.freedom): mitflits.freedom (which holds the-trap-repeats) says the elders remember the lord's workshop and refuse; bobbin.remembers-him and bobbin.elder are the one named case of it, so the pattern's better home is mitflits and Bobbin's is the example.

- **caretaker** (caretaker.misguided-normalcy, directorate.the-three-poles): The open decision directorate.the-three-poles re-describes the Caretaker's pole (and the Commander's and the Advocate's) in its own text; the person files already hold each pole, so the open decision could shrink to the question alone. Better home for the description of the pole: caretaker.md.

- **caretaker** (caretaker.misguided-normalcy, nobu.paying-a-debt): nobu.paying-a-debt (pencil, nobu.md) also says the Caretaker has to beg Nobu for every accommodation from the technician-day pool: the same charge seen from Nobu's side. Not a duplicate, but the two should be read together.

- **central-fortification** (central-fortification.by-clearance, directorate.clearance): by-clearance (each rung of Clearance opens more of Central) repeats the ladder directorate.clearance already sets out; the better home is directorate.clearance, with Central's own file keeping only what is specific to the building (briefings, debriefs and hearings are held there).

- **central-fortification** (central-fortification.the-seat, directorate.base): directorate.base (provisional, from the Episode One scene From Up Here) already describes the flat grey fortress with no windows at the island's southern end; the-seat and the-face are the richer version in pencil. Small tension for Ingram to look at: 'no windows' (base) against 'armoured covers over its windows' (the-face): reconcilable if the covers are shut when seen from the causeway.

- **central-fortification** (central-fortification.the-face, directorate.island-ways-in): directorate.island-ways-in (decided) says the island has two one-way car bridges that run under a huge bunker; the-face says two roadways enter Central's north-west face under escort. Perhaps the same two roads and Central the 'huge bunker', but nothing says so: worth one sentence from Ingram.

- **cicada** (cicada.inner-circle, manako.list-worked-by-inner-circle, kiri.volunteers): The inner circle carrying out Manako's revenge list is stated in all three; manako.list-worked-by-inner-circle is the better home for the list, cicada.inner-circle for the structure of the Cadre around it.

- **cicada** (cicada.heist-loop, manako.subcontractor): Both say the crime lords time Cicada's demonstrations to cover their heists and Manako does not know; cicada.heist-loop is the better home for the mechanism, manako.subcontractor for the humiliation that follows.

- **cicada** (cicada.works-for-crime-lords, manako.underworld-dealings, manako.ways-to-be-noticed): Cicada working for the crime lords restates Manako providing them with things and getting ways to be noticed in return; manako.underworld-dealings (her words) is the better home.

- **cicada** (cicada.the-show, nozomi.shown-the-watcher, nozomi.stayed): Nozomi's account (shown the Watcher undeniably, given an honest choice) restates what a Cicada show is; cicada.the-show is the better home for the show, nozomi.* for her case.

- **cicada** (cicada.the-show, kiri.recruits): kiri.recruits ('her shows are where Cicada recruits') restates the recruiting function of the show; cicada.the-show is the better home for the show, kiri.recruits for who runs them.

- **cicada** (cicada.the-name, sap.the-name, kurose.cicadas-revered): The cicada-nymph lore (years underground, then out) is stated in three files; each uses it for something different (the cult's way of working, the chemical's name, the city's reverence), so they are adjacent rather than duplicates.

- **cicada** (cicada.lieutenants-witnesses, kiri.how-she-met-him, the-young-one.went-to-a-show): 'Witnesses, not believers' who could not un-see what they were shown is repeated for the group, for Kiri and for the Young One; cicada.lieutenants-witnesses is the better home for the group claim.

- **cicada** (cicada.the-files-drift, nao.not-a-spy): nao.not-a-spy calls Nao's dossier the payoff of the files' drift; related rather than a duplicate, but the drift fact should name Nao's file as its landing.

- **cicada** (cicada.name-for-it, watcher.real-name): Two open decisions on what the thing in the sky is called (Cicada's own name for it; the Watcher's real name beyond 'the Observer'); deciding the second largely decides the first.

- **cicada** (cicada.envoy-when, cicada.began-when, envoy.first-hello): Two open decisions and one sketch with two candidate dates ('about 15 years ago | about 1 year ago') all turn on one question, when the Envoy incident happened; settling it largely settles when Cicada began, and the Soldier's 'years later' and Hori's fifteen years of guilt depend on the same answer.

- **column** (column.croglang, baldwin.croglang): baldwin.croglang says what Croglang is (Baldwin's front, the old name of his castle); column.croglang is only the Chapter 9 find in the plant's books, which is the plan beat beat--croglang as well; the pair belong together.

- **column** (column.second-link, watcher.the-runaway): watcher.the-runaway (the Watcher's own children start making Sap, so it no longer needs Manako, the column or anyone else) is the same runaway the column's second-link fact ends on; watcher.the-runaway is the better home for the runaway itself.

- **column** (column.the-plant, nobu.funds-the-column, nobu.whose-money): The nobu.* facts say who pays for the column (Directorate procurement money, washed through the crime lords); column.the-plant says what it is; adjacent, with the money flow best kept in nobu.whose-money.

- **column** (column.power-bills, column.the-plant, column.cadre-defends, column.croglang): Each also appears as a plan beat in nearly the same words (beat--the-power-bills, beat--the-black-column, beat--the-raid-on-the-plant, beat--croglang, all Chapter 9): the plan holds the moment, the fact holds the world state.

- **commander** (commander.resigns, directorate.falls-to-the-six): commander.resigns (after the deepest vault the Commander resigns, taking responsibility for losing the convoy's cargo) is a specific case of directorate.falls-to-the-six (the people whose judgement failed resign and take full responsibility, and the Deputy Director, the founders and the city put their weight behind the six). Both are known by everyone and told in chapter 13. Better home: directorate.falls-to-the-six, which already says the leadership resigns.

- **commander** (commander.surname, commander.name): Same file, but commander.name is an open decision (Oda is only the older notes' guess), and commander.surname builds a joke on that guess ('Oda in the older notes, shared with a famous unifier'). The pencil fact presupposes the answer to the open one; ink the name first or fold the surname joke into the open question's candidate.

- **commander** (commander.mission-first, directorate.the-three-poles): The open decision directorate.the-three-poles carries the same description of the Commander's pole ('puts the mission above the people carrying it'); the person file already holds it, so the open decision could carry only the question about the Advocate.

- **comms-officer** (comms-officer.radio, directorate.contact): directorate.contact already says contacts are read out over the radio as a numbered track ("track forty-one"); comms-officer.radio adds who does the reading. Better home for the term: directorate.contact.

- **comms-officer** (comms-officer.a-woman, directorate-posts.plot-room-supervisor): Both give a person the same defining trait: knowing exactly how out of date the city's picture is (the Plot Room supervisor is 'acutely aware of it'; the Comms Officer 'knows exactly'). Two people, one idea; Ingram may want it to belong to one.

- **deep-storage** (deep-storage.the-custodian, directorate-posts.deep-storage-custodian): directorate-posts.md has the same post (decides what still counts as 'currently being used', signs off requisitions, knows which floors are real); the post is the better home for the person, deep-storage for the place's rules.

- **deep-storage** (deep-storage.old-frames, deep-storage.unfiled-manifest): Both carry their own 'not decided' sentence inside a pencil fact (which units and how many; what the manifest covers), so each is also a small open decision for her.

- **deputy-director** (deputy-director.signature-on-the-letter, deputy-director.name): Same file, and the second is a proposal rather than pencil, so no merge: deputy-director.name (proposal, from Assembly) already says he is called Seiichi Takamine and that his printed signature on every acceptance letter is too hurried to read. signature-on-the-letter restates that, and is out of date on one point: it says the signature is drawn unreadable 'while his name is a placeholder', which Assembly's name has since replaced. Better home: deputy-director.name; signature-on-the-letter can probably be dropped once that is inked.

- **deputy-director** (deputy-director.reachable, deputy-director.hears-refusals): deputy-director.hears-refusals (provisional) is the scene that makes him reachable: he hears every refused order from a new intake himself, the same day, in his office. The pencil describes the man; the provisional fact the act. Related, not duplicate.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.school-civil-defence-instructor, school-civil-defence-instructor.drills, school-civil-defence-instructor.refused-four-times): The same post told twice from the same source (npc:school-civil-defence-instructor, D075): this 134-word entry here is the whole NPC in one fact, and school-civil-defence-instructor.md already holds it split into drills (some) and refused-four-times (few) plus pays-off (nobody). Better home: school-civil-defence-instructor.md, where each part has its own known level; this entry can go. It also restates directorate.alarm-never-real (provisional): nothing taught has been used in 150 years.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.deep-storage-custodian, deep-storage.the-custodian): The same post told twice. deep-storage.the-custodian (few) says the custodian runs the place day to day, decides what counts as 'currently being used' and remembers everyone let onto a floor above their rank; this entry (some) says the same and adds that the custodian signs off requisitions, knows which floors are real storage and answers to the Commander's pole. Known differs (some here, few there). Better home: deep-storage.md, with the extra sentences added to its fact.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.hospital-chief-of-staff, general-hospital.chief-of-staff, general-hospital.who-oversees-it): general-hospital.chief-of-staff says a Chief of Staff runs the hospital day to day; this entry repeats it and states as fact that the hospital 'nominally answers to Shucks Mordant', which general-hospital.who-oversees-it still holds as an OPEN question ('Ingram has not confirmed it'). A pencil fact is answering an open decision. Better home: general-hospital.md; keep the Shucks line out until she decides.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.school-registrar, main-school-complex.the-real-archive, main-school-complex.nobody-compares): The registrar's office, its second-floor archive and the fact that it sees the raw data and has never been told why are in main-school-complex.md already (the-real-archive, few; nobody-compares, nobody). Better home: main-school-complex.md for the place; this entry for the person only.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.vermilion-block-custodian, directorate-posts.vermilion-block-director, vermilion-block.who-knows): vermilion-block.who-knows (few) says the custodian is no spellcaster and has no idea what the reagents are for, and that the director is the one sign-off that means anything: both posts here say it at length. Better home for the contrast: vermilion-block.md; the person entries here can be shortened to their own details.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.the-rock-warden, directorate-posts.the-rock-research-director, the-rock.warden-and-director): the-rock.md says in one line that a warden runs the prison side and a research director the laboratory side, neither a known face of the Directorate; these two entries say it at length. I folded the one-liner into the-rock's own merged item (see the-rock.json). Better home for the people: this file.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.switchboard-supervisor, the-telephone-exchange.every-call, the-telephone-exchange.eleven-positions): Every call is connected by hand, which makes the operators the best-informed people on the island: stated in the-telephone-exchange.md (every-call, some) and again in this entry. Better home: the-telephone-exchange.md for the place; this entry for the supervisor.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.plot-room-supervisor, the-plot-room.what-it-is, the-plot-room.always-behind): The plotting floor, its markers and headsets, and that the picture is always a few minutes behind are in the-plot-room.md; this entry restates 'acutely aware of how out of date the picture is'. Better home: the-plot-room.md.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.registry-clerk, the-registry.typed-per-clearance, the-registry.the-typists): 'Every Directorate document typed once per clearance level' and 'has read every version and holds no clearance worth the name' are in the-registry.md (typed-per-clearance and the-typists, both few) and again here (some). Better home: the-registry.md.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.works-manager, directorate-posts.chief-draughtsman, directorate-posts.generating-hall-engineer, the-works.the-strip, the-works.crane-schedule): the-works.the-strip already describes the strip of shops (the drawing office where every part exists on paper first, the generating hall, every shop feeding the Erecting Shop), and the-works.crane-schedule says the booking of the Erecting Shop's cranes is the works' real currency, close to what the works manager decides; these three posts restate the place before adding the person and some detail (a hundred metres of drawing boards; four sets, three running). Better home for the places: the-works.md; the posts here for the people.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.canteen-manager, the-works.the-canteen): Feeds the whole works strip in three sittings and rank treated as a suggestion: the-works.the-canteen already says it (some). Better home for the place: the-works.md; this entry for the manager.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.intake-medicals-clerk, directorate.form-12b): This entry (146 words) restates the ink fact directorate.form-12b (decided): Form 12-B asks for mother's maiden name in box 4 and again in box 19. The clerk's own details (a dwarf, flat and patient, one copy for Records and one for Welfare because the two departments have not spoken since the flood) are the new part. Better home for the form: directorate.form-12b.

- **directorate-posts** (directorate-posts.repair-chiefs, hulda-brack.machine-shop, hulda-brack.the-request-loop): hulda-brack.machine-shop says every repair crew answers to her, and hulda-brack.the-request-loop (some) says the six repair-crew chiefs answer to Brack and she is the face of the party's requests to their ground crew: the same six lesser people as this entry. Better home for the chiefs: this file; Hulda's file only needs to say she is their head.

- **directorate** (directorate.reads-the-messages, directorate.two-readers, manako.messages-read): manako.messages-read (decided, D045) already says trainee messages are read by the Directorate as routine policy and by the Watcher, which reads what is typed. two-readers restates it and adds that neither reader knows of the other; reads-the-messages adds the FORM 1-B clause. Better home: manako.messages-read for the fact itself; the pencil here need only keep what it adds.

- **directorate** (directorate.vault-inventory-refused, transparency-advocate.refused-three-times): The same fact in two files: the Transparency Advocate has asked three times for the deep-storage inventory and been told it is above their clearance. Known by some here, by few there. Better home: transparency-advocate.md, the person it happens to.

- **directorate** (directorate.paperwork-disappears-people, directorate.leaves-it-lying, envoy.two-institutions, aoi.unidentified): Aoi filed as 'one deceased, unidentified', and the police court hearing her name while the Directorate never did, is told in four places: here twice, envoy.two-institutions (few; the fullest version, from the Directorate's report written on the day to nobody correcting it) and aoi.unidentified (few, told in chapter 11). Better home: envoy.two-institutions, with aoi.unidentified as the short form; directorate.md keeps only the Directorate's side (Kudo, Nozomi, the pattern of ordinary departments).

- **directorate** (directorate.falls-to-the-six, commander.resigns): Both known by everyone and told in chapter 13: commander.resigns is the Commander's case of the leadership resigning that falls-to-the-six describes. Better home: directorate.falls-to-the-six.

- **directorate** (directorate.first-gen-1, frames.first-gen-1, frames.gen-1-not-alive, frames.golarion-made, directorate.digs): frames.md already says the first Gen 1 Frames were built about 150 years ago after the Directorate came to Kurose (first-gen-1, Claude's pencil), that a Gen 1 is Golarion technology only, a clanker with no living core (gen-1-not-alive, her words), and, as ink, that the Frames in service are Golarion technology informed by study of the generation ship's (frames.golarion-made, decided). directorate.first-gen-1 repeats all three and ends with a sentence ('It dug to recover the ship's technology and lock it in deep storage vaults') that restates the ink fact directorate.digs. Better home: frames.md for the Frames; what is left here is that the Directorate dug up the search ship's hull.

- **directorate** (directorate.born-at-kortos, directorate.kortos-coalition, directorate.founded-after): The ink fact directorate.founded-after (decided) already says four of those who stood before the rift founded the Directorate about five years after it so that the response next time would be faster and more decisive; the pencil founding facts restate that purpose ('make sure the response would be faster next time') and add the coalition at Kortos. Better home for the coalition: kortos-coalition (her words), with born-at-kortos kept only for what it adds.

- **directorate** (directorate.vault-goal, exotic-matter.in-the-deepest-vault, exotic-matter.convoy-carries): vault-goal says what the party breaks into the deepest vault for (the refused inventory and what the Chapter 8 convoy brought in); exotic-matter.md already says the convoy carries exotic matter from the search ship and that it goes into the deepest vault. The convoy's contents are better homed in exotic-matter.md.

- **directorate** (directorate.the-three-poles, commander.mission-first, caretaker.misguided-normalcy, transparency-advocate.satoru): The open decision directorate.the-three-poles carries a paragraph re-describing the Commander, the Caretaker and the Advocate; the three person files already hold each pole in pencil. The open decision could shrink to the question alone (keep the Advocate as written, reshape, or cut back to two poles) and point at the person files.

- **directorate** (directorate.not-a-conspiracy, directorate.decent-adults, idea--no-side-to-pick, idea--nobody-to-unmask): ideas/directorate-institution.md holds two ideas (no side to pick; nobody to unmask) that restate the same pair of sections as these facts: that the Directorate's faults are discoverable and institutional, and that every institution in the campaign is sincere. Facts and ideas overlap; the idea files are the plan for using the facts, so only the wording needs checking for contradictions.

- **directorate** (directorate.magical-swat, mayor.support-forces-cooperation): Both are Ingram's words from D068 and D070 on the same missions (against criminals and cultists): the Directorate brings the party in as an armed-response team; the mayor backs the Directorate so strongly that it and the police are forced into uneasy cooperation on every such raid. Related, not duplicate; the Notes at the top of directorate.md point at the second.

- **directorate** (directorate.who-decided-the-silence, shucks.forbidden-to-explain): The open decision asks who decided that candidates mustn't be told what the blood screen tests for (a standing regulation, the Deputy Director, the Commander); shucks.forbidden-to-explain (pencil, in shucks.md) already answers part of it: the silence is the institution's, not Shucks's, because the Directorate doesn't want anyone to know who its compatible pilots are before it does. A pencil fact is leaning on an open decision.

- **envoy** (envoy.police-kept-the-truth, kurose-police.the-file): Both say the police file names the officer who fired; kurose-police.the-file is the better home (it is a clue road in aois-name).

- **envoy** (envoy.two-institutions, directorate.leaves-it-lying, directorate.paperwork-disappears-people, the-soldier.on-the-cordon): The Directorate filing Aoi as 'one deceased, unidentified' and the police court hearing her name are told again in the directorate and the-soldier files.

- **envoy** (envoy.directorate-line, directorate.leaves-it-lying): The Directorate's own line that a cultist was killed while someone summoned a monster is stated in both.

- **envoy** (envoy.watcher-learned, envoy.swarm-felt-nothing, watcher.answers-the-clash, watcher.what-children-are-for): 'Since the Envoy they are also defenders of what the Watcher makes' and 'the Envoy's lesson repeating small' are the same lesson in the watcher file; watcher.what-children-are-for is the better home for its effect on the children.

- **envoy** (envoy.first-hello, watcher.sightings, cicada.envoy-when): Her watcher.sightings already says the Envoy was its first real appearance in public, and cicada.envoy-when (open) already asks when it happened; first-hello restates both.

- **envoy** (envoy.shot, aoi.manakos-wife, hori.tried-to-save-her): One of Hori's rounds killing Aoi, Manako's wife, is stated in all three; envoy.shot is the better home for the event.

- **era** (era.uneven-tech, era.engines): era.engines (provisional, in effect decided) already says ordinary society's great works are built with engines; uneven-tech restates that and adds the Frames.

- **era** (era.dug-up-not-invented, era.uneven-tech, frames.look-of-gen-1): The Frames being far more advanced than the street they work in is also stated in the frames file; frames.look-of-gen-1 is the better home for what Gen 1 looks like.

- **exotic-matter** (exotic-matter.convoy-carries, exotic-matter.in-the-deepest-vault, directorate.vault-goal): What the chapter 8 convoy brought into the deepest vault is also the goal of the break-in in directorate.vault-goal and the Watcher's take in second-rift.md.

- **exotic-matter** (exotic-matter.what-it-is, rift.cause): The container of exotic fuel the four fumbled on the cargo deck is the ink fact rift.cause; what-it-is restates 'what the four dropped at Kortos'.

- **exotic-matter** (exotic-matter.the-convoy, search-ship.second-excavation): The convoy comes into the base from the dig, which is the second excavation; the two could cite each other instead of restating.

- **founders** (founders.founding-day-anthem, founders.ribbon, rupiah.cuts-the-ribbon): founding-day-anthem restates two ink facts (founders.ribbon; rupiah.cuts-the-ribbon: Rupiah usually cuts the Founding Day ribbon) and adds the anthem about the harbour. It also says Rupiah has cut it 'since the party's grandparents were children', which sits oddly with the Assembly's rule that every character is a newcomer, and rupiah.public-face says he wrote the civil defence anthem without saying whether that is the harbour anthem. plan/books.md (Book II's breather) repeats it.

- **founders** (founders.never-knew-the-watcher, watcher.unknown): Both say the founders do not know the Watcher exists. watcher.unknown (her words, D040) is the better home for the general claim; never-knew-the-watcher adds that they knew they caused the Kortos rift and that it is news to all four in chapter 11, which plan/chapter-11 and questions/how-first-impact-happened also carry.

- **founders** (founders.as-they-were, hyuck.bonebox, rupiah.as-he-was, shucks.shuck-mord, anabala.macthata): as-they-were is the four's old-campaign classes in one paragraph; each founder's own file already holds their half (Hyuck the barbarian with his brother's skull, Rupiah the oracle, Shucks the skeleton rogue, Anabala the pact-broker). The per-person files are the better home.

- **founders** (founders.diplomacy-first, mitflits.gauntlight): Both say the four befriended the mitflits and helped them build a government at Gauntlight Keep. mitflits.gauntlight is the better home for the mitflits' side; diplomacy-first adds the first sessions in Otari bending away from the script.

- **founders** (founders.the-plea, mitflits.gauntlight-council): Comhphas appears in both; mitflits.gauntlight-council names the triumvirate (Comhphas, Scrawng and Anabala).

- **founders** (founders.luminara, nirvana.luminara, nirvana.the-relic, rift.sarenrae-sent): Sarenrae receiving the four in Luminara and setting them to mend the tear appears in founders.luminara and again in nirvana.luminara (the city) and nirvana.the-relic (what she gave them and the promise to return them). nirvana.md is the better home for the place and the relic. Note the open rift.sarenrae-sent still asks what exactly she sent them back with, which nirvana.the-relic already answers (a relic).

- **founders** (founders.three-forts, founders.baldwin-manor-fight, baldwin-manor.the-siege, baldwin-manor.croglang): The fight at Baldwin Manor and Croglang Castle's renaming are also in baldwin-manor.md. the-siege says family members died in the siege by accident and not at the four's hands, which answers the accusation in founders.baldwin-manor-fight; baldwin-manor.md is the better home for the manor.

- **founders** (founders.the-nuns, founders.the-horse, baldwin.hunter-band, baldwin.known-on-sight): The hunters, Regina Key, the killing of a gnoll and Hyuck's horse are also in baldwin.md (hunter-band, known-on-sight: 'for Hyuck it is also about a horse'); baldwin.md is the better home for the hunters.

- **founders** (founders.the-others, nirvana.djarvik): Djarvik, the gnoll sorcerer, appears in both: the-others says he joined before Nirvana, nirvana.djarvik what happened to him there.

- **founders** (founders.old-loose-ends, baldwin.gauntlight-siege): Two open decisions asking the same thing: what became of the besieged mitflits at Gauntlight (loose-ends lists 'the rest of the mitflits at Gauntlight'; gauntlight-siege asks what happened to them). Both are 'before the founders' flashbacks'. Open facts are never merged.

- **founders** (founders.old-campaign-closure, rift.sarenrae-sent): old-campaign-closure (her words: this campaign closes the old one, which stopped in Nirvana) is a campaign-level claim also reflected in rift.sarenrae-sent and plan/chapter-15, which say Kortos was closed and the old campaign ends in Nirvana; it is not really about the founders.

- **frames** (frames.first-gen-1, directorate.first-gen-1): Both say the first Gen 1 Frames were built about 150 years ago after the Directorate came to Kurose. directorate.first-gen-1 is the richer home (it dug up the search ship's hull, Golarion technology only, no living core, technology locked in deep storage), and the two disagree on `known` (frames: some; directorate: few).

- **frames** (frames.gen-1-not-alive, directorate.first-gen-1, taka.flies-gen-1): All three say a Gen 1 has no living core and is a mechanical clanker. frames.gen-1-not-alive is the best home (her words, and it adds no seat that shapes itself and nothing that takes control).

- **frames** (frames.gen-1-diggers, frames.gen-1-not-alive, frames.mechanical, frames.golarion-made): frames.gen-1-diggers and frames.gen-1-not-alive restate two ink facts (frames.mechanical: purely mechanical clankers, hard work to drive; frames.golarion-made: Golarion technology informed by study of the ship). The ink ones stand.

- **frames** (frames.small-programme, anabala.flew-it): Both say almost nobody could fly a Gen 2 and the programme was mothballed. frames.small-programme (her words) is the better home; anabala.flew-it adds that Anabala flew it, suffered Frame Sickness and that Shucks's screen later found the pilots.

- **frames** (frames.compatibility, directorate.the-marker, shucks.found-the-marker): directorate.the-marker is ink (decided): a heritable blood marker, only carriers can pilot a Gen 2, all six of the party carry it. frames.compatibility restates it and adds, in her words, that it is not always a gene (a robot, android or being of light can have it), which loosens the ink fact's 'in their blood'. Worth her eye.

- **frames** (frames.repair-crews, directorate-posts.repair-chiefs): Both say each of the six pilots has their own repair crew chief. directorate-posts.repair-chiefs holds the roster (vehicle upkeep, answering to Hulda Brack, names open); frames.repair-crews holds the pilot's side (met at Familiarisation, round-the-clock baseline repair, the unasked-for night shift). One home would do.

- **frames** (frames.gen-2-living, frames.gen-2, directorate.knows-gen-2-alive): gen-2-living (Gen 2 is living flesh melded with the ship's technology) restates ink frames.gen-2 (built from mitflit technology), her gen-2-core, and directorate.knows-gen-2-alive (the Directorate knows the prototypes are alive).

- **frames** (frames.prototype-under-the-tarp, main-mech-hangar.nine-bays, frames.legacy-hangar): Three hangars with unclear relations: a Gen 2 prototype under a tarp in 'Hangar Four', the Main Mech Hangar of nine bays (six hold Frames), and a hangar of retired and never-assigned Frames. Whether Hangar Four is one of the nine bays, and whether the legacy hangar is a separate place, is not stated anywhere.

- **frames** (frames.legacy-hangar, deep-storage.old-frames): Two answers to where retired Frames go: frames.legacy-hangar says a hangar of retired and never-assigned Gen 1 stock, deep-storage.old-frames says hardware too compromised, obsolete or politically awkward is quietly moved to Deep Storage. They may be two places or one; deep-storage is the better home for what is hidden, frames for what the party could be issued.

- **general-hospital** (general-hospital.chief-of-staff, directorate-posts.hospital-chief-of-staff): Both say a Chief of Staff runs the hospital day to day and decides what a visitor can reach; the directorate-posts version adds wards, staff and who is allowed to see whom. Better home: general-hospital.chief-of-staff, since it is the hospital's own file.

- **general-hospital** (general-hospital.requisitioned, rest--general-hospital): The haunt scene rest--general-hospital already has the hospital as a department store once, with the front windows painted over from the inside and the old brass sign over Ward Four; the pencil fact only says the building was requisitioned and how it shows. Better home for the play: the scene.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.mind-and-eyes, watcher.what-it-is, mother.ships-mind): All say the ship's mind is Mother and its eyes are the Watcher, torn apart at First Impact; watcher.what-it-is is the better home.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.sap-store, sap.what-it-is): The ship's stock of raw material for its repair nanites is Sap is said in both; sap.what-it-is is the better home (told in chapter 9).

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.technology-leaked, era.dug-up-not-invented, watcher.native-to-the-phones): The ship's technology leaked outwards from Kortos, which is why a 1950s world has phones and giant machines, is said in three files; era.dug-up-not-invented is the better home.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.restricted, frames.gen-2-source, directorate.kortos-coalition): That the Directorate holds the Kortos site and keeps its technology restricted is restated in frames.gen-2-source and, with the coalition that formed it, in directorate.kortos-coalition.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.inside, mother.met-by-the-four): The reasoning-core chamber, the bridge deck and the cargo deck are described in both; generation-ship is the better home for the ship, mother for the meeting.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.name, search-ship.name): One old open question (excavated-spaceship-name) filed twice, once for each ship, with the same decide-by; they could be one open decision.

- **generation-ship** (generation-ship.shuttle-matters, generation-ship.fuel-purpose): Two open decisions about the same buried shuttle (does it matter again as Anabala's way home; what the mitflits meant to do with it), both decide-by Book IV; they could be one.

- **glamour** (glamour.the-road-in-choice, glamour.debate, glamour.refusers): The ink facts already say that aliens refuse to disguise and draw stares and that glamour is cheap; the-road-in-choice adds the player's own call and how the GM rolls with it, so it extends rather than repeats them.

- **hori** (hori.and-the-soldier, the-soldier.on-the-cordon): the-soldier.on-the-cordon already says 'Hori fired in a street, and the Soldier filed it: neither knows the other carries the same day'; it is the better home for the Soldier's side.

- **hori** (hori.kept-her-name, kurose-police.the-file, envoy.police-kept-the-truth): The police file that names the officer who fired is also stated in kurose-police.the-file (a clue road in the question aois-name) and envoy.police-kept-the-truth; kurose-police.the-file is the better home.

- **hori** (hori.tried-to-save-her, envoy.shot, aoi.manakos-wife, envoy.manako-broke-down): The shooting and Aoi's death are told again in the envoy and aoi files; envoy.shot is the better home for the event, this file for what he did next.

- **hori** (hori.peril, nozomi.peril): Peril Roster entries (v4:21) are filed one per person across files; the roster could have a single home instead.

- **hulda-brack** (hulda-brack.the-shop-boss, directorate-posts.repair-chiefs): That the six pilots' repair-crew chiefs answer to Hulda Brack is said in both; directorate-posts.repair-chiefs is the better home for the six chiefs (their names and manner are open), hulda-brack for her role over them.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.statue-as-described, hyuck.statue): The decided hyuck.statue already says people take the harbour statue for somebody's mascot; statue-furniture adds that it is ordinary civic furniture, which is the same attitude described more fully.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.unnamed-until-12, founders.pictures-are-the-lie): 'The statue and the test pilot agree, and the pictures don't' (founders.pictures-are-the-lie, told in Chapter 12) is the same moment as the party learning that the statue was always Hyuck; founders is the better home for the lie about the pictures, hyuck for the unmasking.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.statue-an-earlier-body, founders.older-printing): The older printing's memorial plate (the four as they were) is described in founders.older-printing, which is the better home; this item should only point at it.

- **hyuck** (hyuck.old-ways, founders.as-they-were): founders.as-they-were already gives Hyuck as a gnoll barbarian who talked to his ancestors; hyuck.old-ways is the longer version of the same habit.

- **directorate-episode-two** (idea--speak-for-the-comms-officer, deputy-director.hears-refusals): The idea builds on the provisional fact that the Deputy Director hears every refused order from a new intake himself, the same day; the fact is the premise and the idea is what he does with it in Episode Two.

- **directorate-episode-two** (idea--a-doctor-without-an-appointment, shucks.standing-with-the-deputy): The idea is a scene that works on the open question of why Shucks has the Deputy Director's ear, and hands a critical failure a tidy wrong answer (his physician, an old friend from training); the open fact lists the real candidates.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--mitflits-feel-the-ship, idea--why-gen-2-unsettles, mitflits.avoid-gen-2, mitflits.dont-know-gen-2): Same idea in two files, near word for word (wild mitflits feel something of their ancestors' ship in living flesh; uncomfortable isn't unable); mergeable once filed together, and the facts already hold the unease itself.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--the-watchers-early-voice, idea--the-voice-changes, watcher.g6): Both are how the Watcher sounds later in the campaign (the 'reel' line is also canon's G6); they belong in one file and one reading of its voice.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--themes-on-both-sides, watcher.the-two-themes, manako.messages-read): The first sentence restates Ingram's two themes (surveillance and the runaway) and the decided fact that trainee messages are read by the Directorate as routine policy and by the Watcher.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--the-mirror-of-two-institutions, idea--nobody-did-evil, idea--themes-on-both-sides): All three state the thesis that nobody did anything evil and every catastrophe is an ordinary person flinching and building on it; the Watcher idea (watcher-the-swarm) says it for the swarm.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--nobody-to-unmask, school-civil-defence-instructor.refused-four-times, deputy-director.holds-both): The idea's examples (the instructor refused four times; the Deputy Director who says they were wrong, would give the same order, is glad they live) are already facts; only the rule they illustrate is new.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--directorate-money-reaches-baldwin, nobu.whose-money, nobu.knows-whose): The fact already says procurement money washed through the crime lords pays for the column, and that if they are the founders' oldest enemy the Directorate pays for what poisons its sky; the idea is the 'yes, Baldwin's' answer, and the open fact asks whether Nobu knows.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--hyucks-crash-protocol, hyuck.protocol): The open fact only says Hyuck's protocol is a Gen 2 thing still to be pinned down; the idea is a candidate answer to it.

- **directorate-institution** (idea--true-forms-hidden, glamour.cheap, glamour.debate, glamour.the-road-in-choice, frames.gen-2-inside): The idea says the reason it works in the fiction is not decided, but canon has decided it: glamour is cheap and everywhere, and whether to disguise (and whether to show a true form from a Gen 2 seat) is already each player's call. Likely adopted.

- **flashbacks** (idea--book-three-flashback, idea--flashback-two-moves-to-anabala): Same flashback in two files (Anabala building Gen 2 and deciding what the Directorate will be told about the core); mergeable once filed together (both live, same author, only one carries 'book iii'). This one adds the alternative of the founding itself, the four signing the charter.

- **flashbacks** (idea--book-three-flashback, frames.gen-2-when, anabala.party-learns-gen-2): Canon's open facts already refer to this flashback: frames.gen-2-when estimates 'decades ago' for it, and anabala.party-learns-gen-2 lists 'never, until the flashbacks' as a candidate.

- **flashbacks** (idea--book-three-flashback, beat--flashback-baldwin-manor, beat--flashback-first-impact, beat--flashback-nirvana, beat--flashback-the-arrival): The plan holds four flashback beats (chapters 3, 7, 15 and 17); this is the old list's remaining entry (the old flashbacks.2), the only one never made a beat.

- **flashbacks** (idea--book-three-flashback, idea--the-last-stand-flashback, idea--dropped-in-without-warning): Flashback ideas are filed in four files (this one, people-founders, sweep-v4, sweep-ledger); joining them in flashbacks.md would let them be read, and merged where they repeat, together.

- **misc-antagonists** (idea--the-dig-is-the-danger, search-ship.digging-louder, watcher.drawn-by-the-call, search-ship.still-calling): Largely restated in canon: the Directorate's digging makes the search ship's call louder, the call drew the Watcher, and the dig under Kurose is its nest; the only addition is holding it back as the reserved 'real danger' reveal.

- **misc-antagonists** (idea--something-worse-beyond, idea--the-gods-knew, mitflit-home-world.who-fells-ships, beat--why-ships-fall): Both float Golarion's gods as what brings ships down; canon holds Ingram's 'perhaps' version as an open fact and chapter 17's beat answers why ships fall. One idea is parked and one live, and they sit in different files, so they stay apart.

- **misc-clues** (idea--the-reagent-stakeout, idea--the-lab-stakeout): Same idea filed twice (dull reagents missing in bulk over three months, a two-in-the-morning fight through a window with four professionals who fight to flee); mergeable once filed together (both parked, same author, only the other carries 'chapter 3').

- **misc-clues** (idea--the-reagent-stakeout, idea--the-stores-cupboard): The stakeout 'would pay off' the stock walking off the instrument makers' shelves, which is the stores-cupboard plant (unplanted-clues); that plant pays off in Chapter 9 as the column's raw materials, so the two are rival payoffs for one plant.

- **misc-clues** (idea--first-gen-2-sighting, beat--gen-2-offered, beat--first-gen-2-flight, frames.gen-1-clank-grace): The idea lists ways the plan's chapter 4 offer and chapter 5 first flight could play (seen before the offer, the offer itself, or only once flown); the clank-and-grace contrast is already the fact frames.gen-1-clank-grace.

- **misc-clues** (idea--censored-papers, idea--the-longer-version, the-registry.typed-per-clearance): The same several-versions-of-one-document premise: the-longer-version (places-hooks) agrees with the Registry fact (a typist who has to be persuaded); censored-papers is the one that disagrees.

- **misc-hyuck-origin** (idea--hyuck-brothers-skull, idea--hyucks-count, hyuck.bonebox, hyuck.fratricide): Rival readings of the elder brother's skull: it holds him and he rides with Hyuck now (hyucks-count), or it holds a construct of Hyuck's idealised brother, or somebody else's skull (this idea); canon holds only that Hyuck carried it and consulted it (hyuck.bonebox) and that he may have killed his brother (hyuck.fratricide).

- **misc-hyuck-origin** (idea--hyuck-offended-a-god, idea--a-carrying-seen): a-carrying-seen (people-founders) asks whether Steve's original pitch, the offended god and the time-magic mishap, comes in; this idea is that pitch.

- **misc-hyuck-origin** (idea--hyuck-psychic-cockpit, idea--a-seat-that-cannot-settle, frames.gen-2-inside): Both are Hyuck in a Gen 2 seat that reshapes to the pilot's true form (the fact); psychic-cockpit adds latching beliefs, the seat idea adds the crowd inside him that stops it settling.

- **misc-hyuck-origin** (idea--hyuck-contained-asset, idea--hyucks-bearers): The 'well-looked-after asset' line in hyucks-bearers grows from this pitch, which itself concludes that the living Hyuck is a test pilot, not a contained asset.

- **people-ch1** (idea--the-lab-stakeout, idea--the-reagent-stakeout): Same idea filed twice (this file holds only this one idea): bulk dull reagents missing over three months, a two-in-the-morning fight through a window with four professionals who fight to flee; mergeable once filed together (both parked, same author, only this one carries 'chapter 3').

- **people-ch1** (idea--the-lab-stakeout, idea--the-stores-cupboard): The plant this stakeout would pay off is the instrument makers' stores cupboard, but that plant is set to pay off in Chapter 9 as the column's raw materials, while this idea says chapter 3.

- **people-founders** (idea--mothers-pieces, idea--mother-in-the-cores): Same idea in two files: mothers-pieces is word for word the first sentence of mother-in-the-cores (watcher-mitflits), which adds the consequences; mergeable once filed together (both live, same author, no dates).

- **people-founders** (idea--flashback-two-moves-to-anabala, idea--book-three-flashback, frames.gen-2-when): Same flashback in two files (Anabala building Gen 2 and deciding what the Directorate will be told about the core); mergeable once filed together (one carries 'book iii'), and canon's open fact frames.gen-2-when already estimates 'decades ago' for it.

- **people-founders** (idea--rands-archive-published, beat--the-archive-published): Restates the chapter 20 beat ('Rand's archive is published as testimony'); the only addition, 'at the end of the campaign', is where chapter 20 already sits.

- **people-founders** (idea--the-carrying-in-detail, hyuck.the-carrying): The closing sentence (the losing spirit rides as a voice, the winner wears the body, the bearer is pushed back) restates the fact; the rest is added detail.

- **people-founders** (idea--hyucks-count, hyuck.never-lost, hyuck.fratricide, hyuck.bonebox): That Hyuck shares his body with a crowd of beaten spirits is already a fact; the new parts are 'five or six times' and his elder brother riding with him (idea--hyuck-brothers-skull offers a different reading of that skull).

- **people-founders** (idea--a-seat-that-cannot-settle, frames.gen-2-inside, idea--hyuck-psychic-cockpit): Its first sentence restates the fact that the seat reshapes to the pilot's true form; the new part is that a crowd inside Hyuck stops it settling, which is close to the psychic-cockpit idea in misc-hyuck-origin.

- **people-founders** (idea--hyucks-bearers, idea--hyuck-contained-asset): The 'well-looked-after asset' line grows from the contained-asset pitch in misc-hyuck-origin, which itself says the living Hyuck is a test pilot, not a contained asset.

- **people-founders** (idea--the-hospital-answers-to-shucks, general-hospital.who-oversees-it, directorate-posts.hospital-chief-of-staff): Already in canon: the open fact lists 'Shucks Mordant' as its first candidate and the Chief of Staff post says the hospital nominally answers to him; the idea is a third copy of that candidate.

- **people-founders** (idea--a-carrying-seen, idea--hyuck-offended-a-god): a-carrying-seen asks whether Steve's original pitch (the offended god, the time-magic mishap) comes in; idea--hyuck-offended-a-god in misc-hyuck-origin is that pitch.

- **people-founders** (idea--what-abraxas-costs-her, anabala.lived-through-the-ages): Builds on the fact that Anabala lived through the ages because of Abraxas; the new part is the cost of letting the demon out.

- **places-hooks** (idea--nine-and-nine, main-mech-hangar.nine-bays, main-mech-hangar.three-empty, the-rock.nine-doors, the-rock.what-the-cells-hold): Canon already holds both nines (six Frames and three empty bays; nine doors onto cells sized for a Frame) and the open question of what the cells hold; the idea only asks whether they are one plan.

- **places-hooks** (idea--the-director-knows-more, vermilion-block.who-knows, vermilion-block.what-the-reagents-are-for): A question, not an idea: canon says the director is the only person on site who might know what the reagents are for, and holds their purpose as an open fact; this belongs there.

- **places-hooks** (idea--deep-storage-custodian-cast, deep-storage.the-custodian, directorate-posts.deep-storage-custodian): Canon already has the custodian, the favours and the debt called in later; the idea asks only whether they become a named, recurring character (a casting question, not an idea).

- **places-hooks** (idea--deep-storage-is-the-vaults, directorate.digs, exotic-matter.in-the-deepest-vault): Canon says the Directorate locks what it digs up in deep storage vaults and that the convoy's exotic matter goes into the deepest vault; the idea asks whether Deep Storage is those vaults, which is an open question rather than an idea.

- **places-hooks** (idea--the-longer-version, the-registry.typed-per-clearance, idea--censored-papers): Agrees with the fact that a fuller version is a different, typed object that takes persuading a person (a typist); the censored-papers idea in misc-clues is the one that disagrees with that fact.

- **places-hooks** (idea--the-hospital-ward, idea--the-hospital-answers-to-shucks, general-hospital.chief-of-staff): Same place, different questions: this hook has an empty-looking ward and a Chief of Staff's favour; the parked idea in people-founders asks who the hospital answers to.

- **places-hooks** (idea--the-pictures-were-chosen, idea--four-portraits-confuse, idea--founders-letterhead, founders.pictures): About the founders' approved pictures, not a place: the importer filed it here from the gnoll statue's record, but it belongs with the founders' picture ideas (why the portraits differ from the truth).

- **sweep-ledger** (idea--four-portraits-confuse, idea--founders-letterhead, founders.pictures, idea--the-pictures-were-chosen): All build on the accepted fact that every approved picture shows four tall, handsome humans; the-pictures-were-chosen (places-hooks) adds why the founders wanted the pictures changed.

- **sweep-ledger** (idea--expeditions, search-ship.second-excavation): The second excavation, off the city map in chapter 8, is the first of these expeditions and is already canon; the idea generalises it to 'a few times'.

- **sweep-ledger** (idea--dropped-in-without-warning, idea--book-three-flashback, idea--the-last-stand-flashback, party.flashback-characters): A surprise alternative to the announced, optional flashbacks the plan holds (and canon notes Jamie and Anne still need flashback characters of their own).

- **sweep-v4** (idea--nobody-has-to-die, watcher.nothing-dies, watcher.condensations): 'No corpses on the enemy side' is already canon: nothing the party beats ever dies, and the children are condensations that cannot be killed by attrition; the new part is the first-class ending and the kill, capture, destroy, protect options.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--the-last-stand-flashback, idea--book-three-flashback, idea--dropped-in-without-warning, beat--flashback-first-impact, beat--flashback-nirvana): Flashback ideas filed in four files. The plan's First Impact flashback ends 'on the catch' (the decision that began it) and the Nirvana one is the old campaign's unfinished ending; this idea adds the rule that every decision was defensible and is not softened afterwards, but does not say which flashback it is.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--the-three-modes, idea--the-hull-breach, idea--mother-in-the-cores, idea--mothers-pieces): The same hole in the same position on every Gen 2 (called metal fatigue, really the core making a seat) is the hull-breach plant, and the three modes all rest on a piece of Mother being in every Gen 2.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--six-threads, manako-and-cicada, the-watcher, the-gathering, the-founders): The six old threads map onto canon's thread files only in part: The Cult is manako-and-cicada, The Observed is the-watcher, The Mitflits is the-gathering and The Founders is the-founders; The War and School have no thread file, and the questions and currencies given here are not in any.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--the-stray-as-tonal-keystone, stray.visible): The open fact on whether the stray can be seen starts from the same older notes (a companion that follows one character around waiting to be fed); the idea adds the tonal claim and that it is a broken-off piece of the Watcher.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--the-kindest-payoff, idea--the-voice-changes, watcher.drowning-instrument): The same turn told three ways: the Watcher was never stupid, it was new and drowning; the voice idea gives its early lines and the fact gives the mechanism.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--the-reel-with-the-anthem, watcher.g6, beat--the-reel, idea--the-watchers-early-voice): The replay of the party's last fight is canon's G6 and chapter 11's beat (and the early-voice idea's 'I'll show you the reel'); the anthem hummed back is the only new part.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--what-hand-sized-threats-take, watcher.g4, beat--close-to-home): Restates the G4 beat (a shop, a sibling's school, a teacher's door); the new part is that what they take is trust and time, not a life.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--heroes-and-they-hate-it, watcher.the-mirror, question:why-it-fights-like-them): The Mirror (how they fight teaches it) is canon; the idea is its public consequence for the campus and the party.

- **sweep-v4** (idea--the-asking, mitflits.young-pilots, mitflits.the-trap-repeats): Canon has the young mitflits volunteering to fight and the elders refusing; this idea says mitflits enter Frames only to defend and asks the end-of-campaign question of who asks them to attack.

- **the-mayor** (idea--mayor-first-appearance, mayor.fan-of-directorate, mayor.glad-to-say-so, mayor.support-forces-cooperation): That the mayor is a big fan of the Directorate, glad to say so, and forces it and the police into uneasy cooperation is already canon; the idea adds when and how the mayor is first met (from a distance in Book I, in person in Chapters 3 and 4), the form of the blessing and the curse, which the mayor file's own notes list as not yet decided.

- **the-title** (idea--what-the-title-means, idea--escheat, founders.old-campaign-closure): Three readings of Blank Inheritance: the party inherits the founders' mistake, a ship's eyes broken loose with the ship's mind missing (this); an estate whose heir hasn't been named, the mitflits (escheat, watcher-the-swarm); and Ingram's own 'an attempt to tie everything up at once', closing the old campaign. They may layer or compete, which is hers to say.

- **unplanted-clues** (idea--the-hull-breach, idea--the-three-modes): The same hole in the same position on every Gen 2 prototype, called metal fatigue and really the core making its seat, is the premise of the three modes (sweep-v4); both parked, but the three-modes text is already over the 170-word cap.

- **unplanted-clues** (idea--the-stores-cupboard, idea--the-reagent-stakeout, idea--the-lab-stakeout): Plant and rival payoffs: the stakeout ideas say the missing reagents pay off at a chapter 3 fight, while this plant pays off in Chapter 9 as the column's raw materials.

- **unplanted-clues** (idea--the-gentle-frame, hyuck.unnamed-until-12, beat--the-statue-agrees): Canon has the gnoll test pilot in the prototype, learned in chapter 7 to be like the statue marked HYUCK and not named until Chapter 12; the plant here is the gentle Frame that carries the chapter 7 payoff.

- **watcher-deep-past** (idea--baldwin-looted-kortos, idea--seen-through-baldwin, idea--directorate-money-reaches-baldwin, baldwin.alive): Three ideas in three files about Baldwin: he looted the opened ship, which is part of how he is still alive (this); if ship technology keeps him alive the Watcher may see through him (watcher-the-swarm); Directorate money reaches him (directorate-institution). The first two could merge once filed together (both live, same author).

- **watcher-deep-past** (idea--the-gods-knew, idea--something-worse-beyond, mitflit-home-world.who-fells-ships, prophecy.asked): Both float Golarion's gods as what brings ships down; canon holds Ingram's 'perhaps' as an open fact, and prophecy.asked has Rupiah asking the gods whether it would happen again; one idea is live and one parked, in different files, so they stay apart.

- **watcher-mitflits** (idea--why-gen-2-unsettles, idea--mitflits-feel-the-ship, mitflits.avoid-gen-2, mitflits.dont-know-gen-2): Same idea in two files, near word for word (directorate-institution holds the copy); mergeable once filed together (both live, same author, no dates). The facts already hold the unease itself, and Ingram's own fact adds that the mitflits must not instantly understand where Gen 2 comes from.

- **watcher-mitflits** (idea--mother-in-the-cores, idea--mothers-pieces, watcher.the-temptation, watcher.g3): mothers-pieces (people-founders) is the first sentence of this idea; merge it here once filed together. Canon's open watcher.the-temptation names 'a piece of Mother in every Gen 2' as the likeliest mechanism, and watcher.g3 already has the Seekers prefer Gen 2 because it is built from their own ship's technology.

- **watcher-mitflits** (idea--rapid-adaptation, mitflits.degraded, mitflits.muddled, mitflits.look): Her own 'perhaps' offers a different reading of how the mitflits fell from star-faring to small (adapted downward and able to regrow the High form) against her facts that they became weaker, more primitive and muddled; hers to choose or reconcile.

- **watcher-mitflits** (idea--the-slow-reveal, mitflits.rare-at-first, mitflits.the-gathering, mitflits.going-missing): Canon holds the arrival in numbers; the idea adds the earlier beats (the glimpse, the captive who goes quiet near a Frame) and the warning not to compress.

- **watcher-the-swarm** (idea--the-voice-changes, idea--the-watchers-early-voice, watcher.drowning-instrument): Both describe the Watcher's voice over the campaign (the other, in directorate-institution, conflicts with it); the fact watcher.drowning-instrument already holds the overwhelmed early state, and the Voice drafted in world/watcher.md is the calm later voice.

- **watcher-the-swarm** (idea--claimants-stake-claims, watcher.what-children-are-for, watcher.wants): Largely restated: canon already says some children are claim-stakers pushing the edge of what it can see, and that the Watcher wants to expand; the new part is that the city's word, Claimant, is accidentally right.

- **watcher-the-swarm** (idea--escheat, idea--what-the-title-means, founders.old-campaign-closure): Another reading of the title (see the-title): an estate whose heir hasn't been named, with the ships belonging to the mitflits.

- **watcher-the-swarm** (idea--nobody-did-evil, idea--the-mirror-of-two-institutions, idea--themes-on-both-sides, watcher.no-malice): The same thesis in several files (nobody did anything evil, each step reasonable); canon already says the Watcher has no malice and escalates because it observes, adapts and responds.

- **watcher-the-swarm** (idea--seen-through-baldwin, idea--baldwin-looted-kortos, baldwin.alive): Builds on the fact that Baldwin is alive two hundred years later; the premise ('if ship technology is what keeps him alive') is the looting idea in watcher-deep-past, and the two could merge once filed together.

- **watcher-the-swarm** (idea--watcher-fate-in-a-hull, watcher.fate): The open fact lists Ingram's three endings (disperses, survives harmless, the mitflits take it home); the idea adds three older options (bound, contained, put back in a hull).

- **inicrium** (inicrium.shadow-plane, inicrium.what-it-is, baldwin-manor.the-siege): baldwin-manor.the-siege is built from the same two old journals (inicrium and the Shadow Plane intrusion) and restates both: an Inicrium battery pulled the Shadow Plane into the town. The manor file holds the event; inicrium the substance and the rule.

- **kiri** (kiri.recruits, cicada.the-show): kiri.recruits ('her shows are where Cicada recruits') restates the recruiting function that cicada.the-show describes; cicada.the-show is the better home for what a show is, kiri.recruits for who runs them.

- **kiri** (kiri.volunteers, cicada.inner-circle, manako.list-worked-by-inner-circle): The inner circle working through Manako's revenge list is stated in all three; manako.list-worked-by-inner-circle is the better home for the list.

- **kiri** (kiri.how-she-met-him, cicada.lieutenants-witnesses): 'Like all four lieutenants, she is a witness, not a believer' repeats the group fact cicada.lieutenants-witnesses; the group fact is the better home.

- **kiri** (kiri.shares-a-teacher, rupiah.as-he-was): The craft link between Kiri and Rupiah (stage training) is only stated from Kiri's side, but rupiah.as-he-was gives Rupiah the performer's training; related, and worth one cross-reference.

- **kiri** (kiri.the-card, kiri.recruits): Both also appear as plan beats (beat--kiris-card, Chapter 3; beat--kiris-show, Chapter 6) in nearly the same words: the plan holds the moment, the fact holds the world state.

- **kudo** (kudo.the-speech, shinobu.the-phone-in): The ink fact shinobu.the-phone-in (told in the canteen) already gives the table Kudo on the radio, trying hard not to shout, about a seventeen-year-old held without charge; the speech repeats those beats at full volume in public (seventeen, not charged, held). The ink father holds his voice down, where the pencil one is 'very loud about it'.

- **kudo** (kudo.the-lawyer, shinobu.released): shinobu.released says she was released under mounting public pressure from her father Kudo, who is very good at his job and very loud about it: the same sentence as kudo.the-lawyer, told from her side. Better home: kudo.the-lawyer for who he is, shinobu.released for the release.

- **kurose-police** (kurose-police.jurisdiction-quarrel, mayor.support-forces-cooperation): That the mayor forces the police to work beside the Directorate on raids is said in both; mayor.support-forces-cooperation (her words, D068) is the better home for the cause, and kurose-police.jurisdiction-quarrel (raids-only) adds that sharing a raid isn't sharing a file.

- **kurose-police** (kurose-police.the-file, envoy.police-kept-the-truth): The police file on the Envoy incident names the officer who fired and the Directorate has never seen it: the same fact in both, with the same flags (few, Chapter 11). kurose-police.the-file is the likelier home, since it is a road in questions/aois-name; envoy.police-kept-the-truth could point at it (the envoy proposal says the same).

- **kurose** (kurose.crime-trail, baldwin.manakos-boss, manako.underworld-dealings): kurose.crime-trail (her words) is a pointer: organised crime leads to Baldwin and to Manako's dealings. baldwin.manakos-boss (Baldwin is the crime lord Manako works for) and manako.underworld-dealings carry the facts, and threads/organised-crime carries the line; the ink kurose.organised-crime is the base it points from.

- **lens** (lens.treated-with-sap, sap.makes-it-visible): The same claim with the same metadata (known by few, told later): Sap is the chemical that makes the Watcher visible on a treated lens; sap.makes-it-visible is the better home, since it also covers Sap splashed on.

- **lens** (lens.what-it-shows, watcher.how-it-looks): Both give the sky of eyes on stalks and slow tentacles seen through a lens; lens.what-it-shows is the better home.

- **lens** (lens.never-without, watcher.children, sap.children-made-of-it): never-without's last two sentences (the children are built from Sap, solid, visible to anyone) are said again in watcher.children and sap.children-made-of-it; watcher.children is the better home.

- **lens** (lens.feeds-it, sap.grows-the-watcher): Every lens is a little Sap and feeds the Watcher is one case of every use of Sap makes the Watcher permanently bigger; sap.grows-the-watcher holds the general rule.

- **lens** (lens.three-ways, sap.inert-12): 'Find the shelved sample in the Directorate's own catalogue' is the Inert 12 sample; see the conflict filed under sap about whether any sample is left to find.

- **main-mech-hangar** (main-mech-hangar.nine-bays, frames.prototype-under-the-tarp, frames.legacy-hangar): Three hangars with unclear relations: this nine-bay bunker, the 'Hangar Four' where a Gen 2 prototype sits under a tarp, and the hangar of retired and never-assigned Frames. Whether Hangar Four is one of the nine bays, and whether the legacy hangar is a separate place, is not stated anywhere.

- **main-school-complex** (main-school-complex.what-it-is, directorate.school-name, directorate.two-ends): The Episode One scenes call it only 'the school block'; the open directorate.school-name says what the school is called is not decided, and her directorate.two-ends puts the school at the island's south-east end, away from the north-west military end. 'Main School Complex' is a name this pencil file has already settled on.

- **main-school-complex** (main-school-complex.the-real-archive, directorate-posts.school-registrar): Both say the registrar keeps the real archive (the underlying test data) on the second floor and nobody has been told why the Directorate wants it. main-school-complex.the-real-archive is the better home for the archive; directorate-posts.school-registrar for the registrar's role.

- **manako** (manako.inert-12, sap.catalogued-at-kortos, sap.inert-12): The Inert 12 sample handed to the junior chemist is also told in sap.md, which says 'about 45 years ago' (here 44) and puts the cataloguing at about 120 years ago while sap.inert-12 says the sample is about a century old. sap.catalogued-at-kortos is the better home for the sample's history; this file for what he did with it.

- **manako** (manako.the-spill, manako.saw-the-sky, manako.the-conference, watcher.sightings): Her fact watcher.sightings already lists the three sightings (lab spill, private proof, canister at the convention); it is the better home for the list, with the details here.

- **manako** (manako.the-spill, nobu.lab-assistant): Nobu was the assistant present at the spill, saw the tentacle and would not back him up; nobu.lab-assistant is the better home for his side.

- **manako** (manako.disgraced-after-the-spill, shucks.supervised-the-disgrace): Shucks supervising the disgrace 44 years ago is stated in both files; shucks.supervised-the-disgrace is the better home.

- **manako** (manako.list-worked-by-inner-circle, shucks.on-the-list, cicada.inner-circle): Shucks on the list and the inner circle carrying it out are also stated in the shucks and cicada files; cicada.inner-circle is the better home for the inner circle.

- **manako** (manako.directorate-may-know-him, shucks.on-the-list): shucks.on-the-list says Shucks, the person likeliest to suspect the tea-house owner is the man he once ruined, may feel his conscience salved; directorate-may-know-him (hers) says the Directorate may salve its conscience at seeing him about: the same idea from two angles, hers the better home.

- **manako** (manako.suspects-nobu, nobu.funds-the-column): Manako's suspicion is of what nobu.funds-the-column says is true; the two belong together, one as the truth and one as his belief.

- **manako** (manako.arms-length, manako.on-paper, manako.never-met-the-top, baldwin.manako-works-for-him): baldwin.manako-works-for-him says the crime lord is Baldwin's organisation and that the arms trade keeps a gifted chemist solving problems on paper without seeing a weapon; it is the better home for who the criminals are, this file for how it feels to him.

- **manako** (manako.the-conference, cicada.believe-me-now, rika.refused-the-explanation, the-sceptics.who): The conference, and the room's 'mass hallucination' verdict, are restated in the cicada, rika and the-sceptics files.
